# Project library

The guide to this repository's code and the record of the rebuild's decisions, for whoever works on it next, person or coding tool. It was `CLAUDE.md` until 2026-11-17. The rules every coding tool follows are in `AGENTS.md`, which Codex loads by itself and Claude Code loads through `CLAUDE.md`; where the work stands is in `HANDOFF.md`. This file is too long to load in every session: read the parts a task needs.

## Repository layout

- **`web/`** is the site: Next.js 16, TypeScript, PostgreSQL. All code lives there. Work happens on the `rebuild` branch and reaches `master` through pull requests.
- **The repository root** holds this file, `AGENTS.md` (the rules for coding tools), `CLAUDE.md` (which imports it), `HANDOFF.md` (the current state), `RUNBOOK.md` (what the owners do when the site misbehaves: keep it true when a page, a notice or a step it names changes), a short `README.md` and `.gitignore`.

The site replaced an earlier Flask prototype that lived at the repository root (`app/`, `run.py`, `config.py`, `seed.py`, `migrations/`). It was deleted on 2026-11-15 at the owner's request. Its last version is commit `f1420bc`: list its files with `git ls-tree -r --name-only f1420bc app` and read one with `git show f1420bc:app/templates/faq.html`. It is a reference for page design and copy only. It had unfixed defects: card payments could not succeed, the browser could skip payment, and double booking was possible.

The decisions behind the rebuild, and what is still open, are recorded under "Rebuild decisions" at the end of this file.

## The site (`web/`)

### Commands

Node.js is installed with nvm. A shell that did not load the login profile needs `. ~/.nvm/nvm.sh` first.

```bash
cd web
npm install
DATABASE_URL="" npm run dev   # http://localhost:3000 with an embedded database and a pretend card processor (web/.env.local's DATABASE_URL is staging's: see "Conventions and gotchas")
npm test             # every test (vitest)
npx vitest run src/server/bookings.test.ts            # one file
npx vitest run -t "refuses a second booking"          # tests whose name matches
npm run test:e2e     # browser tests (Playwright); starts its own server on port 3211
npx playwright test e2e/a11y.spec.ts                   # the accessibility scan (axe-core) alone
E2E_SHOTS=1 npm run test:e2e                          # also saves full-page pictures to test-results/shots
E2E_NAME=tester E2E_PORT=3213 npm run test:e2e        # a second run at the same time: its own port, database, build and results folders
E2E_BUILD=1 E2E_NAME=prod E2E_PORT=3215 npx playwright test --browser=webkit   # against a production build over HTTPS (see "Conventions and gotchas"); about 8 min
npx playwright test -g "staff see the day sheet"      # one browser test by name (they build on each other; see below)
npm run typecheck    # generates Next.js route types, then runs tsc
npm run lint
npm run knip         # files, exports and packages nothing uses (web/knip.jsonc says what counts); in CI's checks job since 2026-11-20
npm run build
npm run check:square # the site's own Square code against Square's sandbox (charges, refunds, a payment link); reads web/.env.square-sandbox; no money moves
npm run db:generate  # after editing src/db/schema.ts: writes a migration into drizzle/
DATABASE_URL=postgres://... npm run db:migrate        # real PostgreSQL only; also adds the website's own pages a database never had
DATABASE_URL=postgres://... npm run db:seed           # real PostgreSQL only
DATABASE_URL=postgres://... npm run db:clear-test-mode # lists test mode's bookings and what they made, before the live keys or when the site refuses them; -- --yes removes them, and nothing else
npx tsx scripts/import-acuity.ts <export.csv> --as <owner> [--money live|test] [--remind]   # a rehearsal of importing Acuity's appointments still to come; add --write to import
```

Local development needs no database install and no credentials. Without `DATABASE_URL` the app uses PGlite (PostgreSQL compiled to WebAssembly) stored in `web/.data`, migrates it on start and seeds the starting catalog. Delete `web/.data` to reset local data. `web/.env.example` lists the variables for production.

### Architecture

- **Business rules are pure functions in `src/lib/domain/`** (`time.ts`, `pricing.ts`, `availability.ts`). The browser uses them for instant previews and the server uses the same code as the only authority. A new rule about prices or free time belongs there with a test, never in a component or a route.
- **`src/server/`** holds everything that touches the database or the outside world:
  - `catalog.ts` loads studios, opening hours, price rules, add-ons and settings as one serializable `Catalog`.
  - `availability.ts` turns stored holds into per-studio taken time for one day and decides which dates are bookable.
  - `bookings.ts` is the booking service (`createBooking`, `getBookingByToken`).
  - `payments.ts` defines a `PaymentProvider` with a Square implementation (plain HTTPS calls, no SDK) and a mock. A request to Square times out after 10 seconds and is sent again, up to three times; the idempotency key makes a repeat safe. A refused card gets a sentence for the customer from `CARD_PROBLEMS`, and Square's own words go on the payment record. A charge that never gets an answer comes back as `unknown`: the booking is not made, and the customer is told nothing was taken (a booking's card is only reserved, see the booking order). A booking's charge passes `reserve`: Square holds the amount without taking it (`autocomplete: false`) and gives it back by itself after `RESERVE_HOURS` (24) unless `capture` takes it or `release` gives it back first; each of those counts one already done as done. The provider also makes, reads and withdraws a payment link (`createPayLink`, `payLinkState`, `cancelPayLink`: Square's Checkout API). The mock has no payment page of its own, so in test mode the client's page on this site offers a pretend payment.
  - `tz.ts` is the only place that converts between studio wall-clock time (America/New_York) and instants. The browser's one exception is today's date on the studio's clock (`studioToday` in `src/lib/domain/time.ts`, through `Intl`), so a device in another time zone is offered the studio's days. `clockChangeOn` gives the hour the clocks skip or show twice on a day: `loadDayAvailability` counts it as taken in every studio and for the photographer, and `createBooking` and `moveBooking` refuse a time whose real length is not its booked length.
  - `settings.ts` has the default business rules; rows in the `settings` table override them. It also keeps the getting-in text (`loadAccessInstructions`, `saveAccessInstructions`), which is deliberately not part of `Settings`: `Settings` goes to every browser inside the catalog, and that text can hold the code for the building door. The catalog carries only the fact that it is set (`accessInfoSent`).
  - `cached.ts` exposes `getCatalog()` with `'use cache'` and the tag `catalog` for prerendered pages.
- **`src/db/`**: `schema.ts` (Drizzle), `client.ts` (`getDb()` picks PostgreSQL or PGlite; `createMemoryDb()` for tests), `seed.ts` (starting catalog, taken from the studio's live price list).
- **Double booking is prevented by the database.** Every span of time a studio is taken is a row in `studio_holds`, written by bookings and by owner blocks. An exclusion constraint in `drizzle/0001_constraints.sql` rejects two active holds that overlap on one studio. A hold covers the booking plus the studio's clean-up buffer.
- **Booking order in `createBooking`**: validate and price on the server, hold the slot (status `pending_payment`), reserve the amount on the card with the booking id as idempotency key, confirm the booking with the payment recorded as `authorized`, then take the money (`takeReserved` in `src/server/reserved.ts`), which makes the payment `succeeded`. Only `succeeded` payments are money: receipts, refunds and the admin count nothing else. A card is therefore never charged for a booking that was not made: whatever stops before the confirmation, Square gives the reserved amount back. When taking the money does not go through, the booking stands, the payment stays `authorized`, the owner is emailed once, and the scheduled task tries again (`settleReservedPayments`), or gives the amount back if the booking was cancelled first. Once the reservation has lapsed (`RESERVE_HOURS`), the card holds nothing: the task no longer tries to take it, marks it failed with a line in the history, and the booking owes its price, which the studio collects and records ("To pay" on its summary). The owner and the client are emailed only while the booking still owes something: from the moment the reservation lapsed, the appointment's page showed it owing, and the studio may have collected it before the task came by, or the booking been cancelled: it is read again under its lock (`lapseReserved`). While the card holds the amount, the summaries say "Held on card", the appointment's page says when it lapses (`heldCentsByBooking` in money.ts), and the client's cancel and move say what becomes of it. What a booking owes is one rule (`owedCents` in money.ts): for one that stands, its price less what it received and what a card holds reserved for it; nothing for one that ended. Taking it locks the booking (`recordTaken`): money taken for a booking that was cancelled while the processor was taking it is given back in full, as a refund that the same run of the task sends. A failed charge releases the slot and gives back any code that was spent. Unpaid holds expire after `holdMinutes`; `releaseExpiredHolds` runs before every availability read and booking attempt, so freeing slots does not depend on the scheduler, and gives back the code the booking spent (`giveBackUnmade`), as a refused card does. Both outcomes of the charge act only on a booking still waiting for its payment: a refused card gives the code back only when it is the one that ends the hold, and a card that answers after the hold ran out does not confirm the booking: what it reserved is given back (`giveBackReserved` in reserved.ts, or by the scheduled task). After a successful booking the browser loads the booking page as a new document (`navigate` in `BookingFlow`), not through the router, so the finished selection is not kept alive behind it. Each attempt carries a random id (`requestId`) that the screen sends again only when it got no answer at all; the database keeps its SHA-256 (`bookings.request_key`, unique), never the id. A repeat is answered by `answerAgain` before anything is checked again: a booking that was made is answered as the first time, with a second link of its own (`retry_token_hash`; `tokenMatches` takes either), and nothing is booked or charged again; an attempt still on its way gets `in_progress`; one that failed, or that asks for something else, gets `attempt_over`, and the screen starts a new one (one that made no booking is told, as the first time, that its card was not charged).
- **Prices** are a table of studio × day type (weekday or weekend) × duration in `price_rules`. Only durations with a price can be booked. `special_dates` reprices a date as the other day type. Booking several studios applies the `multiStudioPercent` setting. The sentence that advertises that discount (`multiStudioOffer` in `src/lib/catalog-view.ts`) asks the pricing function what each number of studios gets, so it cannot promise more than is charged.
- **Add-ons** are taken once per booking (a tick box), except one whose most (`max_quantity`) is above 1, which is taken in a quantity (a number); today that is the extra guest. The guests a booking can take count every add-on of kind `people` (`extraGuestsAllowed` in pricing.ts, with each studio's `extra_guests` and the settings `twoStudioGuestLimit` and `threeStudioGuestLimit`; `extraGuestsFor` in catalog-view.ts). Each has a kind (`people`, `pet` or `other`; `src/lib/addon-kinds.ts`) that the owner picks. The guest information and policies pages read the kind, never the name, to say whether larger groups and pets can be booked: through the conditions `pets`, `pet free studios` and `extra people` and the live values `{pet studios}` and `{pet fee}`, which use `petPolicy` (`src/lib/catalog-view.ts`). A booked add-on keeps its kind with the booking (`booking_addons.kind`), like its name and price, because the pet fee has a refund rule of its own.
- **The two booking flows** (studio first, time first) are the same steps in a different order in `src/components/BookingFlow.tsx`. Both read one endpoint, `GET /api/availability?date=`, and compute slots in the browser with the domain functions. From the keyboard (since 2026-11-21): a studio's checkbox is not drawn, so its card takes an outline when the keyboard is on it (`:focus-visible` in `globals.css`); a new step takes the focus to its heading (an effect on the step's number, each heading `tabIndex={-1}`), because the button pressed goes away with its step and the focus would fall back to the page's body; Enter in the code field does what "Apply" does. Each time is a button and a Tab stop of its own, which is slow from the keyboard (Known gaps).
- **Routes**: pages `/` (the home page, a page of the admin drawn by `src/app/page.tsx`), `/book`, `/booking/[ref]?t=<secret>`, `/pay/[id]` (where a payment link leads), `/account` (the client portal: overview, `/account/appointments`, `/account/rewards`, `/account/receipts` and `/account/receipts/[ref]`), `/account/sign-in`, `/staff`, `/admin` (with sub-pages for the calendar, one appointment, bookings, customers and one customer, prices, studios and add-ons, hours, staff, codes, settings, and the website editor: `/admin/website`, `/admin/website/design`, `/admin/website/navigation`, `/admin/website/media`, `/admin/website/media/collections` and `/admin/website/media/collections/[id]`, `/admin/website/fonts`, `/admin/website/pages` and `/admin/website/pages/[id]`), `/dev/outbox`, and any other address, which `src/app/[...slug]` answers with a page of the admin: among them the ones the website was built with, `/studios`, `/rates`, `/policies`, `/faq` (guest information), `/services` (photography sessions) and `/contact`; API `GET /api/availability`, `GET /api/booking-config`, `POST /api/quote`, `POST /api/bookings`, `GET /api/cron/tick`, `GET /account/receipts/[ref]/pdf`, `GET /account/receipts/year/[year]/pdf`, `POST /account/sign-out`, `GET /admin/website/preview`, `POST /admin/website/preview/exit`, `GET /media/[id]/[version]/[name]` (a picture of the media library), `GET /font-files/[id]/[name]` (a file of an uploaded font), `POST /admin/website/media/upload` `POST /admin/website/fonts/upload`, `GET /sitemap.xml` and `GET /robots.txt`. Everything else a signed-in person changes goes through server actions (`actions.ts` next to the pages).
- **Payment mode** is chosen in `resolvePayments`: Square when its three credentials are set; the mock when `PAYMENTS_MODE=mock` or outside production; otherwise bookings that cost money are refused. A booking is never confirmed free because payment is unconfigured. Square's sandbox and its live account are two processors (`square-sandbox` and `square`, the provider's `name`); which one the credentials are for is read from the application id (a sandbox one begins with `sandbox-`: `squareEnvironment`), and `SQUARE_ENVIRONMENT`, when set, must agree, or card payments are off with a word in the log. Every payment and link keeps the processor that made it (`provider`), and the site never asks one about what another made, nor marks it: a refund, a reserved amount or a link of another processor waits, untouched, for that processor (a refund says why, where the owner sees refunds that have not gone through, and the owner is emailed once: `waitForProcessor`; such a refund is `waiting`, not one that is tried again, `RefundsState`, and the words for the owner and the client say so; a link can be closed by hand), so that a setting wrong for a while changes nothing for good. Each booking says which kind of money it is (`bookings.money_kind`, migration 0028): `test` when it was made in test mode (the pretend processor or Square's sandbox), `live` when it was made with the live account. With no processor set up, a booking is what the database holds already, so that a setting wrong for a while changes nothing for good: real where real bookings are, test mode's where only test mode's are, and in a database with no booking yet, real on the live site and test mode's anywhere else (`moneyKindNow` in src/server/test-mode.ts). Everything a booking makes is that kind of money too, its payments recorded by hand included; a booking made in the admin takes the kind of the processor set up then (`processor` in `BookingDeps`). The two never pay for each other: a code a booking of one kind issued is refused for a booking of the other, when the price is asked and when the booking is made (`moneyKindOfCode`, `OTHER_MONEY_CODE`; a code no booking issued, the owners' own, is used anywhere), and no payment link is made by a card processor of the other kind than the booking's (`OTHER_MONEY_LINK`, in `sendPayLink`): real money is not charged for a pretend booking, and a pretend payment does not mark a real booking paid. An appointment's page says when its booking is of the other kind than the site's. When a database is opened (`getDb`), the site refuses to run the live account on one that holds test mode's bookings, or any payment or link of test mode, which would count as money there (credit from a reschedule code, a pet fee's credit, a free hour, a refund, a receipt, cash recorded for a pretend booking: all made from nothing), and test mode on one that holds real bookings, or payments of the live account; the refusal says what to do (`assertOneKindOfMoney`). The build (`next build` with `DATABASE_URL`) opens the database too, and is refused the same way: test mode is cleared before the first build with the live keys, not only before the first start. The scripts (`db:migrate`, `db:seed`, `db:clear-test-mode`) open the database themselves (`openPostgres`), without that check: their shell has no card processor set up, and would be taken for test mode. A database refused, or that could not be opened, is closed again and not tried again for 10 seconds (`readyOrClosed` and `openOnce` in client.ts), so that every request does not open it anew. `npm run db:clear-test-mode` lists what it would remove and keep, and with `-- --yes` removes what test mode made, and nothing else (`testModeContents`, `describeTestMode`, `clearTestMode`): test mode's bookings with their payments, refunds, links, history, holds, the codes they issued and the entries about those, and mail about them that has not gone out (a message that names a booking that stays is about it, and stays). Memberships are money too (since 2026-11-18): a membership of a test processor counts as test mode's in `assertOneKindOfMoney` and `otherMoneyNotice`, one of the live account as real, and the clear removes those of test mode with their months (`membership_payments`), their hours (`membership_minutes`, whose lines also keep their bookings: the hours spent on the bookings that go are removed first), the plans' prices at test mode's processors, and mail about them (a member's own email is told by its code, or by the member's address when they keep no membership, as the owners' wording may leave the code out); the plans stay. Every membership of test mode goes, also one that paid part of a booking that stays (`madeReal`): that booking lets go of it, keeps in its price what the hours paid, is named in the listing (`detached`) and gets a line in its history. Kept, such a membership would leave test money the live account refuses the database for, for good (the tester's finding of 2026-11-18). A real booking for which test mode's processor recorded a pretend payment or link stays, without them, and its history says so. A booking made in test mode for which the live account took money, or made a link, stays too, and counts as real from then on (`madeReal`): no record of real money is lost. Codes that stay: the owners' own, and free hours that stamps added by hand completed (one only test bookings used gets its uses back; any other gets back what each test booking still held of it, `gaveCodeBack`), and a code a test booking issued that a real booking used; a client's free hours that stay are numbered anew (`renumberFreeHours`), so that the next one earned is still sent. The listing names what is worth a look, each code with the value it will have: on a database of test mode every such code and change of stamps, on one with real bookings only the codes test mode touched, a free hour of a client whose test visits go among them. The clear is the way out wherever test money is where it must not be, also when a server left running in test mode (a demo, staging, an old deployment) wrote it beside the live account: the live site's overview says what that server left while it runs (`otherMoneyNotice`), and its next start refuses the database until the clear. Payments and links the sandbox recorded as `square`, before it had a name of its own (2026-11-17 09:27, New York), are relabelled `square-sandbox` by migration 0028: the live account had never been used. The overview says when payments go to Square's sandbox, and why they are off when the settings disagree; a browser is told only that they are off (`/api/booking-config`).
- **The bank's check of the client (3-D Secure, 2026-11-20).** Square's card form asks the card's bank while it makes the token: `tokenize(buyerVerification(...))` in `src/components/square-card.ts`, used by the booking screen and the membership's checkout.
  - *What the bank is told:* the amount the server charges, in dollars with two decimals (the server refuses a total that changed from the one on screen); the client's name, split as Square's customer is (`splitName` in `src/lib/names.ts`), and email; and the intent: `CHARGE` for a booking, `CHARGE_AND_STORE` for a membership's first month, whose card is kept for the monthly billing. No phone and no address: the site keeps no billing address, and Square refused a whole customer once over a phone number. The membership's checkout refuses a name or phone of spaces alone before the bank is asked, as the server would.
  - *The check:* when the bank wants one (a code sent to the holder's phone), Square shows it over the page. The token is then all the payment needs: Square deprecated `verifyBuyer` and its separate verification token. The server tells Square who pays (`customer_details`, from `ChargeRequest.customerInitiated`): the client, for every charge today, as each comes from their own card form.
  - *One attempt, one token:* `useCardForm` gives the same token to a press that follows no answer, until `newAttempt()`, which the screens call on any other answer and on a step back. Square answers a request sent again under its idempotency key as before only when it is the same request: with a new token it answers `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED`, which `charge` reads as no answer, never as a refused card, as the first request may have taken the money. Before 2026-11-20 a membership retried after a lost answer was marked failed that way, its first month possibly taken, with no word to the studio. The booking screen and the membership's checkout both use the hook since then; the booking screen had its own copy of the card form's code.
  - *When it fails:* a wrong code still gives a token, which Square declines with `CARD_DECLINED_VERIFICATION_REQUIRED`: "The card's bank did not confirm this payment." A check the client closes makes Square's form throw instead of answering, as does a check that cannot be shown. `tokenizeCard` turns that into `NOT_CONFIRMED` ("The card was not confirmed, so nothing was charged"), where the pages had said the site was out of reach, and logs what Square's form threw in the browser's console.
  - *Not the site's:* Square's risk rules, which the owners set in Square's dashboard.
- **Access control lives in each page and each action**, never in a layout: `requireUser`, `requireStaff` (an attendant, a manager or an owner), `requireManager` (a manager or an owner) and `requireOwner` in `src/server/session.ts`. Someone without the role gets the ordinary not-found page. The admin sidebar (`src/app/admin/frame.tsx`) is rendered by each admin page after its check for the same reason. A role only opens the door: what this person may see and do is asked with `can(viewer, ability, settings)`. The services in `staff.ts`, `manage.ts`, `appointments.ts`, `customers.ts`, `rewards.ts`, `studio-payments.ts` and `pay-links.ts` check the viewer or actor again themselves; the functions in `admin.ts` do not, so every caller must check first (`requireOwner`, or `requireAbility` in `src/app/admin/actions.ts` for the few a manager can be given).
- **`src/server/runtime.ts`** is the glue for requests: site address, which processor and mail service to use, who gets refund notices, and `flushEmailsSoon()` (delivers queued mail after the response).
- **`src/server/auth.ts`** implements passwordless sign-in: emailed six-digit codes, rate-limited to 3 per 15 minutes, hashed in the database. Sessions are 30-day tokens (SHA-256 hashed). `OWNER_EMAILS` controls the owner role; adding or removing an address takes effect at the next request.
- **`src/server/manage.ts`** handles post-booking changes: customer cancellation (with automatic refund by policy), reschedule codes (release the booking and issue a credit), and owner cancel/refund. Refunds are recorded, then sent to the processor with the refund id as idempotency key; failures are retried by the scheduled task. A refund is dated when the processor takes it (`createdAt` is set then): one that goes through when it is tried again belongs to that day, and the booking's history says that it went through, as it says that it failed, or that Square rejected it after accepting it. Only the attempt that turns a refund from waiting to failed writes that line and tells the owner, so two attempts at once (the scheduled task beside a cancellation) tell once; and whether a refund that went through had failed is read as it is marked made, so its history ends with what happened, written after the line that said it failed whatever clock each attempt had; marking a refund failed, its line and the owner's alert are one transaction, so an attempt that goes through meanwhile waits for them. The studio's cancel in the bookings list sends what the card held as the page showed it (`seenCardCents`): money received since (a payment link paid meanwhile) refuses the cancel, for the owner to look again. When the studio cancels, money paid at the studio is named to the owner, in the history and in the client's email, for the studio to give back by hand (`atStudioCents`); the bookings list shows it beside the cancel form (`studioHeldCents`). What can be given back by hand leaves out a pet fee already returned as studio credit that the card did not hold (`studioRefundableCents` in pet-fee.ts), and `recordHandRefund` keeps to it: the pet fee comes back once. A refusal names the part of the pet fee each side held (the credit may have been only part of the fee). A payment record behind a code of studio credit, a pet fee's or a reschedule code's, is not taken back while the code can be spent, nor once any of it was used, whatever became of the booking it paid for (`voidStudioPayment`, `codeStanding` in promos.ts): an owner cancels the code first. What a code was used for is what it was issued for (`promo_codes.issued_cents`; migrations 0024 and 0025 work it out for the codes a database had, from what became of the bookings that used them, and for a reschedule code from its booking's history, the line "Released for a reschedule code worth $126"; `code-issued.test.ts` replays them on codes in every state) less what is left on it; a pet fee's credit is what its code was issued for (`petFeeReturnedCents`), which is part of the fee when only part was paid, and the appointment page and the bookings list count that part. A booking released for a reschedule code keeps its money on the code: the owner refunds it, to the card or by hand, only up to what is left on the code, whether an owner cancelled the code or not (what was spent of it stays spent), and the refund comes off the code (`rescheduleCodeOf`, `takeOffRescheduleCode` in promos.ts); the bookings list says so beside its refund form, and the client is emailed what is left on the code (`codeRefundEmail`). A processing fee of 100% keeps everything, and the words say so: nothing is asked of the owner. Square accepts most refunds as pending (seen in its sandbox): a refund it has not completed has no `settled_at`, and the scheduled task asks about it again (`checkUnsettledRefunds`, through `refundState`). Completed, it is marked so; rejected after all, it becomes a failed refund: the payment's refunded amount is taken down again, the owner is emailed, and it is retried with the others. A reschedule credit is worth what was paid plus what a percent code took off (`rescheduleOutcome` in `src/lib/domain/policy.ts`). A cancellation gives the booking's code back (`giveCodeBack`): a percent code gets its use back and an amount code from the studio (given by hand, or a pet fee returned) its amount, whoever cancels; a reschedule credit only when the studio cancels before the visit starts. The refund rule (`cancellationOutcome`) keeps two things off the card: the pet fee, which comes back as a code for studio credit, and money taken at the studio, which the owner is emailed to refund by hand.
- **Where a booking's money is** (`src/server/money.ts`): a card charged through the site (`payments.provider` is `square`, `square-sandbox` or `mock`) can be sent back by the site; money taken at the studio and recorded by hand (`provider` `manual`, with a `method` and `recorded_by`) cannot. `netCardPaidCents` counts only the first kind, so every refund the site sends is card money. Every refund recorded counts against what can still be refunded, whatever its state, made, on its way or owed (one that failed is tried again until it is made, or recorded as made in Square): so `netCardPaidCents`, `recordRefunds` and the bookings list, and refunds can never add up to more than was paid. The pet fee stays out of every refund, of card money and of money paid at the studio alike, and comes back as credit (`cancellationOutcome`). `src/server/studio-payments.ts` is "mark as paid": `recordStudioPayment` (all or part of what a booking made in the admin still owes, which is nothing once the booking has ended: `STANDS` in money.ts), `voidStudioPayment` (a record made by mistake; it stays, struck out) and `recordHandRefund` (a refund the studio gave by hand). `src/server/pet-fee.ts` is the pet fee: `petFeeCentsOf`, `issuePetCredit` (used by a cancellation in time) and `givePetFeeCredit` (the owner's button when the pet did not come). A fee once returned as credit (`petFeeReturnedCents`) is neither refunded to the card by the owner (`ownerCancelBooking`, `ownerRefund`, and the bookings list's `refundableCents`) nor carried by a reschedule code (`rescheduleOutcome`). Each of them writes a line into the booking's history with the same label on both sides and a note that says what happened. What was paid and kept is `netPaidCentsByBooking` (every payment that succeeded, less every refund of it, made, on its way or owed; a refund by hand is a `refunds` row too), and what a booking that stands was paid beyond what it costs now, its price and its extra charges, is `creditLeftCentsByBooking` ("Remaining credit", follow-up 5): after a move to a day that costs less, or a charge taken back once paid. Nothing gives it back by itself: the studio refunds it from the appointment's page, to the card (`ownerRefund`) or by hand (`recordHandRefund`). What a booking owes (`owedCentsByBooking`) and its remaining credit count what it was paid toward its price (`paidTowardPriceCentsByBooking`): every payment received, less only the part of a refund that gave back remaining credit (`refunds.credit_cents`, set by `ownerRefund` and `recordHandRefund` from the credit left at that moment). So credit refunded is owed again if the price goes up again, and a goodwill refund never makes a booking owe (the tester's finding of 2026-11-18). The summaries' "Paid so far" is that amount too (`creditReturnedCents` beside `receivedCents`). Once anything was given back, the booking's summary (`BookingSummary`: the booking's page, the client's account, the appointment's page) says instead what was paid and how, what was refunded ("Refunded", "Refund on its way" or "Refunded (part on its way)": `refundedCents` and `refundsWaitingCents`, from `refundedCentsByBooking`, which counts every refund made, on its way or owed), and in its last row what that leaves ("Paid, less refunds"), or what is still to pay, or the credit still to be refunded, which stays apart from what was refunded: the owners' payment summary of 2026-11-20, built 2026-11-21. A cancelled booking's page says so too, where it showed only its total before. Where money recorded by hand was paid, at the studio or through Acuity before this website (`ACUITY_METHOD`), is `handPaidByBooking`, in words by `handPaidPlace`: a cancellation, its emails, the owner's notice, the appointment's page and the admin's list of bookings name it so.
- **Extra charges** (`src/server/charges.ts`, table `booking_charges`): overtime, cleaning and damage added to an appointment once it has started (`addCharge`), taken back by an owner while unpaid (`voidCharge`, which keeps the row with `voided_at`). Every charge not taken back adds to what the booking owes in `owedCentsByBooking` (`chargesCentsByBooking` in money.ts), so recording a payment, a payment link, the bookings list and the receipts all count it without code of their own. `BookingView.charges` and `chargesCents` carry them to the price summary and the receipt; `BookingView.totalCents` stays the booking's own price, which the refund and reschedule rules use. `moneyOf` reports `totalCents` with the charges and `chargesCents` apart. Overtime stores the minutes and the hourly rate it was worked out from (`hourlyRateOf`), so a later price change does not alter it. Which money paid which charge is `chargesPaidOf` in money.ts, read as `owedCentsByBooking` reads it: what came in pays the price first, then each charge in the order it was added (`paidCents`, gross of refunds, as what is owed counts it; `keptCents`, net of them). `voidCharge` takes back only a charge with nothing paid of it, and tells apart one still paid from one whose money was given back; the payment link's email lists what is unpaid of each. A booking with a charge is not moved (`CHARGES_BLOCK_MOVE` in appointments.ts, checked by `moveOptions`, `moveBooking` before and under the booking's lock, and `getAppointment` as `moveBlocked`), so a refund or a reschedule code never meets a charge: `factsFor` in manage.ts reads the card's and the studio's money as they are.
- **`src/server/pay-links.ts`** is the payment link for what a booking made in the admin still owes. `sendPayLink` has the processor make a link for exactly that amount and emails the client the address of a page on this site, `/pay/<id>`, which leads to the processor's page. Whether a link was paid is asked of the processor (`checkPayLink`): when the client is sent back to that page, when an administrator presses "Check", and from the scheduled task. A paid link becomes an ordinary card payment on the booking (`settle`), so it is refunded like any other. Before a booking is cancelled, by either side, or released for a reschedule code, the processor is asked whether its open link was paid (`noticePaidLink`), so that a payment made just before counts like any other: under the cancellation's terms, or in the code. So are the client's booking page and their account, before they say what cancelling or moving would do (`noticeBookingPayments` in manage.ts, `noticePaidLinksOf`); and the client's cancel and move send the words they were shown (`seen`): when what would be done differs now (a payment arrived, a deadline passed), nothing is done and they are shown it as it is (`LOOK_AGAIN`). A press on a booking that stopped being confirmed meanwhile is told what became of it (cancelled, released, marked missed, over: `endedText`), and leaves the page as it was, so that the answer is read. When the processor cannot say whether the link was paid (or the link is another processor's, which the site can never ask), nothing is ended: the owners' answer of 2026-11-20 to question 23, built 2026-11-21. The client's cancellation, or release for a reschedule code, waits, and the booking keeps its time (`bookings.endRequest` and `endRequestedAt`, migration 0051; `waitToEnd` in manage.ts). The history says so, and the owners are emailed to look the link up (`ownerUnseenLinkNotice`). The studio's own cancel is refused, with what to do (`unconfirmedLinkText`). Once the site knows, what waited is done under the booking's terms as they stood when the client asked, the link's payment counted if there was one (`finishWaitingEnd`): by the scheduled task (`finishWaitingEnds`), by an owner's "Check whether it was paid" or "Close it by hand" (`finishWaitingOf` in pay-links.ts), or by the client's "Try again now". Two at once refund once: the booking's row is locked, and the second finds it ended. While it waits, the scheduled task sends no reminder, completes no visit and takes no amount reserved on a card; the studio cannot move the booking; the link's page offers nothing to pay (`closed.waiting`); and the booking's page and the client's account say so in place of the choices (`endRequestText`). Question 54 under "Rules built on the builder's reading" has what the builder decided in it. An owner closes a link the processor cannot say anything about with "Close it by hand" on the appointment's page, once they have looked it up in Square (`closePayLinkByHand`); the site then stops asking about it, and a payment can be recorded by hand. The appointment's page shows an open link whenever there is one, for a booking that ended with nothing paid too, and says why such a link is still open. A link made by another processor than the one set up now (one of test mode left after the real keys went in), or with none set up, is one nobody can say anything about: it is closed by hand, its page offers nothing to pay, it is not emailed again (`sendPayLink`), and a pretend payment needs the site in test mode (`pretendToPay`), so that a link of test mode never records money that did not move. The scheduled task asks about the links asked about longest ago first (`payment_links.checked_at`, migration 0026), so links it cannot read never keep the others waiting. A page waits at most `PAGE_WAIT_SECONDS` for the processor (`withinSeconds`), and is drawn without its answer after that, saying that it could not check the link; what the client presses waits for it. What a page leaves running is finished after the response, with the mail it queues (`finishAfterResponse` in runtime.ts, through `after()`, which a serverless host needs). The open link of a booking that is no longer active is withdrawn when the booking's page, the client's account or the page the link leads to is opened (`lookAtPayLink`), and that page offers nothing to pay (`closed`), whether or not the link could be withdrawn yet. One that still arrives after the booking ended (the link could not be withdrawn, and was paid later) is recorded and refunded to the card in full at once; the client is emailed (`latePaymentEmail`, put right when that refund does not go through, as a cancellation's email is), and the page the link leads to says it was refunded, or that the refund has not reached the card yet (`lateRefundOf`, which tells that refund from any other by its reason). A payment that counted while its booking stood stays "paid" on that page, whatever a cancellation refunded of it later. A booking has at most one open link (a unique index), no payment can be recorded by hand while one is open, and a booking that stops being active withdraws its link (`closePayLinkOf`). A link is never marked cancelled unless the processor confirmed it can no longer be paid, nor withdrawn while the processor cannot say whether it was paid (`checkPayLink` answers `unanswered`): the scheduled task asks again. Only an owner's "Close it by hand" closes a link the processor cannot say anything about.
- **`src/server/staff.ts`** serves the day sheet: bookings on the days a member of staff may see (their assigned days, or every day with `staffSeesAllDays`), with contact details controlled by `staffSeesContact`.
- **`src/server/appointments.ts`** is one appointment as the admin sees it, for both levels: `getAppointment` (what this viewer may see and do, with the booking's payments and its pet fee), `setLabel`, `moveBooking` (same studios and length, new time; the holds are updated in place, so the database still refuses an overlap) and `createAdminBooking`. A move to a day of the other kind reprices the booking in the same transaction (`priceOnNewDay` in `src/server/reprice.ts`, follow-up 5): the booking's amounts and each hold's `price_cents` take the new day's price, credit the new price no longer needs goes back on its code (`addBackToCode`), the history note and the client's email (part `price_note` of "Booking moved by the studio") say the new total and what follows. `moveOptions` gives the move form the same price in advance (`MovePricePreview`), or refuses a day with no price for the booking's length. A booking keeps the kind of day it is priced as (`bookings.priced_as`: set when it is made and at each move, filled in by migration 0046 for older ones), which a move compares the new day with, so that a special date added after it was made changes nothing for it. A new price that changes nothing the client reads (a free hour that still pays all of it) is stored without a word to the client (`announced`). `cardReceiptOf` serves `GET /admin/appointments/<ref>/receipt/<payment id>` (a route handler), which asks the card processor for the payment's receipt (`PaymentProvider.receiptOf`: Square's `receipt_url`, which exists once a payment is completed) and redirects to it, or back to the page with why not. `src/server/calendar.ts` loads a span of days for the calendar, and the date maths of its three views is in `src/lib/domain/calendar.ts`. The rules for labels are pure functions in `src/lib/domain/labels.ts`. `src/server/history.ts` writes and reads `booking_events`: call `recordEvent` in the same transaction as any change of a booking's label or status.
- **The admin has three levels** (`src/lib/abilities.ts`). Owners are the addresses in `OWNER_EMAILS` and can do everything. A Studio Manager is the role `manager`, a Studio Attendant the role `staff`. `can(viewer, ability, settings)` answers for all three: an attendant may do what the owners have switched on for attendants (eight switches, the `staff...` settings); a manager can always do what running the calendar needs, and has nine switches of their own (the `manager...` settings: prices, taking payments, refunds, customers, loyalty corrections, blocking time, changing the website's design as a draft, editing its pages as a draft, publishing the website). The owners set both on the Staff page; a change applies at the next request. Prices, studios and add-ons, opening hours, codes, staff and settings have no switch: they stay with the owners. A shared page uses `requireStaff` or `requireManager`, asks the service what the viewer may see, and passes `viewer` and the settings to `AdminFrame`, which builds that person's menu. Someone who may open an appointment and may not see prices gets its history with the amounts taken out (`withoutAmounts`).
- **`src/server/promos.ts`** manages the codes entered at checkout. Three kinds: `percent` (off the studio rental), `amount` (money, decremented as spent: reschedule credits) and `free_hour` (the price of one hour of one studio: the loyalty reward). A code comes from loyalty, from a moved booking, or from the owner, who can make any of the three. Redemption is atomic with the booking. A booking has at most one code.
  - **Its text.** The site makes up its own codes (`THANKS-`, `MOVE-`, `VS-` or `PET-` and two groups of four). Since 2026-11-20 an owner may name a code in Admin → Codes ("Code (optional)"). The name is kept in capitals with no spaces, as checkout reads what a client types (`normalizeCode`). It must be 3 to 30 letters, digits and hyphens, starting and ending with a letter or a digit (`PROMO_CODE_RULE` in `src/lib/parse.ts`, and the field's pattern), and must not be shaped like the site's own codes (`looksSiteMade`), so that staff can still recognise those.
  - **A taken name is refused.** A cancelled or expired code keeps its name, so a name used once stays taken.
- **`src/server/tasks.ts`** runs the recurring work: release expired holds, close payments whose result was never recorded (nothing was taken, so nobody is emailed), take the money reserved for bookings that stand and give back what was reserved for ones cancelled first, mark visits complete, send the free hours those visits have earned, queue the review request a day after a visit (`queueReviewRequests`) and the reminder before one (`queueReminders`: `reminderHours` before the start, once, `bookings.reminder_sent_at`), retry open refunds, ask the processor about refunds it accepted without completing, ask it about every open payment link (recording the ones that were paid, withdrawing the ones whose booking is no longer active), deliver queued email. Called by `GET /api/cron/tick`.
- **Photography**: a session is an ordinary booking with `photographer_id` set. `createBooking` prices it by its package (`photoPackage` in `quote`), asks `loadDayAvailability` for the photographer's day (`withPhotographer`) and holds the photographer beside the studio. A session needs more notice than a rental: `noticeHours` in `src/lib/domain/availability.ts` (the setting `photoMinLeadHours`, two weeks), used by the server and by the booking screen. `src/server/photography.ts` is the admin side: working hours, blocked time, packages. Wherever a booking stops being active, call `releaseHolds` (`src/server/holds.ts`), which frees its studios and its photographer; do not set `studio_holds.active` by hand.
- **`src/server/loyalty.ts`** counts a customer's stamps and sends the free hours they have earned (`sendEarnedFreeHours`). The rule is a pure function, `loyaltyStanding` in `src/lib/domain/loyalty.ts`: one stamp per day visited, and neither a visit made with a free hour nor a photography session gives one. Visit stamps are never stored: they are counted from the completed bookings, so one visit cannot give two. What an owner adds or takes away by hand is a row of `loyalty_ledger` (who, when, why), counted on top (`stampAdjustmentOf`). What is owed is the number earned less the number ever sent, and each free hour is numbered per customer (`promo_codes.loyalty_seq`, unique with the email), so repeating or racing the task, or correcting stamps, cannot send one twice.
- **The client portal** is `/account` and its three sub-pages, each rendered through `AccountFrame` after its own `requireUser`. `src/server/account.ts` splits a customer's bookings into upcoming and past, filters the past by date, studio and status, and has the words a customer reads for a status (`customerStatus`). `src/server/rewards.ts` lists rewards with a status that is worked out, never stored (Available, Redeemed, Expired, Canceled), and holds the owners' corrections (`adjustStamps`, `cancelReward`, `restoreReward`), each of which needs a reason and writes the ledger. `src/server/receipts.ts` makes receipts from payment records and from nothing else (`loadReceipts`, `yearStatement`); a refund counts on them once it is made, or accepted by the processor ("on its way"), and one owed (failed, or not sent yet) is listed as not made yet and not counted (`ReceiptRefund.state`); a refund is dated when it was sent, and one made by hand in Square when that was recorded: the lines are the prices kept with the booking, never today's price list, and a booking with no payment has no receipt. `src/server/receipt-pdf.ts` draws the same `Receipt` as a PDF file with `@cantoo/pdf-lib`, which is pure JavaScript and needs no file on the server. Everything is found by the signed-in email address, which the sign-in code proved.
- **`src/server/customers.ts`** is the owners' view of customers: `listCustomers` (everyone who booked, searched by name or email) and `getCustomer` (one customer's appointments, receipts, stamps, rewards and codes). A customer is an email address. Both answer null to anyone who is not an owner, staff included, whatever switches are on: a customer's payments are not for staff. The pages are `/admin/customers` and `/admin/customers/[email]`, with the owners' own PDF downloads beside them.
- **The website's content** is what the owners edit under Admin, Website, without a deploy. `src/server/website.ts` is the store: a document (`site_documents`) has a published copy, which the public sees, and a draft, which only the admin sees; `saveDraft`, `publishDraft`, `discardDraft`, `listVersions` and `restoreVersion` are the whole life of one, and every publish keeps a full copy in `site_versions`. Two documents exist so far, each described by a `DocumentSpec` (how to read it, the site as it was built, who may change it): the **design** (`src/lib/site/design.ts`: colours, fonts, text styles, buttons, spacing) and the **frame** around every page (`src/lib/site/frame.ts`: the studio's name and contact details, logo, menu, booking button, footer, social links, announcement bar, the site's title and description). What is stored is never trusted: every read goes through `parseDesign` or `parseFrame`, which give a complete, valid value whatever is in the table, so nothing but a design can reach the stylesheet and nothing but a safe link can become a link. Prices, hours, booking rules and money terms are not documents: they take effect at once, as before.
- **How a page gets that content**: the root layout and the pages call `siteDesign()` and `siteFrame()` (`src/server/site.ts`), never the cached functions directly. They give what is published, from the cache (tag `website`, refreshed by `updateTag(WEBSITE_TAG)` on publish), or the drafts when the request is a preview. A preview is Next.js Draft Mode: `GET /admin/website/preview?to=/path` switches it on for someone who may edit the website, the root layout shows a bar that says so, and `POST /admin/website/preview/exit` or signing out switches it off. The cookie alone is not enough: `isPreview()` also checks that the person is still signed in and still allowed. The editor's phone check narrows a preview to one document with `?website-check=`: a layout cannot read a query, so the proxy (`src/proxy.ts`, which runs for that query only, and whose `config` must hold its name as a literal) hands it on as a header, which `site.ts` reads only in a preview, so that every other page stays as static as it was. The design reaches the browser as CSS variables in a `<style>` element of the root layout (`designCss`); `globals.css` holds the same values as its defaults.
- **The studio's name, address and phone** live in the frame document, and nowhere in the code. Pages read them from `siteFrame()`; what is not a page (an email, a PDF receipt, a form's error message) reads the published ones with `loadBusiness(db)`. Email texts carry the marks `{{studio}}` and `{{address}}`, which `queueEmail` fills in.
- **Fonts**: the library the owners choose from is `FONT_LIBRARY` in `src/lib/site/fonts.ts` (25 fonts). Each is declared for the browser in `src/app/fonts.ts` with `next/font`, under the CSS variable `--font-<id>`, with `preload: false`: a browser fetches a font when a page first uses it. To add one, add it in both files; a test keeps them in step. Beside the library there are the owners' own fonts, uploaded as files (`src/server/fonts.ts`, the tables `site_fonts` and `site_font_files`): a `CustomFont`, whose id in the design is `custom:<uuid>`. A font is added with the owners' own words on the licence they hold. The name a browser gets for it (`uf-...`) is made from its id, and `fontFaceCss` writes the `@font-face` rules for the fonts a design uses, so nothing a person typed reaches the stylesheet. Reading the design needs the list of those fonts, which is why a `DocumentSpec` can have a `context`.
- **Uploaded files** (pictures and fonts) are stored through `FileStore` (`src/server/files.ts`). The one store there is keeps the bytes in the database (`site_files`), because no host has been chosen; a host's own file storage is another `FileStore`, chosen in `fileStore()`. A key names one file for good, so a file never changes once written.
- **The media library** (`src/server/media.ts`, the table `media`) is every picture the website can show. `prepareImage` reads an upload with `sharp` (which is also the check that it is a picture), turns it the way the camera held it, makes it no larger than 2560px, and saves it again in its own format, which leaves behind what the camera wrote into the file. Whatever shows a picture names it by its id and gets a `Picture` from `loadPictures`; its address (`mediaUrl`: `/media/<id>/<version>/<name>`) carries a version that goes up when the file is replaced, so a replaced picture changes everywhere at once. Pages show pictures with `next/image`, which is allowed to resize `/media/**` only (`next.config.ts`). A picture that is shown somewhere cannot be deleted: `mediaUses` looks in the studios' photos and floor plans, the collections and the text of every document, drafts included. The library has no drafts: a change shows at once.
- **Collections** (`src/server/collections.ts`) are named sets of pictures in the owners' order, with an optional first and last day outside which they show nothing (`collectionShows`). **A studio's photos** (`src/server/studio-photos.ts`, the table `studio_photos`) are pictures of the library too; the catalog carries them as `CatalogStudio.photos`, and a picture needs a description before it can be a studio's photo. **A studio's facts** (`src/lib/studio-facts.ts`, the column `studios.facts`) are what photographers choose a studio by: its size, ceiling, light, cyclorama and what comes with it, written under Studios & add-ons, and a floor plan, which is a picture of the library chosen on its own (`setStudioFloorPlan`: owners only, and described first, like a photo). Whatever is stored is read through `parseStudioFacts`. The catalog carries them as `CatalogStudio.facts` and `floorPlan`, and the block "Each studio in full" shows the ones that are written, and nothing where none is.
- **The website's editors** are client components in `src/components/site-editor/`: `useDocumentEditor` (the state: autosave of the draft, one save at a time, each naming the revision it was based on, so a change made elsewhere is noticed; from Publish, Discard or a version brought back until the page the server renders again is shown, the editor is busy and its form `inert`, and what went well is said with that page, in one transition around `router.refresh()`: a change made in between would be thrown away when the editor starts again, or published unchecked; a discard moves the revision on even with no draft stored, so the editor always starts again; while it is busy `change()` takes nothing, no key that changes a page is passed on (Puck's undo and redo, and Delete and Backspace, which delete the chosen block, on the whole document and in its canvas; and in a place that takes typing, any key that types: Enter and Space on a link or a button still work), and the conflict box's buttons wait; before a publish the draft is asked of the server even when nothing changed in this window (`save(false, true)`), so that a draft changed somewhere else is not checked and refused in this editor's name: with nothing changed here the editor shows it at once and says so (that it was changed there, or published or discarded there: the conflict's `waiting`), and with changes here the conflict box asks, naming who saved the draft that waits, or nobody when none waits; and Publish sends the revision that was checked, which the server holds the draft to: `publishDraft` and `publishPage` refuse one changed since, here or in another window, with `DRAFT_CHANGED`), `parts.tsx` (the bar with Save draft, Preview, Publish and Discard; the preview pane, which is the real site in a frame at five screen widths; the history; the form fields), `DesignEditor` and `FrameEditor`. Their server actions (`src/app/admin/website/actions.ts`) answer with a result and never redirect, unlike the admin's forms.
- **Pages made of blocks.** A page made under Admin, Website, Pages is a row of `site_documents` (kind `page`) with the same life as the design: a draft, a published copy, and a copy of every publish in `site_versions`. Its content is `{ meta: { title, description, hidden }, blocks }` (`src/lib/site/page.ts`). The blocks are the site's own: each is described once with `defineBlock` (`src/lib/site/blocks/`), as fields (text, rich, choice, toggle, number, link, picture, pictures, collection, list, slot), and three things follow from that description: the editor's form, a new block's values (`startProps`), and the check of whatever is stored or sent (`parseProps` in `check.ts`, which `parsePage` runs on every block). A field marked `phone: true` has a second value, `<name>Phone`, used below 640px: that is how a page differs on a phone without anything changing on a computer. `registry.ts` lists the blocks by the name pages store, so a block is never renamed. To add a block: describe it, list it, and style it in "Blocks of a page" in `globals.css`. A block placed straight on the page, outside a section, gets the page's width and margins from one rule there (`.blk-root .blk:not(...)`); a block drawn as full-width sections of its own is left out of it.
- **Blocks that show the studio's data, and live values.** The blocks of the group "From the studio's data" (`src/lib/site/blocks/site-data.tsx`: studio cards, each studio in full, the studios' prices, the add-ons' prices, the address, phone and hours, the address and map in a box, the contact form) draw what the admin's other pages hold, from the `catalog` and `business` that `pageSite` (`src/server/pages.ts`) gives every block; only their words are the block's own. The text of a page can name a live value (`src/lib/site/live-values.ts`): `{refund window}`, typed in a heading or in formatted text, shows the setting as it is now ("14 days"). `liveValues` is the list: the studio's name, address and phone, the notice and how far ahead bookings open, the refund window and fee, the rules for moving, the loyalty numbers, photography's notice, refund window and lowest price, the pet fee and its studios, the several-studios offer. `PageBlocks` puts them in after the HTML is cleaned, escaping each one; a name that is not on the list stays as typed, and the editor shows the names, with the list beside the page. A number from the settings that a page should state belongs in `liveValues`, with a test. A link cannot name a live value: a link in text is the text's own.
- **Pictures the old website's way (2026-11-19).** The title band (`Banner`) can have a picture behind it (`picture`, and `focus` with a phone value): it fills the band (`next/image` with `fill`, asked for at once with `loading="eager"` and `fetchPriority="high"`), a shade darkens it and the words turn white (`.blk-banner.has-picture` in `globals.css`). A gallery can be laid out in one row that scrolls sideways (`layout: "row"`), drawn by `PictureRow` (`src/components/site/PictureRow.tsx`, a client component): `columns` pictures show at once (a fraction shows a part of the next), one at a time on a phone unless a number is chosen for phones; two 44px arrows move it by a picture, and from the last back to the first, and show only when there are more pictures than show at once; the list carries `data-scrolls-sideways`, without which the phone check would take the pictures out of view for a page wider than the screen. The studio cards have a second look, `arches`: a half circle over each studio's first photo, the studio's name under it, both leading to the studio. "Each studio in full" shows a studio's first photo at 5:4 and its other photos large, in a `PictureRow` across the section (3.3 at once, 1.15 on a phone), where a strip of small photos was. The `Slideshow` block (`pictures.tsx`, same day, from the owner's "the original website has all 3 studios being rotated with arrows") is a list of up to eight slides, each a picture (with what stays in view), a title, words in italics (each line its own), and a button with its link. It is a `PictureRow` with one slide in view (`columns` 1 on both sizes), so a finger swipes it and the arrows ("Previous slide", "Next slide", props of `PictureRow`) go from the last slide back to the first; it does not move by itself, as the old one did not. Every slide's picture is asked for at once (the first with `fetchPriority="high"`), so the next is there when an arrow moves to it. A slide whose picture left the library is left out. `shade` is `light` (a pale veil and dark words, as the old site) or `dark` (the title band's shade and white words). Outside a section it goes from edge to edge, like the title band (`.blk-root .blk:not(…, .blk-slideshow)`); a slide is `min(78vh, 680px)` tall, `min(78vh, 640px)` on a phone, where the two arrows sit side by side under the words, as on the old site. `src/server/old-site-look.ts`, run by `scripts/match-old-site-look.ts` after the photo import, puts the old site's photos that way into the drafts of the home, photography and price pages: once (its blocks' ids start with `old-site-look`; a picture already chosen is kept), never on a page with a draft waiting, and found in the library by their names; the pages are then published in the page editor, which checks them on phones first. On the home page it puts the old site's slideshow at the top (`SLIDES`: Paris, Blanche and Dayglow, their photos `studio-<slug>-01.jpg`, the old words, each button booking its studio), only with all three photos, and turns the title band under it into words only: a `page` band, without the Paris photo the first run put behind it (a picture the owners chose stays).
- **What a page shows only at times** (`src/lib/site/conditions.ts`). A condition is a fact about what the site offers now: pets can come, some studio takes no pets, extra people or any add-on can be booked, a booking can be moved, free hours are on, the getting-in text is written, a photography session can be booked, several studios cost less. Every block has a last field of its own, "Show it" (`when`, added by `defineBlock`), with Always or one condition or its opposite. Words that start with `{if pets}` or `{if not pets}` show only while that holds: a paragraph, list item, heading or quote of formatted text (`showRichText` in `rich-text.ts`, which also drops a list or quote left empty), and a question, card, button or row of a table (the item's title field, in `PageBlocks`). Several markers in a row must all hold. A marker that names no condition is no marker and stays visible. The editor shows everything and marks a block whose "Show it" is set; Preview shows the page as visitors see it now. A new switch that a page should follow belongs in `CONDITIONS`, with a test. What conditions do not cover, a number set to 0, is the owners' to word.
- **The pages the website was built with** (`src/lib/site/built-in-pages.ts`): the home page and the six public pages that were written in code until 2026-11-16, as blocks with the wording they had, live values for every number and conditions where the code had an `if`. `ensureBuiltInPages` (`pages.ts`) puts each into a database once, published, with version 1 by "the site as it was built", and marks it with `site_documents.built_in`: run when PGlite opens (`getDb`) and by `npm run db:migrate`, never in the unit tests' `createTestWorld`. A page a database had once is never put back, whatever the owners did to it, so a deleted one waits in the trash for good (`emptyOldTrash` leaves it). Its history can "Start again from the page as it was built" (`restorePageVersion` with `BUILT_VERSION`). The home page is the page whose address is "": it cannot move, be taken offline or be deleted, and a copy of it goes to `/home-copy`. Changing the starting content changes nothing in a database that has the page already: it is only where a new database starts.
- **Formatted text** is HTML, cleaned by `sanitizeRichText` (`src/lib/site/rich-text.ts`, with `sanitize-html`) when a page is saved and again when it is drawn. It keeps paragraphs, bold, italic, underline, links (through `parseHref`), lists, h2 to h4, quotes and alignment, and nothing else. It never parses a style: a paragraph's or heading's `style` is written anew from its alignment alone (`aligned`, with `parseStyleAttributes: false`), because sanitize-html's style parsing runs postcss, which draws random numbers, and Next.js refuses `Math.random()` while it prerenders a page (the home page is prerendered). The cleaner is for the server only: `fields.ts`, the block files and `paragraphs.ts` go to the browser with the editor; `check.ts` and `rich-text.ts` must never be imported by anything a client component imports.
- **`src/server/pages.ts`** is the page store. `createPage` makes a draft, at an address made from the name unless one is typed; `duplicatePage` copies a page as a draft; `savePageDraft` has the design's revision check; `publishPage` is refused while a picture on the page has no description; `unpublishPage` keeps the page as a draft; `discardPageDraft`, `listPageVersions` and `restorePageVersion` work as for the design. `savePageSettings` changes the name and address at once; moving a published page writes a redirect from the old address and points earlier ones on. `deletePage` puts a page in the trash, `restorePage` brings it back as a draft, and `emptyOldTrash`, run by the scheduled task, removes it after `TRASH_DAYS`, unless the website was built with it. Old addresses are `site_redirects` (`findRedirect`, `addRedirect`, `removeRedirect`); `addRedirect` refuses one that would lead back to itself, by its path (the public route does not look at what follows ? or #, and the website takes off a slash at the end; a full address of the site itself counts as its path: any of the addresses it is known by, `siteOrigins` in runtime.ts, which are `SITE_URL` and the one the request came in at, with "www." or without, with a dot at the end or without, and with every name of the computer itself as one) or round through other old addresses (`roundBack`), where only a published page stops the round; paths are compared as a browser reads them ("." and ".." resolved, also written %2e, escapes decoded). A draft page moved onto an old address leaves it leading on until the page is published, unless it leads round to the page itself, and then it is dropped; what led to the page's old address follows it to the new, however it was written, and with its #section or ?query. Publishing a page drops an old address that led away from its own, so taking the page off later cannot start a round. `linksToPage` says, in words, what links to a page (`frameLinksTo` in `frame.ts`, `pageLinksTo` in `page.ts`), for the warning before a page leaves the website. Editing needs `editPages`; whatever changes what visitors see needs `publishWebsite` as well.
- **The public route for pages** is `src/app/[...slug]/page.tsx`. Next.js tries every other route first, so it only gets addresses the site has no route for. It shows the published page (`getPublishedPage`, cached with the tags `website` and `catalog`), or in a preview its draft (`sitePage` in `site.ts`). Otherwise an old address answers 308 (`getRedirect`) and anything else 404. A sample in `generateStaticParams` and `export const instant = false` are what let it answer with those status codes. A page is drawn by `PageBlocks` (`src/components/site/PageBlocks.tsx`), a server component that gives each block its values: formatted text as the cleaned HTML, a place for blocks as the drawing of its blocks. The home page has its own route, `src/app/page.tsx`, which draws the page at "" the same way. The addresses a page cannot take are `RESERVED_SEGMENTS` in `page.ts`: the site's own routes; a new route of the site's own needs its first part there too. `src/app/sitemap.ts` lists the published pages that are not hidden, the home page among them, and the booking steps; `src/app/robots.ts` keeps search engines out of the admin, the accounts and the private pages.
- **The page editor** is Puck 0.24.0 (`@puckeditor/core`, pinned exactly), loaded in the browser only (`PageEditorLoader`, with `ssr: false`). `buildPuckConfig` (`src/components/site-editor/puck-config.tsx`) makes Puck's configuration from the blocks, with the site's own fields for links, pictures and galleries (they open `PictureChooser`); `toPuckData` and `fromPuckData` turn the site's page into Puck's data and back without a change. `PageEditor` puts the site's bar above Puck (`useDraftEditor`: the autosave and conflict check of the design's editors) and gives Puck's frame the site's fonts. On a wide screen the page editor's admin page folds the admin's menu away (`AdminFrame` with `wide`).
- **The page editor's guard rails** (requirement 24). Before a page is published, the editor draws its draft as visitors would get it (`/admin/website/pages/[id]/check`, for whoever may edit it) in a frame 320px and then 390px wide, transparent and behind the editor, waits until the page is on screen with the site's stylesheet (it arrives in parts, shown by a script after the frame loads; a frame out of sight or hidden may never be drawn), and measures it with the phone tests' own checks (`src/lib/site/phone-checks.ts`: nothing wider than the screen, controls at least 40px tall, fields with 16px text, no text under 11px). A page with a problem is not published, and the editor says at which width and what, by its own words ("BOOK A STUDIO" reaches 336px; a long word that runs out of its own box, inside the screen, past the screen's edge, is named the same way) (`checkOnPhones` in `src/components/site-editor/phone-check.ts`, run by `useDraftEditor` through `DraftActions.check`). It is a guard against mistakes in the browser, not a lock: the server publishes what an editor sends. A change of the design, or of the header, menu and footer, reaches every page: before it is published, every published page (hidden ones included: they are public at their address), the two booking screens and the account are drawn as they will be once that one change is published (`checkWebsiteOnPhones`, with the list the server gives at the moment of publishing, `checkPathsAction` and `websiteCheckPaths`, so that a page published meanwhile is among them): at 320px, the home page and the booking page at 390px as well, three at a time. A page counts as drawn once its header is fixed by the stylesheet, every part the server streams is shown (React marks a part still to come with the comment `$?`, and with `$~` while it waits to be shown: `partStillComing`), and no `.spinner` stands where a part is still to come in the browser; a page with nothing on it is drawn, for it fits any phone. One that does not arrive in time is asked for once more, and a refusal names it. A problem found on many pages names a few and counts the rest, or says "every page" (`pagesListed`). A page with a problem is measured again as visitors have it now (`?website-check=published`): when it has no more problems of each kind than before and none of them worse (reaching further, or smaller to read or to touch; each sentence is read by how it ends, `SENTENCES`, so the words of the element it names, capitals and numbers included, do not count), they are not the change's doing (`broughtBy`), and "Published" names them as visitors have them once the change is out (`already`); a new kind of problem, or a wider page, refuses the change. The page editor's own check does the same for a page on the website, with its route's `?as=published`, so that a problem the studio's data makes on a page (a long word in an add-on) does not keep the page's own changes back. Each is asked for with `?website-check=design` or `frame`, which shows that document's draft and everything else as published, whatever other drafts wait; a page's own check asks with `page`, so the design and the header are as published there too (src/lib/site/website-check.ts).
- **Search engines and shared links.** The root layout sets `metadataBase` from `SITE_URL` (`metadataBase()` in `src/server/site.ts`) and the site's sharing card; never from the request's address, which would make every page dynamic (`siteUrl()` asks the request when `SITE_URL` is not set; `configuredSiteUrl()` does not). The home page, the pages of the admin and the two booking pages (`/book` and `/book/photography`, since 2026-11-21) give their own (`shareMetadata`: Open Graph and X's card, and the page's canonical address). A page written in code gives its `path` there, or it has no canonical address: `/book?studio=paris` is `/book` to a search engine. The sharing picture is the frame's `search.image`, chosen under Header, menu & footer, Search engines, else the first studio's first photo (`sharePicture`). The home page carries the studio as a `LocalBusiness` in JSON-LD (`src/lib/site/structured-data.ts`): the name, address, phone and map of the frame, and the opening hours of the studios; `jsonLd` writes `<` as an escape, so no text of the owners' can end the script. What the Reviews block shows is not given to search engines as review markup: they do not count the reviews a business shows of itself. In production `SITE_URL` must be set, or the sharing card has no picture address and the JSON-LD no address of the website.
- **`src/server/email.ts`** queues all mail in `email_outbox` (same transaction as the change it reports). A message that is of no use after a time carries `expires_at` (migration 0029; a sign-in code's email, which lasts the code's minutes): one still waiting then, as when no email service was set up yet, is not sent, and is marked out of date (`OUT_OF_DATE`). `queueEmail` answers the message's id, and `replaceQueuedEmail` puts a corrected message in the place of one that has not started on its way: a cancellation's email says its card refund is on its way, and is put right when the refund then does not go through (`refundNotSent` in manage.ts); one that has left already is followed by `refundNotYetEmail`. Delivery sends a message as it is once it has claimed it (the claim's `RETURNING`), so one put right a moment before goes out put right. Delivery happens right after the response and again from the scheduled task: through Resend when `RESEND_API_KEY` and `EMAIL_FROM` are set, to the server log outside production, and not at all in production without those two (mail then waits in the table). A message can carry a reply address (`reply_to`).
- **Memberships (follow-up 3, being built from 2026-11-18).** `src/server/memberships.ts`. Step 1 of the plan under follow-up 3: the plans (`membership_plans`, and `membership_plan_studios` for the studios each is for), listed, changed, added and switched on and off by the owners under Admin → Memberships. A plan is for studios that can be booked together (`studioCombinationError`, the same rule as the booking screen's); its studios stay as made, and it is never deleted. The plans on sale travel in the public catalog (`Catalog.membershipPlans`), so the plan actions make the catalog again. Step 2: `joinMembership` buys one (`/book/membership`, `MembershipCheckout`, the server action `joinMembershipAction`, limited like bookings) and `cancelMembership` cancels one (`/account/membership`). The card processor has a `memberships` part (`MembershipBilling` in `src/server/payments.ts`: `createCustomer`, `saveCard`, `createPlanPrice`, `subscribe`, `cancelSubscription`, `subscriptionState`), in Square's version, the pretend one (which keeps nothing: a subscription's first day and price are in its id, and it bills every month as days pass) and the test world's (`world.subscriptions`, `world.billMonth`, `world.failMembership`). A membership's minutes are the sum of its lines in `membership_minutes`; each month paid adds one line, at most once (`payment_id` is unique). Square's card form is loaded by `src/components/square-card.ts` for the booking screen and the checkout alike. Step 5: `listMembers`, `memberDetail` and `correctMinutes` behind Admin → Memberships and the member's page. Step 4: `runMembershipBilling` (each month, from the scheduled task; `finishStarting` is the end of buying, shared by `joinMembership` and the carrying on of one left half-done). Step 3: a membership's code is recognised by its `MEMBER-` (`isMembershipCode`) and checked by `findUsableMembership`; `quote()` takes `membership` (minutes left, the weekend rule) and gives `membershipMinutes` and `membershipCents`; the booking takes the minutes with `takeBookingMinutes` (`src/server/membership-minutes.ts`, which locks the membership) and an unmade one gives them back with `giveBackUnmadeMinutes`; a cancellation gives them back with a `returned` line (`minutesComeBack` in manage.ts); `rescheduleOutcome` refuses a booking with minutes (`membership`). The name, email and phone rules and `hashToken` live in `src/server/input-fields.ts`, shared by bookings and memberships, which import each other. All five steps of the plan are built. After the tester's pass of 2026-11-18: the checkout sends the price and minutes it showed (`expected` in `joinMembership`), and a plan changed since is refused with nothing charged (`price_changed`); `recordPaidMonths` records each month Square was paid, used by `settleMonths` and by `cancelMembership`, which records a month taken before the cancelling first; `runMembershipBilling` asks about an active membership whose month has come or that has a problem (hourly until it is put right), and about a cancelled one until its months up to its end are recorded; a problem stays, untold again, until no month is left unpaid; the account's My Membership lists every change to the hours (`minutesLinesOf`); the booking screen applies a membership's hours only to the studios of its plan (`studioIds` in the quote's `membership`).
- **The emails' wording (Admin → Emails, 2026-11-18).** Every email to a client is written from a template in `src/lib/email-templates.ts` (`EMAIL_TEMPLATES`): a subject and a text with parts in double braces, each part listed with what it holds, whether only some of these emails have it (`only`), whether it can be several lines (`lines`, never in the subject), and whether the email needs it (`required`).
