A builder in `email.ts` works out the parts (`written`: the money lines, refund sentences and codes are still the site's sentences) and renders the site's template, and keeps the parts on the message (`QueuedEmail.template`). `queueEmail` and `replaceQueuedEmail` render the owners' wording instead when they have saved one that passes `templateProblems` (`ownersWording` in `src/server/email-templates.ts`), then put in the studio's name and address (`{{studio_name}}`, `{{studio_address}}`) from the published contact details. `renderEmail` drops a line that holds nothing but empty parts, never leaves two empty lines together, and puts each part in once (what a part holds is not read for parts). Table `email_templates`: a row per email the owners changed or set back, the subject and text null for the site's wording, and `revision`, which a save sends back so that one made over someone else's is refused. The studio's own notices (`STUDIO_NOTICES`, listed on the page) keep the site's wording. Only owners open the page.
- **`src/server/contact.ts`** takes messages from the contact form (the block of that name; its action is `src/components/contact-actions.ts`). A message is not stored on its own: it becomes one queued email to the owner (`OWNER_NOTIFY_EMAIL`, else the first of `OWNER_EMAILS`) with the visitor's address as the reply address. Nothing is ever emailed to the address a visitor typed. With no owner address the form refuses messages and gives the phone number.
- **`src/server/admin.ts`** holds what only the owner can do: staff and shifts, prices, special dates, opening hours, blocked time, the bookings list, codes, each studio's text, guest number and clean-up time, and the add-ons.
- **Importing Acuity's appointments still to come (step 5 of the plan in `HANDOFF.md`, 2026-11-19).** Before the switch, each upcoming appointment in Acuity becomes a booking here that holds its studios and the photographer, so that the site books around it.
  - *Running it:* `scripts/import-acuity.ts <export.csv> --as <owner> [--money live|test] [--remind] [--write]`, run by an owner from `web/` against the site's database. Without `--write` it is a rehearsal.
  - *Reading the export:* `src/lib/acuity.ts` reads Acuity's appointments export (CSV). The columns are found by their names, each by its names in order wherever it is ("Appointment Price" before a question called "Price"), a missing one the import needs stops it, and a row whose time or amount cannot be read is set aside with the reason. Times are on the studio's clock, in the ways Acuity writes them; a row whose Timezone column names another zone is set aside. Acuity's Email field can hold several addresses, separated by commas: the first is the client's (`email`), the others stay apart (`otherEmails`, question 50).
  - *The real export* (the owners', 2026-11-20) had 21 columns and no intake questions: Start Time, End Time, Timezone, First Name, Last Name, Phone, Email, Type, Calendar, Appointment Price, Paid?, Amount Paid Online, Certificate Code, Notes, Date Scheduled, Label, Scheduled By, Date Rescheduled, Canceled, Date Canceled and Appointment ID. Its times read like "March 3, 2027 9:20 am", all in America/New_York; its amounts "68.00"; Canceled is "canceled" or empty, so an export may list cancelled appointments. Its 30 appointment types all name their studios, an add-on taken written into the name ("Studio Dayglow Booking (1 hr) + Bringing a Pet? Add Required Pet Fee"). 7 of its 189 rows had more than one address in Email.
  - *Which studios:* read from the appointment type's name ("Blanche + Paris (2 hr)", "Photography DAYGLOW (1 hr)", which also holds the photographer). Acuity books every bundle on Paris's calendar, so the calendar is used only when the type names no studio.
  - *The booking* (`importAcuityAppointments` in `src/server/acuity-import.ts`):
    - It keeps Acuity's price (`total_cents`). A bundle's price is shared between its studios as the site's own prices share theirs (`shareOf`).
    - It is `confirmed`, and `made_by` the owner who imports, so it reads as a booking made in the admin: the rest is paid at the studio.
    - It takes the site's terms of the day (`terms`), with none accepted.
    - Acuity's id goes in `bookings.imported_from` (`acuity:<id>`, unique, migration `0049_acuity_import`), so a second run adds only what is new.
  - *Money paid in Acuity* becomes payments recorded by hand, with the method `acuity` ("Through Acuity, before this website"): what was paid online, and, when Acuity marks the appointment paid, the rest. The note says which, and names a code that paid. The site cannot refund these.
    - The price summary says "Paid through Acuity" (`receivedThroughAcuityCents` in `BookingView`), not "at the studio".
    - A receipt says "Through Acuity, before this website" (`handPaymentWords`).
    - A cancellation, its email, the owner's notice and the appointment's page say what of it is given back by hand was paid through Acuity, not at the studio (`handPaidPlaceOf`).
  - *Sends nothing:* nothing is charged and no email is sent. `reminder_sent_at` is set unless `--remind`, since Acuity sends its own reminder while it holds the appointments.
  - *Holds:* written as `createBooking` writes them, each studio's clean-up gap after the booking and the photographer's after a session. The rules a client meets (opening hours, notice, the photographer's hours) do not apply: Acuity booked these.
  - *Refused, with the reason:*
    - an appointment whose time is taken here, by a booking, by blocked time, or by another appointment of the export once the site's gap is added (the report names what takes it);
    - one the site cannot hold: no studio named, Blanche and Dayglow without Paris, no email address, or more paid online than its price.
  - *Left out:* past and cancelled appointments.
  - *One transaction:* everything runs in one transaction, with a savepoint for each appointment. A rehearsal throws the transaction away at the end, so it reports exactly what writing would.
  - *The report* names appointments by row, Acuity id, time and type, never a client.
  - *Real or test money:* the kind of the card processor set up where the script runs (the `SQUARE_` settings), or else `--money`. A script never takes the pretend processor's, which it would get by default.
  - *What it decides for the owners:* question 49 under "Rules built on the builder's reading", which the owner confirmed as built on 2026-11-20, and question 50 (several email addresses), which waits for an "OK".
- **Importing Acuity's history (step 7 of the plan in `HANDOFF.md`, 2026-11-20).** The past visits of an Acuity export become read-only bookings, so that a client's account, their receipts and the admin show the visits made before this site.
  - *Running it:* `scripts/import-acuity.ts <export.csv> --history --as <owner> [--money live|test] [--write]`, as for the appointments still to come; without `--write` it is a rehearsal. One export serves both imports, each taking what is its own (`importAcuityHistory` in `src/server/acuity-history.ts`).
  - *Which appointments:* those over when it runs and not cancelled in Acuity; one not over yet is the other import's. Each is brought in once, by one import or the other: `imported_from` is `acuity-history:<id>` here and `acuity:<id>` there, and each looks for both (`ACUITY_SOURCE`, `ACUITY_HISTORY_SOURCE` in `src/lib/acuity.ts`).
  - *The booking:* completed, or a no-show where Acuity's label says so; Acuity's price; made by the owner who imports. Its studios are in holds that are not active, so it holds no time: the rule against overlaps and the booking screen leave such holds out. A session names the photographer, with no hold. `reminder_sent_at`, `review_requested_at` and `loyalty_issued_at` are set at the import, so the scheduled task sends nothing for it.
  - *Money:* what Acuity records as paid becomes payments recorded by hand through Acuity, as for the appointments still to come (question 49 (b)), never more than the visit's price; what was paid online beyond it is named in the visit's history (question 52).
  - *Loyalty:* `loyaltyStandingOf` leaves these bookings out, so they count for no stamp (question 51).
  - *Set aside, with the reason:* no email address, a type and calendar that name no studio (workshops, content days, setup previews), Acuity's Reschedule Pending, and a visit Acuity marks unpaid (questions 52 and 53).
  - *The owners' export of 2026-11-20* had 3,709 rows from 2023-03-10 to 2027-01-17, with the same 21 columns as the export of appointments still to come. Rehearsed on a new local database: 3,226 past visits would come in (27 of them no-shows), 173 are not over (the other import's), 196 were cancelled, 113 are set aside (50 name no studio, 43 are marked unpaid, 18 have no email address, 2 are Reschedule Pending), and one row is in another time zone. Acuity's prices included 6.5% until 2025 ($85.20 for $80); in 2026 they are whole dollars.
  - *What it decides for the owners:* questions 51 to 53, which wait for an "OK".
- **`src/server/rate-limit.ts`** is an in-memory sliding-window limiter used by the booking endpoint. `limitRate` keeps the counts the other limits share in the database (`rate_limit_hits`, keyed by an IP address with what it asked for), and the daily task removes those older than two days (`pruneRateLimits`).
- **`src/server/email-change.ts`** is a client's change of their email address (question 27; the readings in question 58). `requestEmailChange` emails a code to the new address (`email_changes`, migration 0053, the code kept hashed, as at sign-in); `confirmEmailChange` counts each try, then, in one transaction with the account locked, looks again for records under the new address and a running membership, and moves the account, bookings, codes, loyalty's corrections, memberships and the access log (`holdsRecords`, `hasRunningMembership`). The form is `ChangeEmailForm` on the account's Overview; `earlierAddresses` gives the admin's customer page its "Earlier address".
- **`src/server/code-balance.ts`** is "Check Code Balance" on the booking pages (the owner's brief of 2026-11-22, item 5; the readings in question 60). `checkCodeBalance` looks a code up among the promo codes and the memberships' codes and says what it is, whether it can be used, what is left, when it ends and what it is for, by the checkout's rules, naming no one. It changes nothing. The page asks it through `POST /api/code-balance`, limited per address; the form is `CodeBalanceCheck`.
- **`src/server/customer-views.ts`** is the access log of the customers' pages (`customer_views`, migration 0052; question 27, the readings in question 57). `recordCustomerView` writes a line when a customer's page is on screen (`RecordView`, a client component that sends the browser's beacon from an effect to `POST /admin/customers/<email>/viewed`, which takes only same-site requests: a page drawn for a prefetch writes nothing, as Next.js 16's guide on prefetching warns) and when a receipt is downloaded (the two PDF routes). The same viewer, thing and detail within `VIEW_GAP_MINUTES` add nothing. `listCustomerViews` gives the owners the newest lines on the customer's page.
- **`src/server/retention.ts`** deletes, from the daily task, what is kept only as a record once it is older than the owners' retention period (`retentionYears`, 5 years, Settings → Records; the owner's answer of 2026-11-21): an email's copy (`email_outbox`) counted from when it was written, a sign-in code from when it was given, a session from when it ended, a line of the access log from when it was written (question 56). Years are calendar years (`retentionCutoff`).
- **Personal data and where it goes** (read from the code on 2026-11-21 for the privacy policy's draft, the owners' private doc "Privacy policy: draft for legal review"; when a flow below changes, the policy changes too):
  - **Kept here:** a client's name, email address, phone and notes on `bookings` (and on `users` and `memberships`); a booking's add-ons and codes; its payments (`payments`: the processor, its payment id, the amount, the method and who recorded it; never a card number) and refunds; its history (`booking_events`, naming the staff member who changed it); stamps and codes by email address (`loyalty_ledger`, `promo_codes.email`); every email sent, its whole body included (`email_outbox`; a contact-form message is an email to the owners); sign-in codes and the tokens of sessions and booking links as SHA-256 hashes only; the rate limits' counts by IP address (above); and from Acuity, the name, first email address, phone, notes, prices and payments, with an Email field's other addresses in the booking's history.
  - **Sent out:** Square gets the card in its own form (`web.squarecdn.com`), the buyer's email address, the booking's reference and a note with each payment, the name and email address for the bank's check (`buyerVerification`), and a customer, a card on file and a subscription for a membership. Resend gets each email. Vercel serves every request, and its logs hold IP addresses; Neon holds the database (staging's in AWS US East 1).
  - **In the browser:** one cookie of the site's, `vs_session` (HttpOnly, Secure in production, SameSite Lax, 30 days), and Next's draft-mode cookie for staff previewing the website. No analytics or advertising script. A video loads only once pressed, from `youtube-nocookie.com` or from Vimeo with `dnt=1`; Google Maps is a link.
  - The studio's own people: who opened a customer's page or downloaded their receipts, and when (`customer_views`, since 2026-11-21), read by the owners only.
  - Email copies (`email_outbox`, contact-form messages among them), sign-in codes, ended sessions and the access log are kept for the owners' retention period, 5 years since the owner's answer of 2026-11-21 (Settings → Records, `retentionYears`), then deleted by the daily task (`deleteOldRecords`, `src/server/retention.ts`). Bookings, payments, clients and their history have no end yet.
- **`/dev/outbox`** lists queued emails, including sign-in codes, and can run the scheduled tasks on demand. It answers "not found" in production. Never expose a non-production build to the internet.

### Conventions and gotchas

- **Next.js 16 differs from older versions.** Read the bundled guide in `web/node_modules/next/dist/docs/` before using an API you have not verified there (`web/AGENTS.md` says the same).
- **Cache Components is on.** A page that reads uncached data must put it behind `<Suspense>` or cache it with `'use cache'`. A page rendered per request (see `src/app/booking/[ref]/page.tsx`) calls `await connection()` first. Never cache bookings or availability.
- **A page published from another server reaches the hosted preview by the cache's clock.** Publishing refreshes the cache of the server that published (`updateTag(WEBSITE_TAG)`). A local server on staging's database cannot refresh Vercel's. The public pages' cached data lives for minutes (`cacheLife("minutes")` in `src/server/cached.ts`), so the hosted preview shows the change a minute or two later, with no redeploy. A page published in the hosted preview's own admin shows at once.
- **The linter rejects `setState` called synchronously in an effect.** Derive values during render, or set state from event handlers and promise callbacks.
- **Money is integer cents. Times in the database are `timestamptz`.** Domain functions work with a `YYYY-MM-DD` date and minutes from local midnight.
- **Never trust totals from the browser.** `POST /api/bookings` recomputes the price and compares it with `expectedTotalCents`, so a customer is never charged an amount they did not see.
- **Text from a form goes through `formText`** (`src/lib/parse.ts`) before it is measured or stored. A browser sends a line break as two characters while a field's `maxlength` counts it as one; without this, text the field accepted is refused as too long. It also drops the NUL character, which PostgreSQL refuses. Text of one line has its inner line breaks and tabs made spaces as well: `lineField` (`src/server/input-fields.ts`) for a customer's name and phone number in a booking and a membership's checkout, as the contact form does for its name. A name is in the greeting of every email about its booking, where a line break would begin a paragraph of the typist's own (2026-11-21). Notes keep their lines (`textField`).
- **Amounts the owner types go through `dollarsToCents`.** It takes a comma only between thousands: "12,50" is refused, not read as $1,250. Money fields spread `MONEY_FIELD`, so the browser applies the same pattern before the form is sent.
- **An admin form the server refuses comes back with the stored values, not with what was typed.** The browser's own checks (`required`, `type="number"` with a range, `maxLength`, the money pattern) are what keeps typed text from being lost, so give every new field the check that matches the server's: `EMAIL_FIELD`, `MONEY_FIELD`, `PRICE_FIELD` (up to $100,000) and `CREDIT_FIELD` (above zero) in `src/lib/parse.ts`. There is one rule for an email address, `EMAIL_RULE` (the booking's own `z.email()`): sign-in (`normalizeEmail`), bookings, the admin's forms and the fields' pattern follow it, and a test keeps them in step. The booking screen, sign-in and the contact form follow it too, and the booking's own refusals name a field in the screen's words (`FIELD_WORDS` in bookings.ts). A number field lets through what `intInRange` reads ("5.0", "1e3", ".5e2", "0050"). Text that must say something (a picture's description, a review) is checked with `hasVisibleText` (`src/lib/visible-text.ts`), which counts neither spaces nor characters that show nothing. The settings' ranges are `NUMBER_SETTING_RANGES` (`src/server/settings.ts`), which the Settings form reads for its `min` and `max`; their words are in `src/app/admin/settings/fields.ts`.
- **A sentence with a number the owner can change** uses `count` and `hoursInWords` from `src/lib/words.ts` ("1 day", "100 hours", "7 days"), and has to read well when the number is 0 or 1.
- **A code's issue date is the moment of the change that issued it** (`issuedAt` in `issuePromo`), not the database's clock: a reward shows the day it was earned.
- **A PDF knows Western European letters only.** The receipt files use the fonts every PDF reader has built in. `pdfSafe` writes any other letter as its plain letter or as "?", so a name in another alphabet cannot break a download; the screen shows it in full.
- **Constraints written by hand are not in `schema.ts`.** Add new ones with `npx drizzle-kit generate --custom --name <name>`.
- **Try a new migration on a copy of the local database before committing it.** Stop the development server, copy `web/.data/pglite` somewhere else, open the copy with PGlite and its `btree_gist` extension, run Drizzle's `migrate` with `web/drizzle`, and look at what it left. Migrations 0011 to 0029 were tried this way.
- **A migration that was pushed is never edited.** Drizzle runs only the migrations newer than the last one a database applied, so an edited migration never runs again where its first version ran: correct it with a new one, written so that it does no harm where the first was already right (0025 corrects figures of 0024 that way). 0024 was edited once, on 2026-11-17, while no database that is kept had run it: the development database had not reached it, and the browser tests' databases are made anew at every run.
- **A date written while a page is drawn must come out the same in Node and in every browser.** Intl joins a date and a time with words of its own, and they differ between engines: "Nov 20, 2026 at 2:30 AM" in Node, "Nov 20, 2026, 2:30 AM" in Safari. A client component that formats a date the server also drew then fails to hydrate in Safari, and React draws the page again. Use `studioClock` and `studioDayAndClock` (`src/lib/domain/time.ts`), which take only the parts from Intl and join them here, or Luxon's `toFormat` on the server. Found in the page editor's history by the browser suite in WebKit (2026-11-20).
- **Safari ignores the height of a select drawn in its own style.** The touch rules ask for 44px; WebKit drew the booking page's extra guests and the contact form's topic 24px tall. On a touch screen or a narrow one, selects are drawn plain (`appearance: none`) with an arrow of their own (`globals.css`).
- **The browser suite also runs in WebKit, Safari's engine**, which every iPhone uses: `npx playwright install webkit` once, then `E2E_NAME=webkit E2E_PORT=3214 npx playwright test --browser=webkit`. Differences that are the tools', not the site's:
  - the clipboard is given and read back only in Chromium;
  - WebKit reports a request cut off by leaving the page as an error of the page left ("Load failed"), such as the rest of a server action's answer when a test opens the next page as soon as the result shows; Next's development tools may then add errors of their own. The tests' one browser watcher, `watchBrowser` in `e2e/helpers.ts`, leaves out "Load failed" only while a page is being left, and the development tools' messages (`frameworkNoise`) always: no production page has them;
  - in the same way, WebKit reports a part of the site's code that was still loading when the page was left as "Failed to load chunk /_next/static/chunks/… from module …" (the page editor's text-editor code, prosemirror-view, when a test opens the next page as soon as "Page made." shows: CI's `webkit` on 2026-11-21, and one local run in three on 2026-11-22). The trace shows the download "Load request cancelled" and the error coming while the next page is on its way. `watchBrowser` leaves it out only then (`cutOffChunk`): a chunk that fails at any other moment still fails the test;
  - WebKit's page editor takes no simulated drag whose mouse jumps onto the page in one burst of moves, as Playwright's `mouse.move` with `steps` makes it: no drop line is ever drawn, and Puck drops a block nowhere else. A person's mouse glides, and so does the test's drag (`dragBlockOnce`: a move each time the browser draws), which lets go once the editor has drawn its line (`[data-puck-line-placeholder]`);
  - the accessibility scan (`@axe-core/playwright`) finishes in a blank page it opens in the same browser context and then closes. In WebKit a press 1 ms after that did nothing, once (the membership test, 2026-11-21), so `accessibilityProblems` brings the page under test back to the front before it returns;
  - in WebKit, Playwright's `fill("")` on a date field that had a value leaves the field failing the form's own check (`checkValidity()` false, though `validity.valid` is true and there is no message), even once it is left, so the form sends nothing and the next page never comes (2026-11-21, a collection's dates). A person who empties the field with Backspace, a part at a time, leaves it valid, and so does a value set by a script. A test empties a date field that way: Backspace and the arrow keys, as in the collections test of `website.spec.ts`.

  CI runs it too, in the job `webkit`, on Linux, and `master` requires it to pass since 2026-11-20.
- **Square replays a request only when it is the same.** Under an idempotency key used before, any other detail (a new card token, another note) is refused with `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED`, and the first request may have gone through. A retry sends the same request again (`useCardForm` keeps the attempt's token), and that refusal is read as no answer (`charge` in payments.ts). Seen in Square's sandbox on 2026-11-20.
- **A test that something is absent from serialized data does not look for a few digits.** The random ids in it hold any four digits now and then: a door code, "4821", was found inside an id in about one run in twenty. Look for an amount as a value of its own (`asAmount` in `src/server/testing.ts`), or for text no id can hold.
- **`web/.env.local` holds staging's `DATABASE_URL`**, written by `neon link`, and `next dev`, `next build` and `next start` read it. Started without `DATABASE_URL=""`, a local server runs on the database that the hosted preview shares, with whatever card processor its own environment sets. The browser tests blank it (`playwright.config.ts`); a script run with `tsx` reads only its own environment. On 2026-11-20 a scratch server for Square's sandbox was started without it and made five test bookings on staging. They were undone; see the decision log.
- **PostgreSQL does not index a foreign key by itself.** A column that pages look rows up by (a booking's payments) needs an index of its own in the schema, beside its `.references()`. Migration `0050` added the four that were missing (2026-11-20).
- **PGlite's data folder can be opened by one process at a time.** Stop `npm run dev` before running anything else against `web/.data`. `next build` without `DATABASE_URL` prerenders from an in-memory copy of the starting catalog.
- **Tests** come in four kinds: domain unit tests; server tests against an in-memory PGlite, set up by `src/server/testing.ts` (the real constraints are exercised); component tests of the booking screen in jsdom; and browser tests in `web/e2e/`. The pretend processor has a card whose every refund is refused (`MOCK_TOKEN_REFUNDS_REFUSED`), which a browser test sends in place of the usual one, so that a refund that fails can be tried in a browser. Resend has never been called. Square has been called in its sandbox only, by `npm run check:square`, which is not part of `npm test`.
- **The accessibility tests** (`e2e/a11y.spec.ts`) scan the public pages, every step of a booking, a customer's account and the pages of the admin with axe-core, against WCAG 2.1 A and AA. They run first, on an empty database: the pages that need a booking, a customer or a page are scanned in the other test files, where those exist, with `accessibilityProblems` (`e2e/helpers.ts`, whose `exclude` leaves out what is not the site's own). Colour contrast is left out: the brand's colours are the owners' choice. A new page or control has to pass them: a link inside a sentence is underlined (the rule after the base link style in `globals.css`), and a control named with `aria-label` has its visible words in that name.
- **The mobile tests** (`e2e/z-mobile.spec.ts`) measure with the functions of `src/lib/site/phone-checks.ts`, which the page editor runs too: each must stay complete in itself, because Playwright sends it into the page as text. They run after `site.spec.ts`, on the database it leaves, with the fourth owner address (`owner4@studio.test`) and their own customer and days. The touch rules (controls at least 40px tall, fields with 16px text) apply below 760px and on any touch screen (`(pointer: coarse)` in globals.css): a tablet is measured against them with touch emulated (an iPad, 768px, and held sideways, 1180px, where the whole menu shows, in its own context with the twelfth owner), which a browser at that width without touch would not show. A new page or control has to pass them: nothing wider than the screen (only a box made to scroll sideways excuses what is wider: `.data-table-wrap`, `.blk-table-wrap`, or one marked `data-scrolls-sideways`; the admin's column, which scrolls up and down and so sideways too by the rules of CSS, does not, so a wide table on an admin page goes in a `.data-table-wrap`, and the admin's buttons, which often carry a name the owners typed, wrap it: `.admin-main .btn`, with `overflow-wrap: anywhere`, so that the row or the box it is in can be narrower than that word; but not in a table's cell, where `anywhere` lets the table squeeze a button, or a summary, to a letter's width: there words stay whole and the table scrolls in its frame), controls at least 40px tall, fields with 16px text, and no word broken in two that would have fitted on its line (`brokenWords`, which the page editor does not run yet; a word in a box of a flex row, a button among buttons, could have had the row's width less that box's own padding (a box in a grid's track has what the track gives it, as a column's width); a box as wide as its words, a button, its container's; a table's cell the table's room less what the row's other cells need at the least, their longest words, or any width in a frame that scrolls sideways; a block, a flex or a grid box in a cell, however deep, the cell's, less what the other columns need at the least, their longest words, fields and pictures, over every row, with a cell that spans rows or columns placed as the browser places it, in an HTML table and in one made with CSS; a block inside a box of a flex or grid row, through blocks only, that box's; a table in a flex or grid row climbs on to the row, and one inside an inline element to the box around that; a table with fixed columns, or one given a width narrower than the room around it, gives a cell only what it has; the first phone test runs the check on made-up layouts, each with the word it must report). A row of two sides, a label and its price, never squeezes either: in `.price-row` the first side may be as narrow as its longest word (`flex-basis: min-content`), and the second moves to a line of its own only when a word would otherwise break; an add-on row has its name and price on one such line (`.addon-head`) and its description below, so a word is broken only when it is longer than a whole line, as a web address in a description is. The phone tests measure the booking screens, the booking page, the account and /rates with an add-on of such words, which they add (`LONG_ADDON`). The rules that make this so are the last blocks of `globals.css` ("Phones").
- **Browser tests** start their own dev server on port 3211 with a throwaway database (`.data/e2e`), their own build folder (`.next-e2e`), `SITE_URL` set to their own address, the mock processor and fourteen owners (`owner@studio.test` to `owner14@studio.test`). Sign-in codes are limited to three per address in 15 minutes, and the first three owners each use all three in `site.spec.ts`; the fourth is for the mobile tests, the fifth for the customers test, the sixth to tenth for `website.spec.ts`, the eleventh for `a11y.spec.ts`, the twelfth for the tablet test in `z-mobile.spec.ts`, the thirteenth for the emails and memberships tests in `site.spec.ts`, and the fourteenth for its test of a move that lowers a booking's price. In the page editor a block is dragged with `dragBlock` (`website.spec.ts`), which puts it in the middle of the list of blocks (giving the list room below its last block, so that it too can be put there) (a drag that starts at the list's edge makes the editor scroll by itself, and the drop lands elsewhere) and moves the mouse in steps. It judges a drag by its outcome, a block more on the page, and makes it again, more slowly, when the editor missed it, as it did twice on the tester's machine under load; those tests use a window 1200px high, so that the editor is on screen. The editor draws the page zoomed out, which Playwright's `boundingBox` does not count: a drop at the bottom of a block (the default) lands on the page's top level, after everything; to drop into an empty place for blocks, such as a column, use `"middle"`, which aims through the zoom (`onCanvas`). Change Puck's fields one at a time, waiting for each to show on the page: two changes made at once can lose the second. An item of a list in Puck's panel (a review, a card) opens when its summary is pressed (`[class*="ArrayFieldItem-summary"]`, "New" while its title is empty). Puck keeps a copy of the panel out of sight, so a locator that is not a role locator needs `onScreen`, or the click waits for the hidden copy until the test times out. The files run in the order of their names: `a11y.spec.ts`, `site.spec.ts`, `website.spec.ts`, `z-mobile.spec.ts`. `website.spec.ts` changes the design and the menu and must end with them as the site was built, because the phone tests after it measure the site's own design. The two photos, the facts and the floor plan it gives Studio Blanche stay, so the phone tests also measure a studio that has all of them. `accessibilityProblems` (`e2e/helpers.ts`) is the accessibility scan's check, for a test that wants to scan a page it changed. It waits for the page's title first: after a form is sent, the framework puts the title back a moment after the page, and a scan in between reports a page without one. Its pictures are made in the test with `sharp`; its font files are two real ones that come with Next.js, read from `node_modules`. When a new test needs an owner and they have run out, add another in `playwright.config.ts`. The suite books from one address more than ten times a minute, so its server raises the booking limit with `BOOKING_REQUESTS_PER_MINUTE` (ignored in production); without it the last tests fail with "Too many requests". They read sign-in codes from `/dev/outbox`. Before the tests start, every page is asked for once (`e2e/global-setup.ts`), so that the development server compiles it then and not while a test waits: a new page, or a new route with an id in its address, goes on that list, or the first test to open it fails on a busy machine. They share one database and run in order, so a single test run with `-g` only works if it does not depend on an earlier one. Any error in the browser console fails the test. A second run can go on at the same time under its own name: `E2E_NAME=tester E2E_PORT=3213` gives it `.data/e2e-tester`, `.next-e2e-tester` and `test-results-tester`. `tsconfig.json` already lists the `tester` build folder; with another name Next.js adds lines there (and reformats the file) on its first start: put the file back (`git checkout web/tsconfig.json`) and remove that run's `.data/` and `.next-` folders before committing.
- **CI retries no failed browser test** (`retries: 0` in `playwright.config.ts`). `site.spec.ts` and `website.spec.ts` (46 of the 63 tests) are serial: Playwright retries such a file whole, from its first test, on the database the failed try left, and they sign in with several owner addresses three times each, the most the site allows in 15 minutes, so the second pass is refused a code. Only the 17 tests of the other two files could be retried one at a time. In CI's first 87 runs (to 2026-11-20) a browser job failed by chance twice, and a retry would have saved neither: once the runner was lost, and once the Logo group's hydration warning (2026-11-19) failed a test of a serial file. Read a failed job first, then run it once more (`gh run rerun <id> --failed`). A failure that does not come back is a flake: record it in `HANDOFF.md` and fix its cause. Retries can come once each test makes the data it needs. CI also refuses a `test.only` (`forbidOnly`), which would let a job pass on one test.
- **The browser suite also runs against a production build**, in WebKit, served over HTTPS as a host serves the site: CI's job `webkit-production`, which `master` requires to pass since 2026-11-20, or `E2E_BUILD=1 E2E_NAME=prod E2E_PORT=3215 npx playwright test --browser=webkit` (about 8 minutes). It tests what the development server does not have: the production bundle, prefetching (only a production build prefetches the links on screen), prerendering and its caches, and Secure cookies. How it is set up (`playwright.config.ts`):
  - `scripts/e2e-database.ts` makes the tests' database first, as the server would on its first start, and writes a copy of it, which `next build` reads (`PGLITE_BUILD_SNAPSHOT`, `src/db/client.ts`). A build from a database of its own gave the pages other ids than the server's, and the booking screen found no times: a page is served from the build until it has been drawn again, in the background.
  - `e2e/https-server.mjs` serves the build with Next.js's own request handler over HTTPS, with a certificate made for the run (`openssl`). Over plain http WebKit keeps no Secure cookie, and a production build makes the session's and the preview's cookies Secure. The server asks itself for pages too (the page a server action sends the browser to), so Node trusts the certificate through `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`.
  - A production server offers the tests' tools (the outbox at `/dev/outbox`, its task runner, the raised request limits, the email log in place of a service) only when it is the tests' beyond doubt: `mayUseTestTools` (`src/server/browser-test-server.ts`) wants `BROWSER_TEST_SERVER=1`, no `DATABASE_URL`, `ALLOW_PGLITE_IN_PRODUCTION=1`, `PAYMENTS_MODE=mock` and no `RESEND_API_KEY`, and a live site has a database address. Development is as before. The tests' server blanks `RESEND_API_KEY` in both modes.
  - Nothing is warmed up first (`e2e/global-setup.ts`): the build has compiled every page.

  What the tests do for it: `watchBrowser` leaves out a prefetch cut off by the next page, which WebKit reports as "Fetch API cannot load … due to access control checks", at times after that page has arrived (`cutOffPageData`, for the framework's page data only); a link to a page drawn when it is asked for is followed with `followLink`, which waits for the rest of that page (next bullet but one); `isNotFoundPage` and `isPermanentRedirect` accept the streamed forms of "not found" and of a redirect (next bullet). The development runs stay: React reports a mismatch of attributes with the server's HTML only in development, and the suite has caught real ones so (the Logo group, 2026-11-19).
- **A missing page's and a moved address's status codes depend on the host.** The website's pages have one route (`src/app/[...slug]/page.tsx`), which renders an address it does not know when it is first asked for. On Vercel, and from the development server, a missing page then answers 404, and an old address 308 with its `Location`, at once (the hosted preview, 2026-11-20). A server that Next.js runs itself (`next start`, a container, the tests' production build) sends the route's shell first, with a 200, and streams the "not found" (marked `noindex`) or the redirect after it, which Next.js's router follows in the browser (Next.js's guide, "Status codes"). After that a missing page answers 404 from the cache, and an old address 200 again, or from the cache a 308 with no `Location` (Next.js 16.4.6, 2026-11-20, `next start` and the tests' server alike). So search engines get the old site's addresses as proper redirects only from a host like Vercel: something to weigh when the hosting is chosen.
- **In WebKit, leaving a page while the rest of it is still coming in makes the framework load that page again in full**, over the page being opened. A production build shows a page opened by a link as soon as its first part has come, and changes the address at once. When the page is then left by other means than the site's own links (in the tests, `page.goto`), WebKit cuts the rest off ("Load failed"), and Next.js's router answers with "Failed to fetch RSC payload … Falling back to browser navigation", a full load of the page it was fetching. Chromium says nothing then. The tests wait for the rest (`followLink`, 2026-11-20); a person in Safari would have to leave by a bookmark or a typed address in that instant.
- **Earlier pages stay in the document, hidden.** With Cache Components, the router keeps recently visited pages mounted (`display: none`) so their state survives going back. Client state therefore outlives navigation: reset it deliberately where that matters. Signing out is for the same reason a plain form post to `/account/sign-out` and not a server action: the browser loads a new document, so nothing of the pages a signed-in person had open stays behind. In browser tests use role locators, or `byLabel`, `byText` and `onScreen` from `e2e/helpers.ts`; a plain text or label locator also matches the hidden copies.
- **After a route is deleted, remove its generated types.** `web/.next/dev/types` and `web/.next-e2e/dev/types` still name the deleted page, and `tsc` fails on them. With no server running, delete those two folders; `npm run typecheck` writes the types again.
- **One build folder, one process.** A dev server started on a build folder that another process is still writing to corrupts the cache, and every page then reloads in a loop (about 30 times a second, with nothing in the log but "Compiled"). It happened twice: with two sessions active, and when a server was started within a second of killing the previous one. Wait for the old process to exit before starting a new one. To recover, stop the servers and delete `web/.next/dev`. It happened to the tester too (2026-11-17), twice, on a build folder an earlier server had left, after that server had exited: delete the folder before starting a server on it by hand. The browser tests avoid it by building into their own folder, `.next-e2e` (`NEXT_DIST_DIR`), which they delete at every start.
- **More than one coding tool works in this checkout** (Claude Code and Codex, since 2026-11-17). `AGENTS.md` has the rules for that. Before committing, run `git status` and `git log -5`, and commit only what you changed and verified.
- **A test customer on the hosted preview gets Resend's test address.** The preview sends real email to any address (since 2026-11-20), now and later: a confirmation at once, a reminder before the visit, a review request after it, a free hour when one is earned. `delivered+<name>@resend.dev` takes an email and delivers it to nobody, and each name is a different customer to the site ([Resend's guide](https://resend.com/docs/dashboard/emails/send-test-emails)). An address at the studio's domain is a real inbox, `hello+<name>@` included. A made-up address bounces, which counts against the domain. A sign-in code sent to a test address is read from `email_outbox` (its subject holds the code). The contact form and a client's request to move a booking email the first address of `OWNER_EMAILS` (`OWNER_NOTIFY_EMAIL` when set).
- **Vercel's firewall stands in front of the hosted preview.** On 2026-11-21 a probe of about 45 plain requests in under a minute, `/.env`, `/.git/config` and a source map's address among them, made Vercel answer everything from the builder's Mac with 403 and its "Security Checkpoint" page (`X-Vercel-Mitigated: challenge`) for about ten minutes. Headless Chromium and WebKit did not get through either: the checkpoint's own request answered 708. It lifted by itself, and nothing was set in Vercel. So: send odd requests from a browser page of the site (`page.evaluate(fetch)`), a few seconds apart, as `fuzz.mjs` of that day's test did, and give attack-like text (a script, an SQL quote, a second address after a line break) to the code off line: `bookingInput.safeParse` needs no database. The owner's own browser is on the same network as the builder's Mac.
- **Vercel reports a deployment as the commit's status, not as a GitHub deployment.** After a merge, `gh api 'repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/<sha>/status'` (run in the checkout, `gh` fills in the two names) shows the context `Vercel` going to `success` ("Deployment has completed"), about a minute later. The repository's deployments list (`repos/{owner}/{repo}/deployments`) is empty, so a script that waits on it waits in vain, as one did after PR #25 (2026-11-21). Read the commit through GitHub (`repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/<sha>`) rather than through a local checkout, which may not have fetched the merge commit GitHub made.
- **A confirmed booking may have a cancellation or move waiting** (`bookings.endRequest`, since 2026-11-21): the client asked while Square could not say whether the booking's payment link was paid ("Architecture", `pay-links.ts`). Code that ends a booking, changes its time, emails the client about the visit, or takes money for it looks at it: the reminders, the completion of visits, the capture of reserved amounts and the studio's move skip such a booking, and a cancellation clears it. A new path of that kind does the same.
- **A browser test that saves twice and then reads a public page waits between the saves** (`requestsSettled` in `e2e/helpers.ts`). A production build prefetches the pages a screen links to each time it is drawn again. A second save pressed a tenth of a second after the first lands while the public pages are being drawn for those prefetches, and one of them can be kept with the values from before it (Known gaps). `webkit-production` failed twice on 2026-11-21 that way, on the test of an add-on's price and a studio's guest number, in the three runs after a change to the booking pages' metadata, and in no run before it.
- **A script kept outside `web/` cannot import a package by its name.** Node looks for it from the script's own folder. Import the site's modules by their full path, and a package the same way (`…/web/node_modules/drizzle-orm/index.js`), or get it with `createRequire("…/web/")`. In drizzle's `sql` template a backslash is lost (`\+`, `\1`): pass a pattern as a parameter.
- **A button whose `formAction` is a server action cannot have a `name`.** React overrides it and logs an error, which fails the browser tests. Give each button its own action (see the two add-on actions in `src/app/admin/actions.ts`).
- **A `<details>` that can be clicked while the page loads carries `suppressHydrationWarning`.** A click on its title before React takes the page over is the browser's own and opens it; React then reports that `open` differs from what the server sent, and the browser tests fail on that message. The editors' `Group` (`src/components/site-editor/parts.tsx`) has it, since CI caught the header editor's Logo group so on 2026-11-19.
- **A picture's `quality` is one of `images.qualities` in `next.config.ts` (75 or 85).** Next.js 16 serves any other value as the closest one in the list, the lower on a tie (80 is served as 75), and warns about it in development and in the tests. The title band asked for 80 until 2026-11-19, and was served 75 all along.
- **One stylesheet.** `src/app/globals.css` was carried over from the Flask prototype. Its first `:root` block is the design: the same values as `DEFAULT_DESIGN`, which a test compares, so change both together.
- **Text styles are for the public pages only.** A public page wraps its content in `.site-page`; inside it running text and headings follow the design's sizes, weights and spacing. The booking steps, the account and the admin are outside it and keep the sizes they were tested with; they follow the design's colours, fonts and buttons only. A heading's size on a public page is the size its place gives it times the design's scale, so a rule (or an inline style) for such a heading sets `--h1-base`, `--h2-base`, `--h3-base` or `--h4-base`, never `font-size`.
- **Text on the accent colour is `var(--on-accent)`,** never `#fff`: the owners can make the accent light.
- **The header folds its menu by itself.** At 1000px and less the stylesheet does it; on a wider screen `SiteNav` measures whether the logo and the links fit and sets `data-folded`. The folded rules exist twice in `globals.css` for those two cases (and twice more under "Phones"): change them together. A link, a button or the logo made a flex box for a finger (the touch rules) gets `gap: .3em`: a flex box drops the space between its words and a part of them in an element ("Info" and its arrow, the logo's two parts).
- **A draft-mode preview needs HTTPS in production.** Next.js sets its cookie `Secure` there. On `localhost` in development it works over HTTP.
- **Never change an element of a page from outside React,** a page shown in a frame included: do it before the page has started and React reports a mismatch with the server's HTML, which fails the browser tests. To restyle a framed page, give its document a stylesheet of its own (`adoptedStyleSheets`, see `overlayCss` in `parts.tsx`).
- **Forms do not nest.** The page editor's panel is Puck's own form, and the fields the site adds to it (links, pictures, the picture chooser) are inside it: they use no `<form>`, and every button in them is `type="button"`.
- **A field's `pattern` is read with the `v` flag** by today's browsers: inside `[...]`, escape `/` and `-` (`[A-Za-z0-9\/\-]`). Otherwise the browser logs an error, which fails the browser tests.
- **The page editor (Puck), as its code shows it** (its documentation leaves these out): it cleans nothing it is given, which is why the site has its own cleaner and its own renderer for visitors. At run time it injects a stylesheet that loads a font from another server unless its CSS is already on the page; `no-external.css`, imported by `PageEditor`, is that CSS without the font. `<Puck>` reads `data` only when it mounts, so the editor gets a new `key` after a publish, a discard or a version brought back. Its canvas is a frame that takes the admin page's stylesheets (`syncHostStyles`) but not the classes on `<html>`, so `PageEditor` gives the frame the font classes. A custom field's `render` gets `{ field, name, id, value, onChange, readOnly }`; a slot is drawn as `<Content className="…" />`. Check these again before upgrading it.
- **Queries that do not depend on each other, in a function that may run on a transaction, go through `together` (src/db/client.ts), not `Promise.all`.** A transaction has one connection: node-postgres queues queries given to it at once, with a warning that its next major version refuses them, and PGlite says nothing. `together` runs them at once on the database and one after another on a transaction (`isTransaction`). The catalog, the money of bookings, the booking views, the page's data, the uses of a picture and the clear of test mode go through it.
- **A file goes to a route handler, never to a server action.** A server action refuses a request over 1 MB. The two upload addresses check who may upload before reading anything (`mayUseMediaLibrary`, `mayEditFonts`), and read the body with a limit, a part at a time (`readFormData` in src/server/upload.ts): a client can leave out the length (a chunked body), which a check of the header alone lets through. They answer in JSON (pictures, sent by the uploader one at a time) or by sending the browser back with a message (fonts, a plain form). In a route handler cached pages are refreshed with `revalidateTag(tag, { expire: 0 })`: `updateTag` works in server actions only.
- **A control that only the page's code in the browser can work is disabled until the page has started** (`useStarted`, `src/components/use-started.ts`). A file chosen in the media library's "Add pictures" or "Replace the file" before then was lost without a word. The design and the header editors' fields wait too (their form is a `<fieldset disabled>` until then): what a second window typed at once never reached the editor. A browser test waits for such a control to be enabled before it uses it.
- **The test world bills memberships by hand.** Its processor's subscriptions bill nothing by themselves: a test calls `world.billMonth(subscriptionId, paid)` for each month, and sets `world.failMembership` to make one step refuse. Square's sandbox stops a subscription whose customer has an `example.com` address, so `npm run check:square` uses one on the studio's own domain (the sandbox sends no email).
- **An email to a client is written from its template** (`src/lib/email-templates.ts`). To change the site's own wording, change the template there: the owners' saved wording, if any, is not touched. A new fact in an email is a new part, added to the template's `parts` and given by its builder (a test checks that every builder gives exactly its template's parts). Making a part `required`, or taking one away, makes any saved wording of the owners that lacks it, or names it, unusable: the site's wording is sent instead and Admin → Emails says so, until they put it right. A new email to a client gets a template; a notice to the studio does not.
- **A `page.tsx` may export only what Next.js expects of a page** (the page, `metadata`, `generateMetadata`, `generateStaticParams`). A helper that another file needs goes in a module of its own, or the build fails.
- **`sharp` is a native module.** It is installed for the machine it runs on; `npm install` on the host fetches the right one.
- **A write a page makes by itself goes as a beacon, not a fetch or a server action.** WebKit reports a request still on its way when the page is left as an error of the page ("Fetch API cannot load … due to access control checks"), and the browser tests fail on it, rightly, except for the framework's own prefetches (`cutOffPageData`). The access log's line went by server action at first and failed CI's WebKit job twice (2026-11-21). `navigator.sendBeacon` is made for this: it outlives the page and reports nothing. Its route must refuse another site's post (`Sec-Fetch-Site`), since a server action's own origin check does not cover it.
- **The keyboard's way through a page.** Every page's first stop is the link that skips the menu (`layout.tsx`; drawn only while it has the focus), which gives the focus to `main#main` (tabindex -1, no ring), so the next Tab goes on inside the page. On the booking screen the times are one stop (`moveAmongTimes` in `BookingFlow.tsx`): Tab reaches the time last moved to, else the chosen one, else the first; the arrow keys, Home and End move among them, and Enter or Space chooses. WebKit's Tab passes links by, as Safari's does unless its setting asks otherwise, so a browser test that needs a link focused in WebKit focuses it.
- **A browser-test run started in the background** must end with the test runner's own exit status. `npx playwright test > log; echo done` reports success whatever the tests did: read the log's last lines before saying a run passed.
- Next.js telemetry was turned off on this machine with `npx next telemetry disable`.

## Agents

`.claude/agents/tester.md` defines a second agent, the **tester**. It runs the test suites, uses the site in a real browser as a customer, a staff member and the owner, and reports defects with steps to reproduce. It does not change application code, commit or push: fixing what it finds is the builder's job.

- Ask for it by name ("use the tester agent to test the contact form") or with `@agent-tester`. `claude --agent tester` runs a whole session as the tester.
- It works on port 3213 with its own database, build and results folders (see "Browser tests" above), so it can run while a dev server or the usual browser tests are running.
- Claude Code picks up a changed agent file within seconds. A session that started before `.claude/agents/` existed has to be restarted once to see it.
- Agent definitions are in git. The rest of `.claude/` is personal and ignored.

## Rebuild decisions

The record of what was decided for the rebuild, what was built, and what still needs an answer.

Record each outcome here, with the date, when it is decided. Change a line's status from **Open** to **Decided** and write the outcome, or to **Superseded** and name the replacement. Do not leave outcomes only in chat.

Live reference: the public site is Squarespace, with Acuity Scheduling for bookings. Acuity's full price list and policy text are public at `https://app.acuityscheduling.com/schedule.php?owner=28681884`.

### Questions for the owners (list of 2026-11-17)

This numbered list tracks the questions left open from 2026-11-16 and the questions raised on 2026-11-17. Completed items keep their numbers for cross-references. Record decisions and implementation status under the relevant subject and in the decision log.

**To go live** (nothing can launch without these):

1. **Hosting.** (was 1) Where the site runs. It also decides the database, where pictures are stored, and what runs the scheduled task (free hours, refund retries, payments not taken yet). The builder's suggestion: Vercel for the site, a managed PostgreSQL (for example Neon), and Vercel's scheduler. **Answered 2026-11-18:** Vercel and Neon are acceptable; the hosted preview runs there, with Vercel's scheduler.
2. **Web address.** (was 2) Which domain the new site takes over from Squarespace, and who manages its DNS. **Answered in part:** the existing domain stays (2026-11-18). Its DNS is served by Squarespace (found 2026-11-20), where the owner adds records; who restores DNS in a fallback goes in the runbook.
3. **Email.** (was 3) Which service sends the site's email (confirmations, sign-in codes, notices). Resend is built in; it needs an address on the studio's domain to send from, and three DNS records. Until then nothing can be emailed in production, sign-in codes included. **Answered:** Resend, on the hosted preview since 2026-11-19; the domain waits for its three DNS records (`HANDOFF.md`, "Now" 1).
4. **Real card payments.** (was 4) When to switch Square from test mode to the live keys. The keys are typed into the host's settings by the owner, never sent in a message. The builder's suggestion: one real booking on the live site, refunded, before it opens. Planned as the last step of the cutover (`HANDOFF.md`, step 10), on the owner's word then.
5. **Privacy policy.** (was 6) Answered 2026-11-20. Draft the policy for lawyer review before publication. **Drafted 2026-11-21**, a private doc for the owners (the decision log).
6. **Sales tax.** (was 7) Answered in part 2026-11-20. Prices exclude tax. Show it separately at checkout and on receipts. The 6.5% rate is provisional until the accountant confirms the rate and treatment.

**Raised by the answers of 2026-11-17**

7. **Weekend prices of longer bookings — answered and built.** See follow-up 1 under "Katya's answers to the 16 follow-up questions".
8. **Extra people — answered and built.** See follow-up 2 under "Katya's answers to the 16 follow-up questions", and question 37 below (confirmed 2026-11-18).
9. **Memberships — answered 2026-11-17 and 2026-11-18.** See follow-up 3 under "Katya's answers to the 16 follow-up questions". Four points remain: questions 41 to 44 below.
10. **Unused pet fees — answered and built.** See follow-up 4 under "Katya's answers to the 16 follow-up questions".
11. **A booking the studio moves or changes — answered 2026-11-17, built 2026-11-18.** See follow-up 5 under "Katya's answers to the 16 follow-up questions", and question 47 below for how it was built.
12. **"One reschedule is complimentary".** Follow-up 6 set $20 for later moves. The 2026-11-20 answer limits customers to one self-service move. See "Owner decisions of 2026-11-20" for the remaining fee question.
13. **The review request after a visit — answered and built.** See follow-up 7 under "Katya's answers to the 16 follow-up questions".
14. **Blanche and Dayglow together — answered and built.** See follow-up 8 under "Katya's answers to the 16 follow-up questions".
15. **Loyalty starting over — answered and built.** See follow-up 9 under "Katya's answers to the 16 follow-up questions".
16. **The photographer's day — answered and verified as built.** See follow-up 10 under "Katya's answers to the 16 follow-up questions".
17. **Two weeks for photography — answered and verified as built.** See follow-up 11 under "Katya's answers to the 16 follow-up questions".
18. **Photography's codes — answered and built.** See follow-up 12 under "Katya's answers to the 16 follow-up questions".
19. **Extra charges — answered and built.** See follow-up 13 under "Katya's answers to the 16 follow-up questions", and questions 36 and 48 below (confirmed 2026-11-18).
20. **The Instagram feed — answered (follow-up 14): the owners connect it once the site is on its host.** An owner connects it once by signing in to Instagram; it has to be a professional account (business or creator).
21. **After hours, by request — answered and built.** See follow-up 15 under "Katya's answers to the 16 follow-up questions".

**Raised by the membership answers of 2026-11-18** (follow-up 3):

41. **Member hours at weekends.** The answer of 2026-11-17 said that at weekends the minutes cover the weekday price of the time they pay for, and the member pays the difference to the weekend price. The answer of 2026-11-18 says members use their hours however they like, weekends included. Does a weekend booking still pay that difference, or do the minutes cover weekend time in full? Built (2026-11-18) as a switch the owners set themselves: Settings → Memberships, "At weekends, members pay the difference to the weekend price", on, as decided on 2026-11-17 (`memberWeekendDifference`). Two hours in Paris on a Saturday are $144 and on a weekday $136: a member pays $8 with it on, nothing with it off. **Answered 2026-11-18:** "membership only looks into the time balance, not the price": the minutes cover weekend time in full, so the switch is off: its default is off, and migration `0047_member_weekend_full` switches it off where the settings were saved (built 2026-11-18). The owners can still switch it on under Settings → Memberships.
42. **What the rest of a longer booking costs.** A member with 60 minutes left books 2 hours: the 60 minutes are used, and they pay for the other hour. In Paris on a weekday both readings give $68, since 2 hours cost twice one hour. They differ on longer bookings, which cost less by the hour: 4 hours in Paris are $245, and with 60 minutes left the member would pay either the share of the booking the minutes do not cover (three quarters, $183.75), or the price of the 3 hours booked on their own ($195). The builder suggests the share: it works for any number of minutes left, while only some lengths have a price (1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 5, 8 and 10 hours; 150 minutes left on a 3-hour booking would leave 30 minutes, which has none). A free hour works the other way today, as built and confirmed: the one-hour price comes off (4 hours in Paris with a free hour: $245 less $68, $177), and the share only for a length with no price. Built (2026-11-18) with the share. Agreed, or should memberships follow the free hour?
43. **Moving a member's booking.** Online, a client moves a booking by releasing it for a reschedule code worth what they paid. For a booking paid with minutes the builder suggests the minutes go back to the balance instead, as when it is cancelled, and a part paid by card becomes a code as today. Moves are allowed until 24 hours before the start, and a cancellation gives minutes back only from 48 hours: should a move 24 to 48 hours ahead give the minutes back? Built (2026-11-18) more simply: a booking paid with minutes is not moved online. Its page says to cancel it, which gives its minutes back from 48 hours ahead, and book the new time, or to ask the studio to move it, which keeps everything as it is. So a move can never be a way round the 48 hours. Agreed?
44. **Blanche's membership prices.** The owners' table of 2026-11-17 says $380 and $700 a month for 5 and 10 hours in Blanche; the live site sells them at $370 and $670. The new site starts with the table's prices, which the owners can change under Admin → Memberships. Are $380 and $700 meant?
45. **Buying a membership, as built.** (new, 2026-11-18, follow-up 3, step 2) Built on these readings. No account is needed to buy one: the client gives their details as for a booking, and the membership belongs to that email address, which their account shows when they sign in with it. A client may hold more than one membership. The first month is charged at once and the hours come with it; Square bills the next on the same day of the next month (the 31st becomes the month's last day in a shorter month). The studio is not emailed about each new member: the admin will list them (step 5). The Memberships page is not put into the site's menu, which is the owners' (Website → Header, menu & footer): the old address leads to it, and they can add it. Its text is the owners' to change, as with any page; it does not yet say what weekends cost (question 41). Should any of these be otherwise?
46. **Each month of a membership, as built.** (new, 2026-11-18, follow-up 3, step 4) Built on these readings. The site learns of each month from Square, not when it happens but within the hour after the scheduled task next runs, and only then adds the month's hours: a member whose payment is late has no new hours until it is paid. The member is emailed each month paid ("A month paid", a template the owners can change or empty of all but its code). A month Square has not taken by the day after it was due, or monthly payments Square stopped, are told to the member and to the studio once, and the member keeps the hours already on the code; the site does not try the card again itself, which is Square's to do. A membership cancelled in Square's own dashboard is cancelled on the site too, and the member told. Should any of these be otherwise?

**Rules built on the builder's reading** (an "OK" settles each):

22. **The website editor.** (was 17) The design, the menu and the pages wait for Publish; prices, hours and booking rules apply at once. A deleted page stays in the trash 30 days. Videos come only from YouTube and Vimeo. A "seasonal collection" is a named set of pictures with a first and last day. **Confirmed 2026-11-18** (`HANDOFF.md`, "Earlier decisions"), with Instagram as a third source of videos, which is not built: the video block takes YouTube and Vimeo only (`src/lib/site/video.ts`). The seasonal collections of 2026-11-20 keep their optional dates.
35. **How the $20 for a further reschedule is collected — confirmed 2026-11-17: taken off the reschedule code.** (new, 2026-11-17) Built as a deduction from the reschedule credit: a $176 booking moved a second time gives a $156 code, so the client pays the $20 by card when booking the new time. Nothing is charged at the moment of the move, which needs no card step. Or should the card be charged $20 when the client presses Reschedule? **Superseded in part 2026-11-20** ("Owner decisions of 2026-11-20"): a client moves a booking online once, and later moves are the studio's, at $20 each (to build).
36. **Overtime and extra charges, how they are worked out — confirmed 2026-11-18.** (new, 2026-11-18, follow-up 13) Built on these readings. The hourly rate is the one-hour price of each studio of the booking for its day (weekday or weekend, a special date as the owners set it), added together for a booking of several studios, without the bundle discount; overtime is a quarter of it for each 15 minutes started, rounded to the cent ($88 an hour: 20 minutes is $44). Overtime is for studio rentals, not photography sessions. A studio with no one-hour price for the day cannot get overtime: the page says so, and the amount can be added as a cleaning or damage charge. A charge is added only once the appointment has started, so it never travels into a reschedule code or a cancellation refund. Which payment paid a charge, taking back a paid one, and a booking with charges not being moved: question 48 — **confirmed 2026-11-18**. Staff who may record payments add charges on their days; only an owner takes one back, and only while it is unpaid and no payment link is open. Cleaning and damage need a note, which the client sees on the receipt, and are at most $10,000. The client is not emailed when a charge is added: they are when it is paid, or when a payment link is sent. The owner said yes to all of it on 2026-11-18.
37. **Extra guests on studios booked together — confirmed 2026-11-18.** (new, 2026-11-18, follow-up 2) The owner's rule is that every guest counts toward the limit for studios together (10 for two, 20 for three). Built literally: the extra guests a bundle can take are what the limit leaves above the guests its studios include, never more than their own extra guests added together. With today's numbers, Paris and Dayglow (or Paris and Blanche) include 4 + 6 = 10, so a two-studio bundle takes no extra guests, while one Blanche alone takes up to 12 people; all three include 16, so 4 extra. The booking screen says so ("No extra guests with these studios together: 10 guests at most in all, and their prices include that many"). Is that what the owners mean, or should a two-studio bundle take more (raise the limit under Settings, "Most guests for two studios together")? The owner said yes to it as built on 2026-11-18 (the reply read "38,38,39 ok", taken as 37, 38 and 39, the three then waiting); the limit stays a setting the owners can raise.
38. **When the reminder before a visit is sent — confirmed 2026-11-18.** (new, 2026-11-18, question 29 of 2026-11-17) Built so: once per booking, `reminderHours` (24) before the start, or as soon after as the scheduled task runs, but never once the visit has started. None for a booking made closer to its start than that (its confirmation was just sent), none for a cancelled one or one waiting to be moved. When the studio moves a booking, a reminder comes again before the new time; moved to a time closer than 24 hours, the email about the move stands for it. A client's own move makes a new booking, which follows the same rules. Should any of these be otherwise? The owner said yes to it as built on 2026-11-18.
39. **Asking the studio to move a booking — confirmed 2026-11-18.** (new, 2026-11-18, question 20 of 2026-11-17) Built so: offered from the booking's page and the account until the visit starts, whatever the booking's terms say about moving online, because the studio decides; the client says when would suit them (required, at most 1,000 characters); one request at a time (while the booking is Reschedule Pending, the page says it was asked); the client cannot withdraw it online, and the studio clears it by moving the booking or setting it back to Confirmed. A booking waiting for its move gets no reminder and is not marked completed by the scheduled task. Should any of these be otherwise? The owner said yes to it as built on 2026-11-18.
40. **The emails' templates.** (new, 2026-11-18, question 33 of 2026-11-17) Built on these readings. "Create" is read as the owners making their own version of each email the site sends; an email sent at a moment the site does not send one now (a birthday, say) would need that moment built, and is not offered. The 18 emails to clients can be changed; the nine notices the site sends the studio (a refund that failed, a payment link to check, a client's request to move, a message from the contact page…) keep the site's wording, as they say what the site did and what to do next, and are listed on the page. The site still writes the parts that depend on the case (the money lines, the refund sentences, the codes and how to use them, the photography line), and the owners write everything around them and choose which parts an email has, except those it cannot do without: the sign-in code, the code an email hands out, the confirmation's link to the booking, the payment link, and every part that states money or a refund. Only owners change the emails, as with prices and settings: no switch gives it to managers. A change reaches the emails written after it is saved, not those already waiting to be sent. Going back means the site's wording; earlier versions of the owners' wording are not kept. The emails stay plain text, as today. There is no test send, as no email service is set up; the preview shows the email with made-up details. Should any of these be otherwise? **Answered 2026-11-18:** "for the automated process we don't need to customize anything, however the owner should have an option to customize every email": the site's wording stays the default, and every email becomes changeable, the notices to the studio included. **Built 2026-11-18:** the twelve notices to the studio are templates too, under "Notices to the studio" in Admin → Emails. Each keeps the parts it cannot do without (the booking's reference, the amounts, what happened and what to do, the client's message), which the site writes as before; the rest is the owners'. They were rewritten as templates without changing a word, checked against the builders as they were over 29 cases covering every branch.
47. **Repricing a booking the studio moves, and the appointment's payment card, as built — confirmed 2026-11-18.** (new, 2026-11-18, follow-up 5) Built on these readings. A booking's price depends on its day only through the kind of day, a weekday or a weekend (a special date counts as the kind the owners set), so a move to a day of the same kind keeps the price as it is, even when the owners have changed the prices since it was booked; a move to a day of the other kind prices it for that day at today's prices, as the booking screen would (its studios and the bundle discount), while its add-ons and extra guests keep the prices they were booked at. What paid for part of it pays the same part again: a percent code its percentage of the new price, a free hour the new day's one-hour price, membership minutes the same minutes at the new day's price (with the weekend difference while that switch is on), and credit (a reschedule code or studio credit) what it paid, up to the new price; credit the new price no longer needs goes back on its code, as the studio's cancellation gives it back, and none is taken from the code when the price goes up: the rest is owed. A photography session is never repriced, its package costing the same any day. A day with no price for the booking's length cannot be moved to: the page says which price to add. The price changes whoever asked for the move. More to pay is owed and collected as today, at the studio or with a payment link; when it costs less than was paid, the difference is the client's "Remaining credit", which nothing refunds by itself: the studio refunds it to the card from the appointment's page when it agrees, or gives it back at the studio and records that against the payment. The client's email about the move gives the new total and what follows (still to pay; "we will refund it to you"; the credit back on their code), and their booking page shows "To be refunded". Added after the tester's pass (2026-11-18): a refund made while the booking holds remaining credit gives that back first, and should the price go up again that part is owed again; the rest of a refund is the studio's goodwill and never makes a booking owe. A booking keeps the kind of day it is priced as, so a special date added afterwards changes nothing for it. The appointment's payment card shows Paid (after refunds), Outstanding balance and Remaining credit. Each card payment taken through Square links to Square's receipt of it: Square documents no address of one payment in its Dashboard, so its receipt is what opens, asked of Square when the link is followed, and it shows the receipt number by which Square's Dashboard (Transactions) finds the payment; a payment of test mode has none. Should any of these be otherwise? The owner said OK to it as built on 2026-11-18.
48. **A booking with extra charges: which payment paid what, and no move.** (new, 2026-11-18, after the tester's third and fourth passes; under question 36) Built on these readings. The site does not record what each payment was for, so it reads what came in as paying the booking's price first, then each charge in the order it was added: the way what a booking owes reads its money, so the payment link's email, the payment card and taking back a charge always agree with the amount owed (`chargesPaidOf` in `src/server/money.ts`). A client who pays for a charge while part of the price is still unpaid is read as paying the price; what the booking owes is the same either way. A charge is taken back only while nothing of it is paid in that reading. A paid one is given back as a refund instead, which undoes it: it stays on the client's receipt beside the refund, owed no more, and an owner who then tries to take it back is told it needs nothing more. The site does not refuse a second refund of the same money: it cannot tell what a refund was for, and a refund beyond what the booking was paid for is the studio's gift, which never makes a booking owe (question 47). A booking with a charge that was not taken back is not moved, by the studio or the client (a client cannot move a visit that has started anyway): the charge shows its visit has taken place, at least in part, and moved to a day of another price, one booking would hold what paid the visit, its charges and the new day's price, which no reading keeps apart. The move card says so, and the rest of a shoot is booked as a new booking (Calendar, New booking); a charge added by mistake is taken back first. Before this, the third pass's reading went payment by payment, in the order they came, a payment made after a charge paying that charge first; the fourth pass found it disagreeing with what the booking owed (a link for the price paid after a charge was added was read as paying the charge) and refunding the wrong amounts after a studio's move.
49. **Acuity's appointments still to come, as imported — confirmed 2026-11-20.** (new, 2026-11-19, step 5 of the plan in `HANDOFF.md`) Built on these readings, for the owners to confirm or change (see "Importing Acuity's appointments still to come" under Architecture):
    - (a) **Price.** An imported appointment keeps the price the client agreed to in Acuity, even where the site's prices differ: a weekend, which Acuity priced as a weekday, or the old extra-people add-ons. A bundle's price is shown shared between its studios as the site's own prices share theirs.
    - (b) **Payments.** What was paid in Acuity counts as paid: what was paid online, and, when Acuity marks the appointment paid, the rest, whether a package or gift certificate code or money the studio received paid it. The site cannot refund it: a refund is made in Square or Acuity and recorded here by hand. The rest is paid at the studio, as for a booking made in the admin.
    - (c) **Terms.** The site's terms on the day of the import (refund window and fee, moves) apply to the booking from then on; the client has not accepted them on the site. Should Acuity's be kept instead?
    - (d) **Emails.** Nothing is sent at the import: the client has Acuity's confirmation. The site sends no reminder either, because Acuity keeps the appointments until the rollback period ends and sends its own; `--remind` makes the site send its own reminder too. The review request after the visit and the visit's loyalty stamp follow the site's rules, as for any booking. Should either wait until Acuity is closed?
    - (e) **Holding the time.** An appointment holds its studios at its time even outside the site's opening hours or the photographer's working hours, since Acuity booked it. Only an overlap refuses it, the site's 15-minute clean-up gap included, where Acuity keeps 7 to 20. Two Acuity appointments 7 minutes apart in one studio cannot both be imported: the report lists the second for the owners.
    - (f) **Add-ons.** Acuity's add-ons are inside its price, not listed separately. An add-on of time (+30 minutes, +1 hour) is inside the appointment's end time.
    - (g) **Codes.** A package, gift certificate or subscription code used in Acuity is named in the payment's note. What is left on it stays in Acuity: carrying members over is next step 6.
    - **Confirmed 2026-11-20:** the owner said yes to all of them as built, (c) and (d) included: the site's terms apply rather than Acuity's, and the review request and the loyalty stamp do not wait for Acuity to close.
50. **An Acuity appointment with more than one email address.** (new, 2026-11-20, from the owners' real export, where 7 of 189 rows had one) Acuity's Email field holds the client's own address, then any others the client asked Acuity to copy its emails to, separated by commas.
