Built so: the booking belongs to the first address, which finds it in the client's account and gets the site's emails about it (the review request after the visit, and the reminder with `--remind`); the booking's history names the others, which the site never emails. Before, the import refused such an appointment as having no valid address.
51. **Past visits and loyalty stamps.** (new, 2026-11-20, step 7: "Importing Acuity's history" under Architecture) Built so: a past visit brought in from Acuity's history counts for no stamp, so no client earns a free hour, or is emailed one, for visits made before this site; whatever stamps clients hold from those visits today stay where they are. Should they count instead, all of them or those since a date? Counting them would send a free hour, at their next visit, to every client with five past visit days.
52. **Past visits and their money.** (new, 2026-11-20, step 7) Built so: a past visit keeps Acuity's price, and what Acuity records as paid is recorded as paid through Acuity, on the client's receipts, as for the appointments still to come (question 49 (b)). A past visit never shows money owed: one Acuity marks unpaid (43 in the owners' export) is set aside for a person, and what was paid online beyond a visit's price (63 visits, after a move in Acuity, a code, or one payment for two appointments) is not recorded as paid, only named in its history. Refunds made in Square are not in Acuity's export: they would come with Square's payment history, not exported yet. Should the unpaid ones come in as paid at the studio, or stay out?
53. **Which past visits come in.** (new, 2026-11-20, step 7) Built so: every appointment of the export that is over and not cancelled in Acuity, as far back as the export goes (the owners' starts on 2023-03-10). A no-show where Acuity's label says No-show; the rest completed, whatever else the label says (Completed, Checked In, Confirmed, Reschedule Done, or none). Set aside: one marked Reschedule Pending (did it take place?), one with no email address (no account would show it), and the workshops, content days and setup previews, whose types name no studio. Should any of these be otherwise?
54. **A cancellation or move that waits for Square — confirmed 2026-11-21.** (new, 2026-11-21, building question 23 below) The owner said OK to it as built on 2026-11-21. Built so:
    - (a) **The terms as they stood when the client asked.** What waits is decided by the booking's saved terms at the moment the client asked, not when Square answers: a refund deadline that passes while it waits costs the client nothing. The booking keeps its time until then, past the visit if need be.
    - (b) **The client waits; the studio is refused.** The client's cancellation and their release for a reschedule code wait. The studio's own cancel is refused instead, with what to do in Square: the owner is the one who reviews. A goodwill refund asks Square first, so that a payment just made counts in what can be refunded, but is not held when Square cannot answer: it can only give back money the site has recorded.
    - (c) **While it waits.** No reminder, no automatic completion of the visit, no capture of an amount reserved on a card, no move by the studio, and nothing to pay on the link's page. The client cannot ask for the other change on top. The booking's page and the account say what waits, with "Try again now".
    - (d) **What the client is told.** On screen, and on the booking's page afterwards; no email of its own when it starts to wait. The cancellation's email, or the code's, follows when it is done, and says when they asked and why it waited.
    - (e) **How it is finished.** By the scheduled task, by an owner's "Check whether it was paid" or "Close it by hand", or by the client's "Try again now". "Close it by hand" means the owner found the link unpaid in Square. A payment the site can never see (made through another processor's link, for example) is not counted when the owner closes the link: the owners' notice tells them to give back what the booking's terms allow of it where it was paid.
    - (f) **A payment after the end.** A link paid after its booking was cancelled (Square said it was unpaid, but the link could not be withdrawn in time) is still refunded in full: that booking had already ended.
    - (g) **A booking that ended some other way meanwhile** (an owner marked it completed or missed): what waited is dropped, and its history says why.
55. **The email of a stamp earned — confirmed 2026-11-21.** (new, 2026-11-21, building the owners' loyalty decision of 2026-11-20) The owner said OK to it as built on 2026-11-21. Built so:
    - (a) **What it says:** "Your visit earned a stamp: you're at 3 of 5 stamps toward a free studio hour." The count is the stamps toward the next free hour, the owners' "Every 5 visits" from Settings → Loyalty; the subject is "You're at 3 of 5 stamps". The owners change its wording under Admin → Emails ("A stamp earned").
    - (b) **When:** when the scheduled task counts a completed visit that earned its day's stamp (the earning rules as they are: one day once, no photography session, no visit made with a free hour). That is its first run once loyalty's delay after the visit has passed (2 hours as set; on the Hobby plan the task runs once a day). Not on the visit that completes a free hour: the free hour's email says it all. Several visits counted in one run get one email.
    - (c) **Once:** each visit is claimed before anything is sent, in the same transaction, so a retry or a second run sends nothing more.
    - (d) **No switch of its own:** it follows loyalty's. On the hosted preview it reaches the test customers' test addresses, and the owner's own booking of December 4 once that visit is counted.
56. **Five years, of what and from when — confirmed 2026-11-21.** (new, 2026-11-21, building the owner's answer "Let's keep them for 5 years") The owner said OK to it as built on 2026-11-21. Built so:
    - (a) **What:** the three the question named (each email's copy, contact-form messages among them; the sign-in codes; ended sign-ins), and the access log of question 57 with them. Bookings, payments, clients and their history are not deleted: their period is the accountant's to say (the privacy draft's point 3).
    - (b) **From when:** an email from when it was written, sent or not; a code from when it was given; a sign-in from when it ended (30 days after it began, or when signed out); a line of the log from when the page was opened. Five calendar years: November 21, 2026 goes on November 21, 2031.
    - (c) **A setting:** "Keep email copies and sign-in records for (years)", 1 to 20, under Settings → Records, read by the daily task.
57. **The access log of the customers' pages — confirmed 2026-11-21.** (new, 2026-11-21, building question 27's "the site logs who opens a customer's page") The owner said OK to it as built on 2026-11-21, the customer's page and receipts logged and the appointment's page and the calendar not. Built so:
    - (a) **What is logged:** a customer's page shown (Admin → Customers → the customer), and each of their receipts downloaded there, one or a year's. Not the appointment's page, the calendar or the customers list, which show a name and an address too; should they be?
    - (b) **Who reads it:** the owners only, at the bottom of the customer's page: the newest 30 lines, each with when, who (name, level and address as they were) and what. A manager's visits are logged; the manager does not see the log.
    - (c) **Once for a visit:** the same person opening the same thing again within 10 minutes adds no line (a reload, a correction saved, a step back).
    - (d) **When the page counts as opened:** once it is on screen, from the browser. A link only prefetched writes nothing, so the line is written a moment after the page shows, and the page shows the log as it stood before this visit.
    - (e) **Kept** for the retention period, 5 years (question 56).
58. **A client's change of their email address — confirmed 2026-11-21.** (new, 2026-11-21, building question 27's "customers may change their own email address") The owner said OK to it as built on 2026-11-21, a member's address changed by the studio by hand. Built so:
    - (a) **Who:** clients, in their account (Overview, "Your email address"). Not the studio's team: an owner's address comes from the host's settings, and a staff member's from the Staff page.
    - (b) **Proof:** a 6-digit code emailed to the new address, which works once, for 10 minutes, with 5 tries; 3 codes in 15 minutes and 10 a day at most, and the sign-in's limit per network. Nothing changes until the code is given back.
    - (c) **What moves:** the account, every booking, the codes of the client's own, loyalty's corrections, memberships and the access log of the customer's page. The copies of emails already sent and the sign-in codes stay as they were. The customer's page in the admin shows "Earlier address" with when it changed. The old address is emailed that it was changed, with "If you did not make this change, please reply"; the owners are not told.
    - (d) **An address that already holds records** (a booking, a code, a correction, a membership, or an account with a name or phone) is refused: "please contact us: we can join them for you". Joining two addresses is the merge, not built (question 27's third answer, "if the effort is small": its questions are in `HANDOFF.md`). An account the new address only signed in to, holding nothing, gives way.
    - (e) **A membership still running** (being bought, active, or cancelled with paid time left) is refused: Square bills it, and emails its receipts, under the address it was bought with, which the site does not change. "Please contact us": the admin has no tool for it yet, so it is done by hand, in Square and in the database. Once the paid time ends, the client can change it.
    - (f) **Later Acuity imports:** an appointment exported from Acuity under the old address comes in under the old address.
59. **Renaming a studio, and taking one off the site — confirmed 2026-11-21.** (new, 2026-11-21, building question 29) The owner said OK to it as built on 2026-11-21. Built so:
    - (a) **A new name** shows everywhere the site names the studio, past bookings' pages, receipts and the calendar included, since they read the studio's name as it is now. Emails already sent keep the old one. The studio's address on the site (`/book?studio=paris`) stays, and so do the website's pages, whose words are the owners' (Website → Pages). No two studios may share a name.
    - (b) **"Take off the site"** is a removal that keeps everything: the studio is offered nowhere (booking screen, new bookings in the calendar, the website's studio blocks, the prices), its past bookings, receipts and history keep it, and it is put back as it was. It is refused while a booking still to come holds it ("Move or cancel them first"). While off, the calendar does not draw its column, so its past bookings are found through Bookings and the customer's page; and the rule that Blanche and Dayglow are booked together only with Paris stays, so with Paris off they cannot be booked together.
    - (c) **Deleting a studio for good** is not offered: its bookings, receipts and prices refer to it.
60. **Checking a code's balance.** (new, 2026-11-22, building item 5 of the owner's brief of 2026-11-22) Built so, until an "OK":
    - (a) **By the code alone**, as the brief asks, though a code works at checkout only with the email address it was sent to. The answer names no one: no name and no address, only "Works only with the email address it was sent to" for a code tied to one. Site-made codes are eight random characters of 31, and the check allows 20 tries in ten minutes from one address (`limitRate`, the key `code-balance:<address>`).
    - (b) **What each kind shows:** a studio credit and a reschedule code, the money left ("$75 remaining"); a discount, its percentage and the uses left ("20% off, usable once"); loyalty's free hour ("One free hour"); a membership, its minutes ("180 minutes remaining") and its studios. A code used up says "Already used", one past its date "Expired on <day>", and a usable one "Valid until <day>" or "Does not expire". A membership's minutes do not expire. A code the studio cancelled, and a membership still being bought or never paid for, are not found, as at checkout.
    - (c) **What it is for** is the checkout's own rules, so the check cannot promise more: a studio rental or a photography session (`photographyOnly`), a free hour for one hour of one studio with no add-ons, a discount off the rental and not the add-ons, a membership's studios.
    - (d) **Where:** "Check Code Balance", a button above the steps of both booking pages (`/book` and `/book/photography`), which opens a small form. Checking spends nothing.
    - (e) **Gift cards:** the site sells none yet. A studio credit is what one would be, and the form names the kinds the site has.
23. **A payment link Square cannot answer about.** (was 45) Answered 2026-11-20. Verify with Square before cancelling or refunding. An unconfirmed status goes to manual review. A verified payment follows the booking's saved cancellation terms. This supersedes the current automatic full-refund fallback for an unanswered status. Inspect the move path that shares this logic. **Built 2026-11-21:** the client's cancellation and release for a reschedule code (the move path that shared it) wait, the booking keeping its time, flagged to the owners; the studio's cancel is refused until Square can say; what waited is then done under the booking's terms as they stood when the client asked. "Architecture" (`pay-links.ts`) says how, question 54 what was read into it.

**Choices about features** (built as described until answered):

24. **Signing in.** (was 19) Emailed codes only (built). Add passwords? Google sign-in now, later or never? **Answered 2026-11-18:** sign-in starts with emailed codes, and Google sign-in may come later (`HANDOFF.md`, "Earlier decisions").
25. **Earlier history.** (was 21) Import past appointments and payments from Acuity and Square into the customers' accounts, or start from the launch? **Answered 2026-11-18:** import as much of Acuity's and Square's history as practical (`HANDOFF.md`, step 7). Built 2026-11-20 for Acuity's past appointments (`--history`, questions 51 to 53); Square's payment history waits for its export.
26. **Apple Wallet loyalty.** (was 22) Answered 2026-11-20. The built-in program replaces Apple Wallet once the replacement is verified. Add a progress email when a completed visit earns a stamp. **The progress email built 2026-11-21** (question 55).
27. **Customer accounts.** (was 23) May customers change their own email address? Should two accounts of one person be merged? Should the site log who opened a customer's page? **Answered 2026-11-18:** customers may change their own email address, the site logs who opens a customer's page, and two accounts of one person are merged if the effort is small (`HANDOFF.md`, "Earlier decisions"). **Built 2026-11-21:** the access log (question 57) and a client's change of their address (question 58). The merge is not small, and the owner put it off on 2026-11-21: "Merging accounts is not an issue rn" (Known gaps).
28. **Newsletter.** (was 25) Answered in part 2026-11-20. Add a signup section. The service and submission destination are deferred.
29. **Studios.** (was 28) Should an owner be able to rename a studio, or add or remove one, in the admin? **Answered 2026-11-18:** yes, without a deployment. **Built 2026-11-21:** renaming, and taking a studio off the site and putting it back (question 59). Adding one is not built: it needs the owners' rules for a fourth studio (`HANDOFF.md`).
30. **The accent colour.** (was 32) Answered 2026-11-20. Darken the tan slightly and verify contrast for links and buttons.

**Testing and the code:**

31. **Testing.** (was 34) May the builder load Square's sandbox keys into the site on this computer, to try the real card form in a browser? Testing on the owner's iPhone is planned for later. (Whether the tester agent should go over everything again was answered on 2026-11-16: yes.) **Answered:** yes; the sandbox has been used since 2026-11-16, and the owners asked for extensive sandbox testing and several independent passes before launch (`HANDOFF.md`, "Earlier decisions"). The iPhone check is still to do.
32. **Merging.** (was 35) When should the `rebuild` branch become the main one (`master`)? **Answered in practice:** `rebuild` reaches `master` by pull requests, each merged on the owner's word once CI has passed (`AGENTS.md`).

**Ideas from another studio's website** (Mint Room Miami, read 2026-11-16; see "Ideas from another studio's website"):

33. **Agreements and waivers.** (was 36) Answered in part 2026-11-20. Owners manage versioned agreements by appointment type. Require electronic acceptance or signature at checkout. Approved text, assignment and signature details remain open.
34. **Membership by yearly spend — declined 2026-11-20, for now.** (was 40) The owner asked how it differs from the loyalty program. Loyalty here counts visits: every fifth day a client comes earns a free hour, whatever they spend, so it rewards coming often. A yearly-spend tier counts money: once a client's bookings in a year pass a set amount, every later booking that year is a percentage off by itself (Mint Room: 10% or 20%, at two amounts), so it rewards spending much, also in a few long bookings. Memberships, now decided, are a third thing: minutes paid for in advance, each month. The three can run side by side, or the yearly tier can be left out. Wanted? If so: the amounts, the percentages, and whether a year is the calendar year or the last twelve months.

### Owner decisions of 2026-11-22

The owner's brief "Website Updates — Homepage, Booking Calendar, Waitlist & Checkout", received 2026-11-22 (pasted into `HANDOFF.md`, which now carries it as [November 22 brief: booking page](HANDOFF.md#november-22-brief-booking-page), with the status of each item). Its aim, in the owner's words: "a booking experience that feels as simple and familiar as our original website while adding more functionality and giving the owner full control over booking forms, seasonal questions, and policies." Keep the design consistent with the original website, especially its colors, typography, calendar and booking experience. The owner's requirements, item by item:

1. **Remove the bundle sentence** "Studio Paris is included in every bundle.": unnecessary, and it makes the bundle options confusing. (It is on the booking page's first step, as "Paris is included in every bundle.")
2. **The discount's wording.** In place of "Paris is included in every bundle. 20% off when you book 2, 22% off when you book 3 or more.": "Save 20% when booking two studios and 22% when booking three studios." The words two and three, not numbers; clear, and consistent with the rest of the page.
3. **The booking calendar.** "Book a Studio" leads to a booking page much closer to the original website's calendar: a larger, more prominent month calendar in the original warm brown and tan, the entire month with navigation between months, only the dates with available times highlighted, the others disabled or muted, easy to use on a computer and a phone. Availability considers the selected studio, the appointment's length, existing bookings, the buffers and the opening hours: a date is highlighted for two hours only if a two-hour time fits on it. Once a date is chosen, only the start times that fit the whole length, in 15-minute steps. For bundles, all the studios at once. "Availability should be calculated based on whether the requested appointment can actually fit, not simply whether the studio has any open time that day." The flow: Select Studio → Select Duration → Select Date & Time and Add-ons → Next → Policies & Intake Form → Payment.
4. **"Join Waitlist"** on the booking page, for when the preferred appointment is unavailable. It collects the appointment type or studio, the preferred date, a preferred time range (for example 9 AM–12 PM or 2 PM–6 PM, a range rather than one start), first name, last name and email. Requests are saved in the admin, which views, manages and removes them. Availability is watched: when a cancellation or a move frees a slot that matches the request's appointment type, date, length and time range, the client is emailed with a direct booking link. The appointment is not booked for them, and slots stay first-come, first-served unless a separate hold is built. The same real-time availability rules as the calendar.
5. **"Check Code Balance"** on the booking page, clearly visible: a client enters a code and sees its balance before starting or finishing a booking. For gift cards, studio credit codes, loyalty reward codes and membership codes: whether the code is valid, the dollars or minutes left ("180 minutes remaining", "$75 remaining"), the expiry date if any, whether it was redeemed, and any restriction on the appointment types it is for. Checking deducts nothing.
6. **A mandatory policies review.** After the appointment's details and "Next", a separate page with the studio policies and booking agreement in a scrollable section. The client scrolls through all of it, which enables a required box: "I have read and agree to The Visuals Photo Studio's policies and booking terms." It must be ticked to go on to payment and cannot be bypassed. The acceptance is saved with the appointment: the date, the time and the version of the policies accepted. Easy to read on a phone, the agreement clearly visible.
7. **Booking Forms & Agreements**, an admin section, "especially important": the owner changes booking forms and policies without a developer and without redeploying. Intake questions: add, edit, delete or deactivate, reorder, required or optional; text fields, checkboxes, dropdowns and multiple choice; assigned to studios or appointment types; start and end dates for seasonal questions; a preview before publishing. The example is the Christmas decor acknowledgment, which clients accept when booking certain studios in the holiday season: the owner adds it, chooses its studios, makes it required, sets its dates and removes or deactivates it after the season. Policies: edit them, different policies or agreements for different appointment types, publish updated versions, preview first, a history of earlier versions, and which version each client accepted; an accepted agreement stays saved when the policies change.
8. **Throughout:** keep the booking, payment and appointment management working; the original branding; computers and phones; actual availability, not static highlights; no double bookings; the waitlist, the balance check and the agreement forms fully working; the intake questions and policies changed without a deployment. Test the whole flow: unavailable dates, the waitlist's emails, the balance checks, the policies' acceptance and payment.

Items 6 and 7 answer the November 20 decision "Agreements" in part: the form of acceptance is a tick box, with the sentence above. Item 3 restates November 20's "Booking calendar", and leaves its question about the date-first flow open.

### Owner decisions of 2026-11-22: the admin calendar

The owner's second brief of 2026-11-22, "Website Updates — Admin Calendar, Booking Management, Appointment Types & Availability" (pasted into `HANDOFF.md`, which carries it as [November 22 brief: admin calendar](HANDOFF.md#november-22-brief-admin-calendar), with the status of each item and the order of work for both briefs). The goal: a booking management system similar to Acuity Scheduling, with more flexibility and better integration with the studio's operations. Keep all existing functionality unless an item says otherwise. The owner's requirements:

1. **"Log In"** in place of "My Bookings" in the website's menu. Clients log in or create an account, and are then taken to their dashboard: upcoming and past appointments, receipts, loyalty rewards, memberships and available codes. Once logged in, the button may say "My Account".
2. **Studio colours** throughout the admin calendar, the booking details, the appointment labels and the other admin pages: Dayglow blue, Paris purple, Blanche green. Each bundle (Paris + Dayglow, Paris + Blanche, Paris + Dayglow + Blanche) a colour of its own, three more chosen by the builder, distinguishable from the studios'. A bundle is created and displayed as one appointment, not two or three: Paris + Dayglow for two hours is one booking in the bundle's colour with its details, and it blocks both Paris and Dayglow for the whole time. The same for three studios.
3. **Photography colours:** a different shade of the studio's colour (Dayglow photography a different blue, Paris a different purple, Blanche a different green), the same for bundles if supported, so that rentals and sessions are told apart without losing the studio's colour.
4. **On each appointment in the calendar:** the client's name; the studio or bundle; the length; the start and end, and the status, where space allows ("Sarah Johnson / Dayglow · 1.5 hours / 10:00 AM–11:30 AM"). Readable when several are close together.
5. **A pop-up, not a new page**, when an appointment is pressed: the calendar stays visible behind it; large enough, scrolling inside. It shows the client's first and last name, email, phone, the studio or bundle, the appointment type, date, start and end, length, the number of guests, the add-ons, the status, when it was booked, the payment details, the notes, the intake answers and the signed agreements and policies. At its top, three clearly visible buttons: **Book Another Appointment** (for the same customer, their contact details filled in), **Edit** (the studio, length, guests, add-ons and other details) and **Reschedule** (the date or time). Always available to the owner and authorized administrators, even when the client can no longer change the appointment; admin changes respect availability and resource conflicts unless an authorized override is explicitly used.
6. **Status colours**, at least for Confirmed, Checked In, Completed, Reschedule Pending, Rescheduled, Canceled and No Show, consistent across the admin and independent of the studio's colour (a Dayglow booking stays blue when it is Completed). Shown in the pop-up and, where space allows, on the calendar.
7. **Two dates and the payment** in the pop-up: the appointment date (when the client uses the studio) and the payment date (when the payment was processed). The payment section: the original price, the amount paid, the payment date, the method, refunds, the remaining balance or credit, and additional charges. The payment date or transaction reference should eventually open the transaction in Square: build for it, and show no fake transaction link before the integration is available.
8. **Forms & Agreements** on every appointment: every intake form, acknowledgment and policy the client completed at checkout. Administrators view the answers, the policies accepted, the exact version signed and the date and time of acceptance, open or download the signed agreement, and reach historical forms even when the form was edited or deleted since. A Christmas decoration policy accepted at booking stays attached to the appointment permanently.
9. **Zoom**: 0.25 | 0.5 | 0.75, as in Acuity: 0.25 compact (more hours and appointments), 0.5 medium, 0.75 expanded (larger blocks). It changes no length or step, and the calendar stays usable with several studios shown.
10. **Props & Resources**, for add-ons that are physical props in limited number (one pair of angel wings: once booked for a time, no overlapping appointment can book them). Administrators add props and equipment with an image, name, description, rental price and quantity; assign them to studios or appointment types; edit or delete them; mark them unavailable for a while; and see each one's upcoming reservations. All from the admin without redeploying. Availability is tracked by itself ("Angel Wings, Quantity: 1, Price: $40, Reserved: 10:00 AM–11:00 AM"); with a second pair the quantity becomes two. It follows appointments created, edited, rescheduled and canceled, and a prop is reserved for the whole appointment unless another window is set.
11. **Eight appointment types:** studio rentals (Dayglow, Paris, Blanche), bundles (Paris + Dayglow, Paris + Blanche, Paris + Dayglow + Blanche) and additional services (Photography, Content Creation). Photography and Content Creation are their own types, each associated with its studio or bundle: a photography appointment in Blanche is of the type Photography, and blocks Blanche's calendar and the photographer. The type and the resources it occupies are tracked separately. For each type the owner sets: the lengths, how far ahead clients can book, the minimum notice, the days, the hours, the start steps, the buffers, the rescheduling rules, the cancellation rules, same-day booking, whether clients see it, and its studios and resources ("make photography available only two weeks in advance while allowing regular studio rentals to be booked further ahead"). In the admin, without redeployment.
12. **Availability & Blocked Time**, a calendar of its own, apart from the appointments: block a time, a whole day, several consecutive days or a range; one studio, several, or all three at once ("Select All Studios", particularly important, for closing the business); recurring blocks; edit and remove. Closing December 24–26: open Availability & Blocked Time, select the days, "Select All Studios", "Block Entire Day", Save, and those dates leave the clients' calendar for every appointment type that uses those studios. An optional reason (Maintenance, Holiday Closure, Private Event, Other). Blocks show in the admin calendar, new bookings are refused during them, bundles and photography included, and the public calendar follows at once. A new block over existing appointments cancels nothing: the administrator is warned and resolves the conflict.
13. **Testing with a fully booked day:** all three studios booked all day, overlapping appointments, a bundle in two studios, one in all three, a photography appointment blocking its studio and the photographer, a prop of quantity one already reserved, the whole studio blocked for a day, a move to another date, a cancellation freeing its time. Verify readability, consistent colours, zoom, the pop-up, no double bookings, a bundle as one booking blocking every studio, resources, and that the public calendar shows only genuinely available dates and times. Test appointments and test payment data only: no real charges and no real client notifications.
14. **Throughout:** keep booking and payment working; studio colours consistent; a calendar easy to navigate and organized; details in pop-ups; every appointment type configurable; resources and blocked time managed without redeploying; changes to appointments, resources and blocks followed by what clients can book; no double bookings across studios, bundles, photographers and props; desktop, tablet and mobile.

Item 8 is built with items 6 and 7 of the first brief of the day. Item 6 was largely built before (the seven labels, each with a colour of its own).

### Owner decisions of 2026-11-20

These decisions follow the tests and Acuity-export rehearsal documented in the decision log. [HANDOFF.md](HANDOFF.md#november-20-implementation-brief) carries the implementation and verification checklist.

- **Manual membership cutover.** Transfer existing members manually, including balance and billing reconciliation. The owners declined an automated member migration. Upcoming appointment and read-only history imports are separate work.
- **Privacy and tax.** Draft the privacy policy for a lawyer before publication (**drafted 2026-11-21**, see the decision log). Prices exclude sales tax. Show a separate line at checkout and on receipts, provisionally at 6.5%, pending accountant confirmation of rate and treatment.
- **Payment summaries and Square uncertainty.** Show original payment, refunded amount, final amount paid, and any outstanding balance or remaining credit. The admin also needs payment status and the original Square transaction. For payment-link cancellations and refunds, check status in Square first. On an unknown status flag for manual review. On a verified payment use the cancellation policy saved when the client booked. This replaces Question 23's automatic full-refund fallback. **Built 2026-11-21:** the summary on the booking's page and in the account (the receipts and the admin's payments card already showed it), and the payment links that Square cannot answer about (question 54).
- **Rescheduling.** One complimentary move if requested at least 24 hours ahead, with one self-service move per customer appointment. Administrators may move it more than once. Additional reschedules are $20. Whether the fee applies to studio-initiated moves remains open.
- **Design and content.** Match the live site's overall look across home, studio pages, Christmas galleries, Styled Setups and reviews, preserving the palette, typography and spacing. Darken the tan slightly for accessible buttons and links. Manage seasonal photo and decor collections in Admin (**checked 2026-11-21**: the collections already built do it, see the decision log). Add a newsletter signup section and choose its delivery service later.
- **Loyalty.** Replace Apple Wallet once the built-in program is verified. Customers see stamps, available rewards and redeemed codes. Email their progress when a completed visit earns a stamp. Skip yearly-spend memberships. **The progress email built 2026-11-21:** "A stamp earned" under Admin → Emails, sent by the scheduled task when it counts a completed visit (`loyaltyProgressEmail`, `completeVisits` in tasks.ts); question 55 has what the builder decided in it.
- **Agreements.** Owners create and edit booking agreements, policies and waivers and assign them to appointment types. Require electronic acceptance or signature at checkout. Retain the exact accepted version and timestamp, visible to the studio and customer. Confirm approved text and the required form of signature.
- **Preview owner access.** The Vercel Preview environment should use the same authorized owner email list as Production.
- **Booking calendar.** Show a mobile-friendly full-month calendar after selecting studio or bundle and duration. Refresh dates and slots as bookings change. Show available start times in 15-minute steps after a date is selected, with all room and photographer conflicts and buffers enforced. Photography requires 14 full days' notice. Add-ons and guest count follow time selection, then use the existing checkout. Confirm whether to retain the earlier date-first flow.

### Answers of 2026-11-17 to the list of 2026-11-16

Katya, one of the owners, answered the business questions of the list of 2026-11-16 on 2026-11-17, in the document "The Visuals Photo Studio — Website questions and decisions": 26 of the 45 (questions 5, 8 to 16, 18, 20, 24, 26, 27, 29 to 31, 33, 37 to 39 and 41 to 44), and asked how question 40 differs from loyalty. Each answer is recorded here, in the document's groups, and where its subject is recorded below; the questions they raised are in the list of 2026-11-17 above. The owner asked for the record first. What has been built since says so on its line, and the rest says "To build". "As built" means the answer confirms what the site does. Here, and where these answers are recorded below, "question N" is a number of the list of 2026-11-16, which the answers follow, and "new question N" one of the list of 2026-11-17.

**Prices and memberships**

- **Weekend prices** (question 5) — **Decided and built:** weekend prices apply on Saturday and Sunday. An hour costs $95 in Blanche, $82 in Dayglow and $72 in Paris. Katya approved the prices for each longer duration in follow-up 1 below.
- **Extra people** (question 18) — **Decided:** $5 for each additional person, in every studio, as one add-on taken in a quantity: five additional people are five $5 add-ons, $25. It replaces the two add-ons of today ($30 for 1 to 2, $50 for 3 to 5), and the rule that an add-on is taken once per booking (a tick box, since 2026-11-15). To build: a quantity for an add-on, on the booking screen and the admin's new booking, in the price, the summaries and receipts, with the most per booking set on the add-on (new question 8).
- **Memberships** (question 24) — **Decided:** the new site sells memberships of Dayglow and Paris at the prices charged at checkout today: Dayglow $330 and $600 a month, Paris $300 and $550. A membership is billed monthly and gives a code that holds a number of studio minutes; the client uses it over several appointments until the minutes run out, and pays the difference for a booking longer than what is left. To build: the memberships themselves (a page to buy one, the monthly billing, what a member and the owners see) and a code of minutes, a new kind, spent by a booking's length. New question 9 asked what each holds and how it is billed: answered in follow-up 3 (2026-11-17, and the builder's eight questions on 2026-11-18), with questions 41 to 43 left.

**Booking, cancellations and access**

- **The refund window** (question 8) — **Decided:** a studio rental is refunded when cancelled at least 7 days ahead, a photography session at least 14 days ahead, both less the 5% processing fee; later, nothing is refunded. It changes answer 6 of 2026-11-15, which gave rentals 14 days too. **Built (2026-11-17):** `refundWindowHours` is 168 again, a setting, which the policies and guest information pages state by themselves; migration `0035_refund_window_and_food` moves a stored 336 to 168 and keeps any other value.
- **Moving a booking** (question 9) — **Decided:** a client moves a booking once, at least 24 hours before it starts, and the credit code is valid 90 days (as built). Administrators move a booking as often as needed (as built: "move to a new time" has no limit). The policies say that one reschedule is at no additional charge and each further one costs $20 (built 2026-11-17, see follow-up 6 below).
- **Terms that change later** (question 10) — **Decided:** changed terms apply only to bookings made afterwards; a booking keeps the policies in effect when it was made. **Built (2026-11-18):** each booking keeps its terms (the refund window and fee, the moving cut-off, the free moves and the fee for a further one, the credit's validity) from the moment it is made, in `bookings.terms` (migration `0040_booking_terms`), and a cancellation, a move, the reschedule code and the pet-fee credit read them from there (`termsFor` in `src/server/terms.ts`). A photography session keeps its own refund window. The appointment's page shows the booking's terms under "Terms of this booking", and says when the settings have changed since. A booking made before terms were kept follows the settings of the day: none of those was a real booking. A booking made with a reschedule code is a new booking, so it takes the terms of the day it is made.
- **The pet fee** (question 11) — **Decided and built:** an owner issues unused pet-fee credit manually under Admin → Codes after being told the pet did not attend. Returning pet-fee credit on a qualifying cancellation follows the existing rule.
- **A booking moved or changed by the studio** (question 14) — **Decided:** the appointment's page in the admin has a payment section, worked out again whenever the appointment is moved or changed: Paid, what the client has paid, each card payment a link that opens its transaction in Square; Outstanding balance, when the appointment now costs more than was paid; Remaining credit, when it costs less. To build: a move or change prices the booking again (today it keeps the price paid), the section, and the links to Square's dashboard. New question 11 asks which changes, and what becomes of remaining credit.
- **Moving, by request** (question 20) — **Decided:** yes, a client can also ask the studio to move a booking. **Built (2026-11-18):** "Ask the studio to move it" on the booking's page and in the account, with "When would suit you?". It marks the booking Reschedule Pending (requirement 10), writes what the client wrote into its history, emails the studio (`OWNER_NOTIFY_EMAIL`, else the first owner; a reply reaches the client) and the client a copy (`askStudioToMove` in `src/server/manage.ts`). The instant reschedule code stays beside it. The studio then moves the booking on the appointment's page, or takes the label off with "Confirmed". When it is offered: question 39 under "Rules built on the builder's reading", confirmed 2026-11-18.
- **The door code, and emails around a visit** (question 29) — **Decided:** the confirmation, with the door code, is sent right after booking (as built); a reminder 24 hours before the appointment, and a follow-up after it asking for a review. **Both built**, sent by the scheduled task, which needs the host (new question 1): the review request on 2026-11-17 (follow-up 7), the reminder on 2026-11-18. The reminder gives the reference, studios, time, address, what is still to pay, and how to get in as the owners have set it then; the hours before the start are the setting "Reminder email (hours before the start)" (24; 0 sends none). When it is and is not sent: question 38 under "Rules built on the builder's reading", confirmed 2026-11-18. Migration `0039_booking_reminder`.
- **Emails** (question 33) — **Decided:** an admin section of email templates where the owners create, edit and customise every automated email. **Built (2026-11-18):** Admin → Emails lists the 18 emails the site sends clients, in four groups, with when each is sent and whose wording it is sent in. Each opens in an editor: its subject and text, the parts the site fills in written in double braces (`{{name}}`, `{{first_name}}`, `{{reference}}`, `{{when}}`, `{{payment}}`, `{{booking_link}}` and so on, each explained, and put in by pressing it), and a preview with made-up details that follows the typing, with or without the parts only some of these emails have. Saving starts the owners' wording from the next email sent; "Use the site's wording" goes back (after a "Yes"), and the site's wording stays shown for copying from. An email stays word for word as it was until its template is changed: checked against the builders as they were, over every branch of each email. A line holding nothing but parts the email has nothing for is left out, so one template serves a rental and a session, paid online or at the studio. Wording the site could not send is not saved: a part the email does not have, braces that are not a part, a part of several lines in the subject, or the leaving out of a part the email cannot do without (the sign-in code, a booking's link, the money it states). **Confirmed 2026-11-18:** The owners should have an option to customize every email. What was decided in building it, for the owners to confirm: question 40 under "Rules built on the builder's reading". Migration `0041_email_templates`; `src/lib/email-templates.ts`, `src/server/email-templates.ts`.

**Loyalty, free hours and credits**

- **Free hours** (question 12) — **Decided:** only Paris + Dayglow, Paris + Blanche and all three are bundles; Blanche + Dayglow cannot be booked together without Paris (built, see follow-up 8 below). On a longer booking a free hour covers one hour, and the client pays the rest (as built). A booking that uses a free hour earns no stamp (as built). A free-hour booking that is moved gives a credit for its whole value (as built). Loyalty switched off and on again starts over: what was earned before does not count, and free hours already sent stay valid (built 2026-11-17, see follow-up 9 below).
- **Codes on a cancelled booking, and redeemed rewards** (question 15) — **Decided:** a code used on a cancelled booking can be used again, and an owner may restore a redeemed reward (as built).

**Photography**

- **The photographer and sessions** (questions 13 and 27) — **Decided:** available from 9 a.m., and the last session may start at 4 p.m. (follow-up 10 below: the photographer's day is 9 to 5, as built); 15 minutes between sessions, always; the same relevant add-ons as rentals, and one price all week; cancelled at least 14 days ahead, refunded less the 5% fee, later nothing; booked at least 336 hours ahead (verified under follow-up 11); one photographer, no second for now, a resource that a session in any studio takes from all the others. All as built. Photography-only percentage codes can now be created and cancelled under Admin → Codes. See follow-up 12 for credit and reward eligibility.

**Studio operations and staff**

- **Studio Managers** (question 16) — **Decided:** the defaults as built: they record payments, send payment links, open customers' pages and block time; prices, refunds, loyalty corrections and the website stay switched off.
- **Extra charges** (question 26) — **Decided:** overtime, cleaning and damage are recorded on an appointment: overtime easy to reach and to see on the appointment's page; cleaning and damage available in a less prominent place, as they are rare. To build; new question 19 asks the amounts and how they are paid.
- **Food and drinks** (question 30) — **Decided and built (2026-11-17):** not allowed inside the studios. The policies and the guest information page say "Food and drinks are not allowed inside the studios." (text the owners edit, and the pages' starting content in `built-in-pages.ts`); migration `0035_refund_window_and_food` replaces the earlier sentence, "not permitted on furniture", where a database still has it.
- **The lights** (question 31) — **Decided:** "Amaran 150, Godox and Neewer" (as on the site).

**Ideas from Mint Room Miami**

- **After hours** (question 37) — **Decided and built:** after-hours bookings are by request. See follow-up 15 below.
- **Events and whole-studio rental** (question 38) — **Decided:** not now; perhaps later.
- **A prep or makeup room** (question 39) — **Decided:** the makeup rooms belong to individual studios and are not rented separately. Nothing to build; a studio's facts can say it has one.
- **Membership by yearly spend** (question 40) — **Open:** the owner asked how it differs from the loyalty program before deciding; the difference is given with new question 34.
- **A pet deposit** (question 41) — **Decided:** no.
- **Fees for damage and cleaning on the policies** (question 42) — **Decided:** not now.
- **An Instagram feed** (question 43) — **Decided:** yes, on the home page. To build: a block of the page editor that shows the latest posts; new question 20 asks the account. **Confirmed 2026-11-18:** Follow-up 14 — the owners will provide the account connection after hosting is set up.
- **A blog** (question 44) — **Decided:** no.

### Ideas from another studio's website (read 2026-11-16)

The owner asked for good ideas from Mint Room Miami's website (mintroommiami.com), a daylight studio with seven rooms. Read on 2026-11-16, its home page, booking page, prices, FAQ, policies, membership, events, decor, contact and two of its rooms. What it does that this site does too: a search by date and time for the rooms that are free (the second booking flow), prices by weekday and weekend and cheaper longer bookings, several rooms in one booking, an account to move or cancel with studio credit, and the door code sent with the confirmation. What the owners can do already, without code: a page of seasonal set-ups (a collection with dates, and a gallery), an events page, and lights or props for hire as add-ons.

To build, needing no decision (the owners fill in the content):

- **Studio facts** — **Built (2026-11-16).** On each studio, set under Studios & add-ons and shown on the studios page: its size, ceiling height, which way its windows face, the cyclorama's size, what is included (an amenities list), and a floor plan picture. Mint Room shows these on every room's page.
- **A reviews block** — **Built (2026-11-16).** For pages: quotes with a name and, optionally, where they were written, and the stars given.
- **A logo strip block** — **Built (2026-11-16)** as the block Logos: the brands the studio has worked for.

Decided on 2026-11-17 (see "Answers of 2026-11-17"): after hours by request only, no events page for now, no makeup room to rent on its own, no pet deposit, no published fees for now, an Instagram feed, and no blog. Still open: the waiver and membership by yearly spend (questions 33 and 34 of the list of 2026-11-17).

### Stack

- **Language and framework** — **Decided (2026-11-14):** TypeScript, Next.js (React), PostgreSQL. Alternative considered and rejected: Python with Django.
- **Where the new code lives** — **Decided (2026-11-14):** `web/` in this repository, on the `rebuild` branch.
- **What happens to the Flask prototype** — **Superseded (2026-11-15).** Decided 2026-11-14: it stays untouched as a reference. Replaced 2026-11-15: the owner had it deleted from the repository and from the development machine. It remains in git history; its last version is commit `f1420bc`.
- **Node.js** — **Decided (2026-11-14):** installed with nvm (current LTS).
- **Database access** — **Decided (2026-11-14, by Claude):** Drizzle ORM with SQL migrations in `web/drizzle/`. PGlite for local development and tests so nothing has to be installed; PostgreSQL through `DATABASE_URL` in production. No production database has been chosen or created yet.
- **Payments** — **Decided (2026-11-15): Square, in test mode for now (answer 3).** Built as proposed: Square behind a provider interface, called over HTTPS, with a mock for development. The owner confirmed Square on 2026-11-15, in test mode for now; real keys are for when the owner says so. It works in Square's sandbox (verified 2026-11-15, see the decision log), payment links included; production keys have never been used. The sandbox keys from the prototype's settings file are kept on the development machine in `web/.env.square-sandbox`. Git ignores that file and the site does not read it; `npm run check:square` does. To see the real card form locally, copy its four lines into `web/.env.local`. The browser tests then fail, because they expect the pretend processor.
- **Hosting** — **Later (answer 1, 2026-11-15).** The owner will decide later. The scheduler (answer 4), the media storage and the database all depend on it.

### Requirements from the owner (received 2026-11-14)

1. **Several studios in one booking** — **Built (2026-11-14); discount decided 2026-11-15 (answer 8); allowed bundles built (2026-11-17, follow-up 8).** Paris + Dayglow, Paris + Blanche and all three can be booked together. Blanche and Dayglow cannot be booked together without Paris. Both customer booking flows and new bookings made in the admin enforce the rule. The admin can still move an existing Blanche + Dayglow appointment to another time. The discount is a setting keyed by studio count, with defaults of 20% for two and 22% for three.
2. **Loyalty: one free hour after five visits** — **Built (2026-11-15); the two open details were decided the same day (answer 9).** The first version (2026-11-14: a 10% code after every visit) is gone. Codes it sent stay valid as ordinary codes.
   - **The owner's rule (2026-11-15):** a client who visits 5 times, on 5 separate days, gets 1 hour free at any studio. The free hour is not applicable to any studio bundles.
   - **The owner's answers to the builder's questions (2026-11-15):**
     - A free hour is valid for 90 days (answer 9, 2026-11-15; it was 60).
     - The count starts again after each reward, so every fifth visit earns an hour, and "people can collect several hours before redeeming them".
     - A visit paid for with the free hour does not count towards the next one.
     - The one-hour price is taken off: "the booking is free if they book for one hour, they pay for 30 minutes if they book for 1.5, etc".
     - A visit made as a bundle counts towards the five, "but max of one count per day".
   - **How it works:** a visit is a completed booking, so a cancelled booking or a no-show does not count. The scheduled task completes a visit `loyaltyDelayHours` after it ends. Visits are counted per customer email address, one per studio-local day (`src/lib/domain/loyalty.ts`). When the fifth day is completed, the customer is emailed a single-use code of kind `free_hour` (`src/server/loyalty.ts`), valid for `loyaltyValidDays`. Every further five days earn another, and a customer can hold several. A photography session does not count (answer 13, 2026-11-15; built the same day). At checkout the code takes the booked studio's price for one hour, on that kind of day, off a booking of one studio. With several studios it is refused with the reason, and it does not pay for add-ons. The account page shows the count ("3 of 5 visits"), and the guest information page states the rule. The owner can hand out a free hour under Codes, and sets the number of visits, the validity and the delay under Settings.
   - **The builder's readings, decided 2026-11-17 (question 12), confirmed but for two:** a bundle is not any two or more studios booked together but one of three combinations (requirement 1); and loyalty switched off and on again starts over, so that what was earned before does not count (built 2026-11-17: follow-up 9; free hours already sent stay valid). The others: on a long booking whose price per hour is lower, what comes off is still the one-hour price. A visit on which a free hour was used does not count even when part of it was paid for; the same holds for a free hour the owner handed out. A booking made with a free hour and then moved gives a reschedule code for its whole value, like any discount (see "A discount on a moved booking"), and the visit made with that code counts. While loyalty is switched off nothing is sent. Since 2026-11-17, switching it on again starts the stamps from nothing (`loyaltyStartedAt`), so nothing earned before is sent afterwards; until then a customer got what they had earned at their next visit.
   - **Decided (2026-11-15):** one free hour per booking (answer 9), because checkout takes one code. Each free hour is valid 90 days from the day it is earned (answer 9).
   - It only happens if something calls `GET /api/cron/tick` on a schedule, which is not set up yet. SMS deferred (US carrier registration needed).
3. **Staff see only their day** — **Built (2026-11-14); contact visibility decided 2026-11-15: shown by default (answer 15).** Owner and staff roles with passwordless sign-in. Staff see a day sheet at `/staff` for dates the owner assigns (times, studio, customer name, add-ons, notes), never revenue or other dates. The owner assigns days in `/admin/staff`. The `staffSeesContact` setting controls whether phone and email are shown. Staff can mark attendance (came / no-show) after a booking's start time. Needs: whether phone numbers are shown by default.
4. **Weekday and weekend prices** — **Built (2026-11-14); approved weekend rates entered (2026-11-17, follow-up 1).** Prices are stored per studio, day type and duration, and a date can be repriced as the other day type for holidays. The owner edits prices in `/admin/prices`. The weekend rates in follow-up 1 replace the original defaults, which matched weekday rates.
5. **Admin and customer accounts** — **Built (2026-11-14).** One user table with customer, staff and owner roles. Sign-in by emailed six-digit one-time code, no passwords. The first sign-in creates the account; `OWNER_EMAILS` controls who is the owner. Guest checkout stays (the secret link in the confirmation email still works). Customer account at `/account` shows bookings, codes and cancel/reschedule actions.
6. **Two ways to book** — **Built (2026-11-14).** `/book` offers "I know which studio I want" and "I know when I want to come". The second shows, for a date and duration, how many studios are free at each time, then which ones.
7. **Reschedule code** — **Built (2026-11-14); cutoffs decided 2026-11-17 (question 9): as built.** Reschedule button on the confirmation page and the account page. Releases the booking and issues a credit code for the amount paid, plus what a discount code took off that booking (added 2026-11-15, see "A discount on a moved booking" below), emailed to the customer. The customer books a new time and enters the code at checkout; if the new booking costs more they pay the difference, if less the rest stays on the code. A rescheduled booking cannot be refunded and cannot be moved again (one move per booking, `maxReschedules`). **Decided (2026-11-17):** a client moves a booking once, at least 24 hours before the start, and the credit is valid 90 days; administrators move a booking as often as needed (their "move to a new time", requirement 10, has no limit); the policies are to say that one reschedule is complimentary (question 12 of the list of 2026-11-17 asks what a further one costs).
8. **Automatic refunds** — **Built (2026-11-14); fee decided 2026-11-15: 5% kept (answer 6); window decided again 2026-11-17 (question 8): 7 days for rentals, not changed in the settings yet.** Cancelling 14 or more days ahead (answer 6; it was 7; 7 again for rentals, 14 for photography, from 2026-11-17) automatically refunds to the card minus the 5% processing fee, via the Square refund API (or recorded for retry when the processor is not configured). The owner is emailed on every refund, and once when a refund first fails. Cancelling inside the window or after a reschedule gives no refund, and the customer is told so before confirming. The owner can cancel any booking and choose the refund amount in `/admin/bookings`, or issue a partial refund without cancelling. Failed refunds are retried by the scheduled task. The pet fee is never part of a card refund, and money taken at the studio is refunded by hand (see answers 11 and 14).

### Second set of requirements from the owner (received 2026-11-15)

Sent by the owner on 2026-11-15, to be recorded here and followed afterwards. **None of it is built yet.** The goal in the owner's words: "A reliable booking system that is easy for clients to use and gives administrators full visibility and control over studio appointments."

**How to work on them (the owner's instructions):**

- Do not redesign or remove existing features unless necessary.
- Preserve existing appointments, customer information and booking records.
- Keep the admin interface clean, organized and intuitive. No decorative buttons or calendar controls that do not work.
- Make every change work with the existing booking system and database.
- If a requirement conflicts with the existing system, explain the issue and propose a solution before making changes. The conflicts found on first reading are listed with each item.
- Do not call a feature complete unless it has been implemented and tested.

**The owner's answers (2026-11-15)** to the conflicts and proposals the builder put to them. With these, requirements 9 to 15 are decided and the builder was told to start, in this order: start times and gap, status labels and the two admin levels, the calendar, photography, then the mobile pass and the test list.

- **Buffers:** yes. "7 minutes before and after appointment so two appointments could be as close as 15 minutes between them." So: start times every 15 minutes, 15 minutes kept free after each booking in every studio, and the calendar draws 7 minutes before and 7 after each appointment.
- **Status labels:** yes to the proposal. The customer's own reschedule code stays as it is. The labels are added, with a history. Reschedule Pending is set by an administrator and keeps the slot. Administrators get "move to a new time".
- **Bookings made by administrators:** yes. Created from the calendar, under the same availability rules, with no online card charge.
- **Photography:** "from the admin side we should be able to block open time and set availability limits, so photographer could choose the time slots they are ready to work and they are not ready." A photography booking blocks that photographer as a resource, so that nobody else can book the photographer for the same time, whatever the studio. The builder's proposal to take the packages and prices from the live price list was not objected to, and is what will be done.
- **Two levels of admin:** see requirement 15.
- **Loyalty:** a visit counts from Completed, not from Checked In, and only once per day even with several appointments that day. This is what requirement 2 already does.

9. **Admin calendar: month, week and day** — **Built (2026-11-15)** at `/admin/calendar`, for both admin levels (staff see the days they may see). Every control is a link or a form, so a view has an address of its own. A phone gets the week as a list, day by day, and the month as a dot for each appointment; the day keeps its columns. From an appointment an administrator can "move to a new time" (two steps: the date, then one of the times that are free), and "New booking" makes a booking for a client (`/admin/calendar/new`). Each studio's colour is a column of the `studios` table.
   - Month view: all appointments of the month. Week view: appointments by day and time. Day view: a detailed timeline of the day.
   - Each of the three studios (Dayglow, Blanche, Paris) has its own colour, the same in every view.
   - Filter by one studio, or show all studios together.
   - An appointment shows the client's name, the time, the studio and the status. Clicking it opens its full details and allows changes.
   - Easy to move between dates, and to return to today.
   - The day and week views show the buffer periods of requirement 11.
   - The deleted prototype's admin had a calendar; nothing of it was carried over.
10. **Appointment status labels** — **Built (2026-11-15).** Moving a booking sets Rescheduled and writes the old and the new time into the history. How it is built: Completed and No Show are the booking's `status`; Checked In, Reschedule Pending and Rescheduled are a `label` beside a booking that stays `confirmed`, so Reschedule Pending keeps the slot without any code having to learn about it (`src/lib/domain/labels.ts`). Every change writes a line to `booking_events`. The labels are set on the appointment page (`/admin/appointments/[ref]`) and on the day sheet. Completed and No Show can be set once the appointment has started, Checked In from an hour before, and "Confirmed" takes a label off again. The scheduled task does not complete a booking that is Reschedule Pending.
    - Statuses an administrator can set and change by hand: **Checked In** (the client has arrived and checked in), **Completed** (the appointment has finished), **Rescheduled** (it has been moved to a new date or time), **No Show** (the client did not arrive), **Reschedule Pending** (the client has asked to reschedule but has not yet chosen a new date or time).
    - Each status has a small, visually distinct badge, visible in the appointment's details and in the calendar.
    - A history of status changes is kept, with the date and time of each change.
    - Reschedule Pending keeps the original appointment's details and does **not** free its time slot. Once a new date is confirmed, the administrator reschedules the appointment and its status is updated.
    - **Conflicts with what exists:** the statuses today are `pending_payment`, `confirmed`, `completed`, `cancelled`, `no_show` and `expired`. There is no "checked in" and no history; staff mark "came" or "no-show" on the day sheet. Moving a booking is the customer's own action (requirement 7): the booking is released at once and a credit code is emailed, so the slot is freed immediately, nothing is ever pending, and an administrator cannot move a booking to a new time. **Settled (2026-11-15):** the customer's reschedule code stays, beside the administrator's Reschedule Pending and "move to a new time"; staff can change all the labels (requirement 15); a visit counts for loyalty from Completed.
11. **Buffers and start times** — **Built (2026-11-15).** The week and the day draw 7 hatched minutes above and below each appointment. A booking made by an administrator goes through the same `createBooking` as one made online, so the same rules hold; only the customers' notice period does not apply, and nothing is charged online (the booking carries `made_by`, and its total reads "To pay at the studio").
    - A 7-minute buffer before each appointment and a 7-minute buffer after it.
    - At least 15 minutes between consecutive appointments in the same studio. The two buffers total 14 minutes; the rule is a minimum gap of 15 minutes, not another 15 on top of the buffers.
    - Clients are offered start times in 15-minute steps only: 9:00, 9:15, 9:30, 9:45 and so on.
    - Each studio's calendar is independent: an appointment in Dayglow does not block Blanche or Paris. No overlapping appointments within a studio. Times that are not available are left out automatically, from the appointment's length, the existing reservations and the buffers.
    - The same rules apply to bookings made by clients and to bookings made by administrators.
    - **Conflicts with what exists:** start times are every 30 minutes (`slotIncrementMinutes`). Each studio has a clean-up time after a booking only (10 minutes, editable per studio under "Studios & add-ons"), and nothing is kept free before one. Administrators cannot make a booking at all today, they can only block time, so "administrator-created bookings" is a new feature. **Settled (2026-11-15):** start times every 15 minutes and 15 minutes free after each booking, which replaces the 10-minute clean-up time as the default; administrators create bookings from the calendar under the same rules, with no online card charge.
12. **Photography booking** — **Built (2026-11-15); hours and lead time given on 2026-11-15 (answer 12).** The booking page has the section, which leads to `/book/photography`: the booking screen with `photography` set. A session is one studio and one package (`photo_packages`, from the live price list: Blanche $375, $575, $730, $840 for 1 to 4 hours; Dayglow $315, $515, $670, $775; Paris $315, $515, $670 for 1 to 3). Its price includes the studio. A time is offered only when the studio and the photographer are both free, and booking holds both: `photographer_holds` has the same exclusion constraint as `studio_holds`, so the photographer cannot be booked twice for one time, whatever the studio. Under Admin, Photography, the owners set the photographer's working hours by weekday, block time, and change the packages; staff can do the first two once the owners switch it on. Since the owner's answers of 2026-11-15 (built the same day): the photographer works 9 am to 5 pm, so the last one-hour session starts at 4 pm; a session is booked at least two weeks ahead (`photoMinLeadHours`, 336 hours; never less than the notice for a rental), which the booking screen, the services page and the policies page all state from the setting; and a session does not count as a visit for loyalty. Built on the builder's reading, confirmed on 2026-11-17 (question 13) but for the hours and the codes: the 9 to 5 applies to every day of the week until the owners switch days off under Admin, Photography; between two sessions the photographer keeps the studios' 15 minutes; checkout separates rental and photography codes (see follow-up 12 below); the same add-ons are offered; one price for weekdays and weekends, as on the live list; a session is refunded when cancelled 14 days or more ahead (`photoRefundWindowHours`); and an administrator, who cannot book a session from the admin yet, would not be bound by the two weeks. **Decided (2026-11-17), questions 13 and 27, not built yet:** the photographer is available 9 a.m. to 4 p.m. (question 16 of the list of 2026-11-17 asks whether 4 pm ends the last session or is its latest start); photography gets discount codes of its own, separate from rentals (**built**, see follow-up 12 below); no second photographer for now.
    - On the public studio booking page, a clearly visible section for clients who have no photographer, attractive and easy to find without distracting from the studio rental. Wording to use: the heading "DON'T HAVE A PHOTOGRAPHER?", the text "No problem! You can book a professional photoshoot with our in-house photographer, including studio rental.", and the button "BOOK A PHOTOGRAPHER".
    - The button leads to a separate photography booking flow: choose the studio; choose a photography package or a session length; see the package's price and what it includes; choose an available date and time; complete the booking and pay.
    - It must check studio availability, so that nothing is double-booked.
    - **The photographer's time (the owner, 2026-11-15):** the admin can set availability limits and block open time, so that the photographer chooses the slots they are ready to work and those they are not. A photography booking holds that photographer as a resource: nobody else can book the photographer for the same time, whatever the studio.
    - **Packages and prices:** taken from the live price list (see "Live reference" above), as the studio prices were. The cancellation terms follow the live site: 14 days for photography.
13. **Mobile** — **"Extremely important" to the owner. Checked and fixed (2026-11-15), in a desktop browser set to phone sizes; not yet on real phones; the check on a real phone is later (answer 16).** `e2e/z-mobile.spec.ts` runs with the browser tests and checks, on eight screen sizes from 320px (the first iPhone SE) to 430px (Pro Max), a folded Galaxy, an Android phone and an iPad: that no page is wider than the screen, that text is at least 11px, that every control is at least 40px tall, and that no field has text under 16px (an iPhone zooms the page on such a field). It makes a whole booking on four iPhone sizes, books a photographer, books starting from the time, uses the menu, and goes through the calendar, an appointment and every admin page. What it found and what was fixed: a studio card wider than the smallest screen, a 28px menu button, fields that made an iPhone zoom, small calendar text, a steps bar whose five names ran together at 320px, and a long unbroken word that pushed a page 15,000px wide. Not done: Safari on a real iPhone, a real foldable, a screen reader.
    - Fully responsive and designed for phones on purpose, not a compressed desktop site. iPhones above all: Pro Max, Plus, standard and mini models, and smaller and older screens. Also foldable and dual-screen phones where supported, Android phones, tablets and desktop browsers.
    - Text is readable without zooming. Buttons are large enough to tap. Nothing is wider than the screen, and nothing scrolls sideways unless a component is meant to. Booking forms are easy to complete on a phone. Calendars and date pickers are made for touch. Menus work smoothly on small screens. Images resize without distortion. Every button, dropdown and control works. Spacing, alignment and type are consistent. No overlapping elements, clipped text or excessive empty space.
14. **Testing before any of it is called complete** — **Done (2026-11-15), each item by an automated test that runs every time.** (1, 2) the three views and the studio filter, (5) moving to a new time, and a booking made for a client: the calendar test in `e2e/site.spec.ts`. (3, 4) labels, and Reschedule Pending keeping the slot and the details: `src/server/appointments.test.ts` and the staff test. (6, 7, 8) no double booking, the 15-minute gap and the 15-minute steps: `src/server/bookings.test.ts` and the "taken time" test. (9) a studio without photography and (10) a session with the studio included: the first and the last test of `e2e/site.spec.ts`, and `src/server/photography.test.ts`. (11) a whole booking on four iPhone sizes and (12) every page on a phone: `e2e/z-mobile.spec.ts`. On 12, the flows are exercised and every page is measured; not every single control is pressed. The owner's list: switching between the month, week and day views; filtering by studio; assigning and changing status labels; marking Reschedule Pending without losing information; rescheduling to a confirmed new date; preventing double bookings; the 15-minute minimum gap; start times in 15-minute steps; booking a studio without photography; booking a photography package with the studio included; completing a whole booking on several iPhone screen sizes; and every page and control working on mobile.

15. **Two levels of admin: owners and staff** — **Built (2026-11-15); a third level, the Studio Manager, was added the same day (requirement 28).** Eight switches under Staff (the seventh, to set the photographer's hours and block their time, came with requirement 12; the eighth, to record a payment taken at the studio and to email a payment link, with answer 14), "What staff can do" (`src/lib/staff-abilities.ts`): change status labels (on by default, as the owner said), see every day and not only assigned days, see contact details, see prices, move a booking, make a booking for a client. Staff get the calendar, the day sheet and the appointment page, with a short menu of their own; every other admin page answers "not found" for them.
    - "Admin should be 2 leveled: 1st is us, the owners - and second is the staff."
    - The owners can adjust what staff are able to do in their admin level, without redeploying the site.
    - "Staff should be able to change all the labels."
    - **What exists:** owners (the addresses in `OWNER_EMAILS`) have the whole admin. Staff have the day sheet at `/staff` for the days the owner assigns, where they mark "came" or "no-show", and one switch decides whether they see phone numbers and emails (`staffSeesContact`). The abilities are to become switches of that kind, stored in the settings and set by the owners.

### Third set of requirements from the owner (received 2026-11-15): the client portal

Sent by the owner on 2026-11-15, after the second set was built. Recorded as requirements 16 to 20. On 2026-11-15 the owner said to start on what is recorded here while they think about the open questions, and **the parts that need none of those answers were built the same day**: the status of each is on its line. Where a requirement conflicts with what exists, the conflict and a proposed solution are listed, as the owner asked. Terms used here: a **stamp** is one counted visit day (the existing loyalty rule). In the spec, **photographers** means the clients who rent studios, not the in-house photographer. A **studio session** is a studio rental. A **reward** is a free-hour code.

16. **Client login, appointments, receipts and payments** (spec section 4, parts 1 to 3) — **Built in part (2026-11-15).** Built: My Appointments, and Receipts & Payments with PDF files. Not built, each waiting for an answer listed under Open: passwords and Google sign-in (sign-in stays the emailed code), appointments from before this site, a tax line, and a "request" to cancel (cancelling still happens at once, within the terms). A request to reschedule was decided on 2026-11-17, question 20, and built on 2026-11-18, beside the instant reschedule code.
    - **How it is built:** *My Appointments* (`/account/appointments`) has two tabs, each with an address of its own. Upcoming shows every booking still to come with its studio, date, start, end, length and status, the full price breakdown, how to get in, and the buttons to move or cancel it within the terms. Past shows everything else and can be narrowed by date, studio and status (Completed, Cancelled, Rescheduled, Missed). A booking the studio moved keeps its reference and says "Moved from ... to ..."; a booking the client released for a reschedule code stays in Past beside the new one. *Receipts & Payments* (`/account/receipts`) shows one calendar year at a time: what was paid, what was refunded and the difference; a receipt for each booking, to view, print or download as a PDF; one PDF with a summary page and every receipt of the year; and each payment and refund by date. A payment counts in the year it was made and a refund in the year it was made, on the studio's clock. A booking that still owes everything is listed apart as "to pay": it is not a receipt. Credit is under My Rewards, not here. A receipt shows the appointment, the studio, each line at the price it was booked at, discounts, every payment with how it was paid, refunds including ones on their way, and a status (Paid, Partly paid, Partly refunded, Refunded). No tax is shown, because none is modelled.
    - **Asked:** sign-in with an email address, with email and password or Google if supported, and password reset. Appointments connected to the account by verified email, including earlier ones. **My Appointments**: upcoming (studio, date, start and end, duration, status, confirmation and studio instructions, a request to reschedule or cancel within policy) and past (completed, cancelled, rescheduled; filter by date, studio and status; a rescheduled booking keeps its original record). **Receipts & Payments**: by date, with appointment date, studio, amount paid, taxes, discounts and payment status; each receipt viewed, downloaded and printed as a PDF; all receipts of a calendar year at once; an annual payment summary for tax, ideally; refunds and partial refunds; receipts kept apart from unpaid invoices and credits. A receipt shows what was actually paid, never the current price, and no receipt is made from an estimate.
    - **What exists:** sign-in by emailed six-digit code, which creates the account and proves the email. Bookings are already found by that verified email, and the account page lists upcoming and past bookings. A customer's reschedule releases the booking and issues a credit; the original booking stays in the database. The payments table records each charge, refunds are recorded too, and a booking stores its discounts and credits. No real payment has been taken by this site yet (sandbox only), so there is nothing to reconcile.
    - **Conflicts and proposed solutions:**
        - *Passwords.* Requirement 5 chose sign-in without passwords. Proposed: keep emailed codes, which already prove the email and reset sign-in. Passwords would add stored hashes, reset emails and lockouts for the same result. The owner decides.
        - *Google sign-in.* Not built. Proposed: an optional later addition that only proves the same email address.
        - *Appointments before the new site.* This site has no history from before its launch. Earlier appointments and payments are in Acuity Scheduling and Square, which the live site uses. Proposed: import them from exports of those two, attach them to an account only once the owner of that email has signed in with a code, and take amounts from Square's payment records, never from current prices. Needs the owner's go-ahead and a privacy policy first.
        - *PDF receipts.* The site makes no PDFs. Proposed: generate them on the server from the payment records; the library is chosen when building.
        - *Taxes.* The site currently has no tax line. On 2026-11-20 the owner requested tax-exclusive prices and a separate line at checkout and on receipts, using 6.5% provisionally. Confirm the live rate and taxable amounts with the accountant. See the [November 20 decisions](#owner-decisions-of-2026-11-20).
