        - *Unpaid invoices.* Decided (answer 14, 2026-11-15): a "mark as paid" record for bookings the owner makes, and a payment link sent to the client through Square. Both are built (2026-11-15): a booking made in the admin shows what it still owes, the client can be emailed a link to pay it by card, and each payment taken at the studio is a record with its amount, how it was paid, who recorded it and when. So such a booking is an unpaid invoice until it is paid, and its payment records are what a receipt will be made from.
        - *"Request" a reschedule or cancellation.* Today both happen at once, within policy. Proposed: a request from the portal sets Reschedule Pending (requirement 10), which keeps the slot until staff choose a new time; the instant reschedule code stays beside it.
    - **Open:** passwords as well as codes, or codes only; Google sign-in now, later or never; import earlier history or start from launch; the tax rule; instant reschedule, request, or both (decided 2026-11-17: both, question 20); receipts as one PDF each, one file per year, or both. Since answered: codes, with Google sign-in perhaps later, and earlier history imported as far as practical (2026-11-18, questions 24 and 25); the tax rule in part (2026-11-20, "Owner decisions of 2026-11-20").

17. **My Rewards and loyalty** (spec section 4, parts 4 and 5, and "My Rewards") — **Built (2026-11-15), except the Apple Wallet link**, which waits for the system's name. The owners' corrections are on each customer's page (requirement 19).
    - **How it is built:** *My Rewards* (`/account/rewards`) shows the stamps as a row of circles in the site's colours with the count ("3 / 5") and the words "two more stamps until your free studio hour"; the stamps collected, the free hours earned and the stamps still needed; the date of every visit that gave a stamp; and any change the studio made by hand, with its reason. Below it, every reward ever given: its name (Free Studio Hour), its code, the day it was issued, the day it expires and its status. Available has a Copy Code button; Redeemed says when it was used and for which appointment; Expired and Canceled stay in the list. Credit and discount codes are listed apart, because they are not rewards. Built differently from the proposal in one respect: stamps earned by visits are still counted from the completed bookings and not copied into a ledger, so a visit cannot give two stamps and a booking that changes later is counted as it now is. The ledger (`loyalty_ledger`) holds only what an owner changes by hand. A reward is always one use: the Codes page no longer makes a free hour usable several times.
    - **Asked:** a My Rewards section listing each reward's name (for example "Free Studio Hour"), its unique code, its issue date, its expiry if any, and its status: **Available** (not used, usable on an eligible booking), **Redeemed** (with the date and the appointment), **Expired**, or **Canceled** (invalidated by an administrator). A Copy Code button. Eligible rewards applied at checkout. No code redeemed twice. The full history of earned and redeemed rewards kept. Administrators can adjust stamps and reward statuses by hand. Kept in step with the existing Apple Wallet loyalty system where technically supported, with no duplicate rewards and no different balances. Progress shown as five circles with the count and the stamps still needed, and the dates of the qualifying visits. One stamp per day, no duplicate stamps for one visit, and a free-hour reward at five stamps.
    - **What exists:** stamps are not stored. They are worked out from completed bookings, one per local day (`src/lib/domain/loyalty.ts`), so one visit cannot give two stamps. Five stamps give a free-hour code, which is emailed (`src/server/loyalty.ts`). Each code has a unique reference, an issue date, an expiry, and a status worked out from its uses, its expiry and whether it was cancelled. A code is spent once, inside the booking that uses it. A code used on a booking that is later cancelled becomes usable again.
    - **Conflicts and proposed solutions:**
        - *Stamps need completed visits.* The scheduled task marks visits completed, and nothing calls that task yet (see known gaps). Proposed: set up the scheduler before this is built.
        - *Manual corrections have nowhere to live,* because stamps are calculated, not stored. Proposed: a stamp and reward ledger with one row for each stamp, reward, redemption and manual change, recording who made it, when and why. Balances are sums of the ledger. Existing rewards are copied in from the bookings and codes.
        - *Redemption date and appointment.* The booking records the code it used, but no redemption date of its own. Proposed: the ledger records it.
        - *Canceled and Expired stay visible.* The customer's list hides spent, expired and cancelled codes today. Proposed: show the whole history with each status.
        - *Undoing a redemption.* An administrator can cancel a code but cannot undo a mistaken redemption. Proposed: a restore action that needs a reason and goes into the ledger. Nothing is edited silently.
        - *More than one use for a free hour.* The Codes page accepts several uses for a free hour. Proposed: a reward is always one use.
        - *Apple Wallet.* Nothing in the code or here describes that system. Passes are issued by whoever holds the pass certificate, so the system's operator decides what is possible. Options: (a) this site is the source and pushes stamps and rewards to it; (b) the wallet system is the source and this site reads it; (c) no link, and the owner reconciles from a report each week. Duplicates are prevented by using each reward's ID as the key on both sides.
        - *Colours.* The spec's example uses its own colours. Proposed: the site's brand colours for the progress circles.
    - **Open:** the name of the Apple Wallet system, who runs it and whether it has an interface; option (a), (b) or (c); whether a code returned by a cancelled booking is Available again (current rule) or stays Canceled, and whether an administrator may restore a redeemed code: both decided 2026-11-17 as built (question 15).

18. **Client dashboard** (spec section 6) — **Built (2026-11-15).** `/account` opens on the next appointment (with "Details and changes" and "View all appointments"), the stamps towards a free hour, the rewards and credit that can be used now (each with Copy Code), a shortcut to receipts, and two buttons, "Book a studio" and "Book a photographer". The menu of the account (Overview, My Appointments, My Rewards, Receipts & Payments) is on every account page. Two buttons were built, as proposed; one is a small change if the owner prefers it.
    - **Asked:** after sign-in, one clean mobile page with the next appointment, a button to view all appointments, loyalty stamp progress, available reward codes, a shortcut to receipts and payments, and a button to book another studio session.
    - **What exists:** /account lists all upcoming bookings, the codes, a loyalty line and sign-out. The studio and photography booking pages exist.
    - **Conflicts and proposed solutions:** the account page has no "next appointment" card: add one above the list, with the full list under "View all appointments". "Book another studio session" is ambiguous now that photography sessions exist: proposed, two buttons, "Book a studio" and "Book a photographer".
    - **Open:** two buttons, or one.

19. **Admin controls and data security** (spec section 7) — **Built in part (2026-11-15).** Built: the customers list and each customer's page for the owners, with the corrections. Not built, each an open question below: changing a customer's email, merging two accounts, and a log of who looked at what.
    - **How it is built:** Admin, Customers lists everyone who has booked, newest booking first, with a search by a part of the name or the email. A customer's page has their contact details and whether they have ever signed in; their stamps (the circles, how many came from visits and how many were added or taken by hand, and the visits that gave them); a form to add or take away stamps, which needs a reason; every reward with its status, the appointment it was redeemed for, and a button to cancel an available one or give a cancelled one back, each with a reason; their credit and discount codes with what became of each; their upcoming and past appointments, each a link to its page; and their payments by year, with a PDF receipt for each booking and one file for the year. Every correction is written to `loyalty_ledger` with the owner's address, the time and the reason, and listed on the page. Stamps added that complete a free hour send it at once. On an appointment's page the client's name leads here. When an owner makes a booking for a client, the client's reward or credit code can be entered, and it is used up as at checkout. An attendant cannot open any of it, as a page or as a file, whatever the owners have switched on for attendants. A manager can when the owners allow it (requirement 28), and then sees money only with the prices switch.
    - **Asked:** administrators see each customer's full booking history, payments and receipts, loyalty stamps and codes; correct loyalty balances; see whether a reward was redeemed; help a client with account problems. Clients see only their own appointments, payments and rewards. Earlier records are linked only after the account's ownership is verified. Financial and personal information is protected by authentication and access control.
    - **What exists:** the owner sees bookings, codes and the calendar. Customer pages check the signed-in email against each booking. Staff see neither prices nor contact details unless switched on. Sign-in codes and sessions are stored only as hashes. There is no customer page for administrators, no account tools and no record of corrections.
    - **Conflicts and proposed solutions:**
        - *Email is the account key* for bookings, codes and loyalty, so a client who changes email would lose their history. Proposed: a verified email change that moves every record and is logged; or only the owner can change it. The owner decides.
        - *Duplicate accounts* (one person, two emails). Proposed: an owner-only merge, logged.
        - *Access log.* Proposed: every administrator view of a customer's personal or payment data, and every correction, logged with who and when.
        - *Earlier records* stay hidden until the owner of that email signs in with a code.
        - *Staff* never see customer payments or receipts. This is today's rule, and it stays.
        - *Encryption and hosting* depend on the hosting choice, which is open.
        - *Privacy policy.* Needed before accounts hold receipts and earlier history. The page is not built: its text was drafted for the owners' lawyer on 2026-11-21, and the page waits for the approved text.
    - **Open:** whether clients can change their email themselves; whether duplicate accounts are merged or prevented; whether an access log is wanted.

20. **Testing the client portal** (spec section 8) — **Done for what is built (2026-11-15).** (1) sign-up and sign-in: the existing sign-in tests, and every browser test signs in. (3) upcoming and past bookings: `src/server/account.test.ts` and the account browser test. (4) receipts matching payments, also after prices change: `src/server/receipts.test.ts`. (5) annual downloads: the same file, and the browser test fetches both PDF files. (6, 7) stamps and a reward at five: `src/lib/domain/loyalty.test.ts`, `src/server/loyalty.test.ts`. (8) a redeemed code is not reusable: `src/server/rewards.test.ts` and the free-hour browser test. (10) desktop and mobile: the account browser tests, one of them on a phone at 375px and 320px. The owners' side: `src/server/customers.test.ts`, `src/server/rewards.test.ts` and the customers browser test. Not testable yet: (2) earlier appointments, which are not imported, and (9) the Apple Wallet balance.
    - **Asked:** ten checks: sign-up and sign-in; secure access to earlier appointments; upcoming and past bookings shown correctly; receipts matching payments; annual receipt downloads; stamps calculated correctly; a reward after five stamps; redeemed codes not reusable; balances consistent with the existing loyalty system; the whole portal smooth on desktop and mobile.
    - **How they can be tested:** checks 1, 3, 4, 6, 7, 8 and 10 with automated tests, extending the existing suites (checks 7 and 8 already have tests of the code rules). Checks 2 and 5 once earlier history is imported and receipts are PDFs. Check 9 only once the Apple Wallet system is known and has an interface; until then, balances can be compared with the owner's report by hand. Mobile checks go into `e2e/z-mobile.spec.ts`.

**Order:** done on 2026-11-15: (1) the record of manual stamp and reward changes, (2) My Rewards, (3) the dashboard, (4) receipts and PDFs, without a tax line, (6) customer pages and corrections for the owners. Waiting for the owner: (5) earlier history, (7) the Apple Wallet link, a tax line on receipts, and the three open questions of requirement 19.

### Answers of 2026-11-15 to the open questions

The owner answered the questions on the same day. Each answer is recorded here, and the lines it changes above have been updated to match. Where a reading is not yet confirmed by the owner, it is marked **to confirm**. **Built on 2026-11-15, after the owner said to start** (see the decision log): answers 6, 9, 11, 12, 13, 14 (both parts) and 15. Not built: the weekend prices of answer 10, which the owner will send. Answers 1 to 5 and 16 are "later" and need nothing built now.

1. **Hosting — later.**
2. **Email service — decide later.**
3. **Payments — Square, in test mode for now.** Real keys when the owner says so.
4. **Scheduled tasks — the hosting service's own scheduler,** once a host is chosen.
5. **A card charged with no booking — decide later.** Settled since 2026-11-16 without a decision: a booking's card is reserved first and only charged once the booking is confirmed, so it can no longer happen.
6. **Cancellations — 14 days before the start, with the 5% fee kept.** **Built (2026-11-15).** The question was about cancellations in general, and option c was "14 days, 5% fee": studio rentals now have the same 14 days as photography sessions (`refundWindowHours` 336). Migration `0011_owner_answers` moved a stored 168 to 336. The policies page and the guest information page state it from the setting.
7. **A moved booking with a discount code — the credit includes the discount** (as built).
8. **Several studios — 20% for two, 22% for three** (as built).
9. **Free hours — one per booking** (as built), **valid 90 days** (changed from 60). **Built (2026-11-15):** `loyaltyValidDays` is 90. Free hours sent before keep the date their email gave.
10. **Weekend prices — higher, to be sent by the owner later.** The weekend days are not changed by this answer.
11. **Pets — allowed in Studio Dayglow, with a $40 fee that is not refunded.** If the pet is not brought, the fee becomes studio credit. **Built (2026-11-15)** on this reading, to confirm: the fee never goes back to a card. It comes back as a code for studio credit (source `pet_fee`, the customer's own, valid as long as a reschedule code) in two cases: by itself when the booking is cancelled early enough to be refunded, and when an owner presses "The pet did not come" on the appointment's page, which also works for a booking cancelled too late, at the owner's discretion. A booking cancelled too late returns nothing by itself. One booking returns its pet fee once.
12. **Photographer — sessions can be booked from two weeks ahead at the earliest; working hours 9 am to 5 pm; the last session starts at 4 pm for a one-hour session, so it ends by 5 pm.** **Built (2026-11-15).** To confirm: which days the hours apply to (every day, as built), and that "two weeks out" means the earliest booking date (as built).
13. **Photography sessions do not count towards loyalty.** **Built (2026-11-15).** A rental on the same day as a session still counts that day.
14. **Bookings made in the admin — a "mark as paid" record, and a payment link sent to the client through Square, if Square supports it.** **"Mark as paid" built (2026-11-15):** on the appointment's page an owner, or staff once the owners switch it on, records what was taken at the studio (cash, card on the studio's own reader, transfer, other), in one payment or in parts, never more than is owed. The client is emailed a confirmation. Owners can take back a record made by mistake and record a refund given by hand. **Payment link built (2026-11-15), and Square supports it:** "Email a payment link" on the same page has Square make a link for exactly what is owed, and the client is emailed a page of this site that leads to Square's payment page. When they have paid, the payment appears on the booking by itself, as a card payment that can be refunded like any other. Tried in Square's sandbox the same day: a link was made, paid in the sandbox's test page, found paid by the site's own code, refunded, and another withdrawn.
15. **Staff see customers' phone numbers and emails by default.** The switch stays in the admin. **Built (2026-11-15):** the default is on, and migration `0011_owner_answers` switched a stored "off" on.
16. **Testing on the owner's iPhone — later.**

Still open, from the batch the owner has not answered: newsletter sign-up, memberships and their prices, food and drinks, the names of the lights, and editing a studio's name in the admin (memberships, food and drinks and the lights were answered on 2026-11-17). Whether the tester agent should re-check everything was answered on 2026-11-16: yes, it goes over everything again. Two items of that batch no longer need an answer: the owners now add the studios' photos themselves (requirement 26) and the social links themselves (requirement 25).

### Fourth set of requirements from the owner (received 2026-11-15): the admin website editor (CMS)

Sent by the owner on 2026-11-15, after the answers above. Recorded as requirements 21 to 28, one for each of sections 1 to 8 of the message. The owner's reference points are Squarespace and Webflow. **Section 9, "Important Requirements", followed on the same day; it is requirement 29.** **Nothing is built, and no work starts until the owner says so.** Terms: the **editor** is the admin area for website content; a **draft** is a change the public cannot see yet; a **version** is a published copy kept for restoring.

21. **Global design and fonts** (spec section 1) — **Built (2026-11-15).** The owners' own fonts came with the media library the same day. Horizon itself is not on the site: its files and licence are the owners' to supply.
    - **How it is built:** Admin, Website, Design. Nine colours (accent, accent under the pointer, pale accent, words on the accent, page, cards, headings and dark parts, running text, lines). Four fonts (headings, subheadings, running text, buttons), each chosen from a library of 25 that the site serves itself, Bebas Neue among them. For each of three levels of heading: the size on a computer and on a phone, the weight, the letter spacing, the line height and capitals. For running text: the size on a computer and on a phone, weight, line height, letter spacing. For buttons: text size, weight, letter spacing, capitals, rounded corners and the space around the words. And the space above and below sections, on a computer and on a phone. The website itself sits beside the form, at five screen widths, and shows each change the moment it is made. A list says how well each kind of text can be read on what is behind it; it advises and never refuses. The site as built has two low ones: the tan accent as a link colour, and white words on a tan button. A new colour, "words on the accent", lets the owners fix the second. Colours, fonts and buttons apply everywhere, the booking steps and the admin included. Sizes, weights and spacing of text apply to the public pages only, so that no choice here can break a booking screen. Every value is a colour, a font of the library or a number inside a range, so a design cannot carry anything but a design into a page.
    - **A font of the owners' own:** Admin, Website, "Your fonts". A font is added with its name, its kind and the owners' own words on the licence they hold, with a box to tick that the licence allows a website. Then its files: WOFF2, WOFF, TTF or OTF, one for each weight and style, or one file for a range of weights (a variable font). A file is checked to be a font by how it begins. The font can then be chosen in the design like a font of the library, and is shown in itself on that page. A font the design uses, published or in a draft, cannot be deleted.
    - **Asked:** a Website Design Settings section: primary and secondary fonts, chosen from a library or uploaded as licensed files; separate fonts for headings, subheadings, buttons and body text; sizes, weights, letter spacing and line spacing; text and background colours; global brand colours; button style, corner radius and spacing; all saved as global styles. The owner's brand fonts are **Horizon** and **Bebas Neue**, to be assigned to text styles and changeable later.
    - **What exists:** colours and fonts are CSS variables at the top of `globals.css`. The two fonts in use (Cormorant Garamond and Lato) are loaded at build time in `layout.tsx`, so changing a font needs a code change today.
    - **Conflicts and proposed solutions:**
        - *Changes need a code change today.* Proposed: keep the design in the database and generate the stylesheet from it. Publishing refreshes the page cache, as catalog changes already do.
        - *Horizon is not a font the site has.* It needs a web licence the owner holds. Proposed: the owner supplies the web font files and the licence. *Bebas Neue* is, as far as I know, free for web use under an open licence; to confirm when building.
        - *Uploaded fonts* must be licensed for web use. The editor records the licence each one was given.
        - *Readability.* The editor warns when text and background contrast is too low, and when text is smaller than the site's rules (11 px minimum; 16 px in fields on phones).
    - **Open:** the Horizon files and licence, which the owners add themselves once they have them; whether the font library holds only fonts the owner approves, or any free font.

22. **Page management** (spec section 2) — **Built (2026-11-16)** for pages made in the admin. Since 2026-11-16 the public pages that were written in code (home, studios, rates, photography, guest information, policies, contact) are pages of this kind too (requirement 29).
    - **How it is built:** Admin, Website, Pages. A new page has a name and an address (made from the name unless one is typed) and starts as a draft, with a heading and a line of text. The list shows each page's address and state (a draft, on the website, or on the website with changes not published), and copies a page as a draft at an address of its own, as a template for another. In a page's editor: its name and address, which change at once (when a published page moves, its old address leads to the new one); its title and description for search engines, and whether search engines are asked not to list it, which are part of the draft; taking it off the website, which keeps it as a draft; deleting it, to a trash where it waits 30 days and can be brought back as a draft. "Old addresses" lists every address that is sent on, the three of the live site that are known to differ among them, and an owner adds or removes one. Visitors find a published page at its address; an address that is no page answers "not found". The sitemap lists the public pages, the made ones that are not hidden included. A page is in the menu only when the menu's editor links to it. Since 2026-11-16, before a page that is on the website is taken off it or deleted, its editor says what links to it: the menu and its dropdowns, the footer, the announcement bar (as published and as drafted) and the other pages.
    - **Asked:** create, edit, duplicate, hide, publish, unpublish and delete pages without a developer; rename; change URLs and SEO settings; reorder the menu; dropdowns and subpages; hide a page from the menu while its direct link still works; duplicate a page as a template; preview before publishing; delete only after confirmation.
    - **What exists:** every page is code. Pages that take bookings, payments or accounts (booking, booking pages with their secret link, account, photography flow, admin, staff, API) must stay code. The text pages are hand-written, and some show numbers from the settings (guest information, rates).
    - **Conflicts and proposed solutions:**
        - *New pages without code.* Proposed: content pages stored in the database and shown by one generic route, with a fixed list of addresses the editor cannot take (the code's own pages).
        - *Numbers on text pages.* The policy text must keep showing the settings' numbers. Proposed: "live value" tokens in the text, such as the refund window, so a page cannot disagree with the settings. This also ends the hand-written "change both together" rule for those pages.
        - *Changing a URL* breaks links and search results. Proposed: an automatic redirect from the old address.
        - *Deleting a page* that the menu links to. Proposed: warn first; a deleted page goes to a trash kept for a set time.
    - **Open:** how long the trash keeps a page: 30 days is the builder's placeholder.

23. **Visual drag-and-drop editor** (spec section 3) — **Decided (2026-11-15): an existing open-source editor, embedded in this site's own admin. Built (2026-11-16)** with Puck 0.24.0.
    - **How it is built:** the editor of a page (Admin, Website, Pages, then a page) has the blocks on the left, sorted into Layout, Text, and Pictures and video; the page in the middle, drawn as visitors will see it, at the width of a computer, a tablet or a phone; and the fields of the block that is pressed on the right. Blocks are dragged onto the page and around it, copied and deleted; undo and redo are Puck's. The blocks: Section (background, width, space above and below), Columns (two to four, their widths, the gap, how they stack on a phone), Space, Line, Heading (typed on the page itself), Text (formatted, typed on the page), Buttons (words, link and look), Cards, Questions and answers, Picture (from the library: shape, what stays in view, size, corners, caption, link), Gallery (chosen pictures or a collection, columns, shape), Video (YouTube or Vimeo, played only when pressed) and Table (two to four columns, up to 40 rows). Since 2026-11-16 also Reviews (what clients wrote, with their name, where they wrote it and their stars) and Logos (the brands the studio has worked for, from the library). Since 2026-11-16 a fourth group, "From the studio's data", has the blocks of requirement 29's content inventory. The site's bar above the editor saves the draft by itself, previews it on the website, publishes it and discards changes, and the page's history brings an earlier version back. Not built: sections reused across pages, and padding and margins set by hand (sections and columns offer set choices instead). The editor is made for a computer or a tablet; on a phone it works, with little room.
    - **To confirm:** what a page holds is the site's own description of its blocks, and Puck is only the screen for editing it, so another editor could replace Puck without touching a page; formatted text is stored as HTML and cleaned by the site; videos only from YouTube and Vimeo.
    - **Asked:** add, remove, reorder and drag sections; add text, images, videos, buttons, galleries and other blocks; padding, margins, alignment and spacing; multi-column layouts; section backgrounds; button text and destinations; galleries; reusable sections across pages; all with a live preview.
    - **What exists:** no editor and no block system. Page layouts are fixed in code.
    - **Conflicts and proposed solutions:**
        - *The largest item in this set.* A visual editor with dragging, undo, live preview and reusable sections is a product of its own. Option (a): build it into this site, with a block model, the editor and a renderer for each block. We own it, but it takes a long time. Option (b): adopt an existing visual content platform and connect the site to it. A usable editor comes sooner, with a running cost, and the content lives in that platform on its terms. The owner chooses after a short comparison.
        - *Videos* only from an approved list of providers. The editor never runs custom scripts.
        - *Text* only in the allowed styles, never raw HTML, so layouts and accessibility hold.
    - **Decided (2026-11-15).** Asked whether the editor is built into this site or an existing platform is used, the owner answered: "I assume, to integrate is much easier? if so, let's do it this way." It is: writing a drag-and-drop editor from nothing is the long way, and integrating an existing one is much easier. So the editor is **Puck** (`@puckeditor/core`, MIT licence, actively maintained), an open-source visual editor made to be embedded in a React application. The drag-and-drop canvas, the panel of blocks and the device previews are Puck's. The blocks themselves, the pages, drafts and versions, the media library and the permissions are this site's, in its own admin and its own database. That takes no account, no monthly fee and no content on anyone else's servers, and it is what requirement 29 asks for: "integrated directly into our custom booking and management system". **The alternative, should the owner prefer it:** a hosted visual platform such as Builder.io or Storyblok. Its editor is more polished from the first day, but it needs an account and a monthly fee, keeps the website's content on the platform's servers, and is a second place to sign in. The builder did not choose it because the owner has agreed to none of those three.

24. **Desktop and mobile editing** (spec section 4) — **Built (2026-11-16), guard rails included.**
    - **How it is built:** the page editor shows a page at four widths: computer (1280), tablet (768), phone (390) and small phone (320). A block's values that matter on a phone have a second field, "… on a phone", whose first choice is "As on a computer": a section's space above and below, a heading's and the buttons' alignment, the height of a space, how many cards and gallery pictures sit side by side, a picture's shape and what stays in view when it is cropped. Columns choose to stack on a phone, stack with the last first, or stay side by side. The computer's value is the fallback, so a value for phones never changes a computer. The design's text sizes and section spacing have their values for phones in the design editor (requirement 21).
    - **The guard rails (built 2026-11-16):** before a page is published, its draft is measured as visitors would get it, at 320px and 390px, with the phone tests' own checks: sideways scrolling, controls under 40px, field text under 16px, text under 11px. A page that fails is not published, and the editor names what failed and at which width. Since 2026-11-16 the design and the header, menu and footer are checked as well, on every published page, the booking screens and the account, because their changes reach every page. Not checked: the widths of a tablet.
    - **Asked:** previews at desktop, tablet and several phone sizes; separate mobile font sizes, spacing, image cropping and layouts; mobile changes must not break desktop; responsive throughout.
    - **What exists:** responsive styles, and the mobile test suite (`e2e/z-mobile.spec.ts`), which checks every public page on eight sizes.
    - **Conflicts and proposed solutions:**
        - *Separate settings per device.* Each block keeps a desktop value and optional tablet and mobile values. The desktop value is the fallback, so a mobile change cannot alter desktop.
        - *Guard rails.* The editor refuses a publish that breaks the mobile checks: sideways scrolling, tap targets under 40 px, field text under 16 px, text under 11 px. The same checks run on every edited page.
        - *Previews* use the same rendering as the public site.
    - **Open:** the list of preview sizes (the owner's phones).

25. **Navigation, header and footer** (spec section 5) — **Built (2026-11-15).** A logo that is a picture came with the media library the same day: chosen from the library in the same editor, shown in the header in place of the logo's text, which stays in the footer and is what is read out for the picture.
    - **How it is built:** Admin, Website, "Header, menu & footer". The studio's name, address, phone, map link and the line "By appointment only". The logo, as two pieces of text. Where the menu sits in the header: on the right, or in the middle. The menu's items, each a link or a dropdown of links, added, removed and put in order. The booking button's words and look; where it leads is fixed. An announcement bar: a message, an optional link, an optional first and last day, one of two colours; a visitor can close it. The footer: the words beside the logo, columns of links, the heading above the address, the last line. Social links: none are set, so none are shown. The site's title and description for search engines. The contact details are the same ones every email and every receipt prints. A menu the owners make longer than the designer's folds behind the Menu button on any screen it does not fit, because the header measures itself. A link can only be a page of this site, a web address, an email link or a phone link; anything else is left out, and the editor says so beside the field.
    - **Asked:** the logo, the main menu and dropdowns, the header layout, the booking buttons, the footer, social links, contact information and announcement bars.
    - **What exists:** the logo is text, not an image. The menu, dropdowns and footer are fixed in code (`SiteNav.tsx`, `SiteFooter.tsx`). The address, phone and map link are constants in `src/lib/site.ts`; the opening hours come from the admin. The social links point at Squarespace accounts. The old site's announcement-bar styles are still in the stylesheet, but no page shows one.
    - **Conflicts and proposed solutions:**
        - *Contact details in code.* Proposed: the address, phone and map link move into the settings, read by every page and email that uses them.
        - *Social links* stay as they are until the owner gives the real ones (in the open batch).
        - *Booking buttons.* The editor changes their text and colour. Where they link is fixed, so checkout cannot be broken.
        - *Announcement bar.* Proposed: one message, optional dates and a link, switched on or off.
        - *Logo.* The owner may want an image logo; that needs the media library (requirement 26).
    - **Open:** nothing that needs an answer: the owners type the real social links and choose a picture for the logo themselves.

26. **Content and images** (spec section 6) — **Built (2026-11-15), except videos and what belongs to the pages**: galleries on a page, decor photos beside text, and the order of photos on a page come with the page editor (requirements 22 and 23).
    - **How it is built:** Admin, Website, Pictures. Pictures are added by choosing or dropping them, several at once; each line says how it went. A photograph is made smaller in the browser before it is sent, and prepared again on the server: turned the right way up, made no larger than 2560 pixels, and saved without what the camera wrote into it (where and when it was taken). JPEG, PNG, WebP and AVIF, up to 15 MB each. Each picture is described once, there: what it shows (the alt text), and the words under it in a gallery. The library can be searched and narrowed to pictures without a description or not shown anywhere, and says for each picture where it is shown. "Replace the file" puts a new file in a picture's place: every page that shows it shows the new file at once, and nobody edits a page. A picture that is shown somewhere cannot be deleted, and the library says where. **Collections** are named sets of pictures in an order the owners choose, with an optional first and last day: this is how "seasonal collections" was read. **Studio photos**: under Studios & add-ons each studio has photos chosen from the library, put in order; the first is on the studio's card and all are on the studios page, in place of the tinted boxes. A picture must be described before it can be a studio's photo. The pictures a visitor gets are resized and re-encoded for their screen by the framework. Files are kept in the database, behind an interface, until a host is chosen.
    - **Not built:** videos. A video would be embedded from a video service (the proposal below), which comes with the page editor's video block.
    - **Asked:** a media library to upload, organise, replace and delete images and videos; update studio photos and decor photos; seasonal collections; gallery descriptions; reorder photos; alt text; replace an image without rebuilding the page.
    - **What exists:** no image storage and no upload. Studio photos are placeholders (tinted boxes), listed as a known gap. The hosting is not chosen, and the storage depends on it.
    - **Conflicts and proposed solutions:**
        - *Storage* follows the hosting choice. Videos are large and go through a video service rather than being stored with the site.
        - *Image handling:* resizing and modern formats by the framework's image optimisation; upload size limits; alt text required before an image can be published, an accessibility rule the editor enforces.
        - *Replacing an image* keeps its address, so every page that uses it updates without a rebuild.
        - *Deleting an image in use.* The library shows where it is used and refuses the delete until it is removed from those pages.
        - *Seasonal collections* are not defined. Proposed: a named set of photos with optional start and end dates, shown while those dates apply.
    - **Open:** where the files live once a host is chosen (they are in the database until then). Whether "seasonal collections" means what was built: yes, the owners answered on 2026-11-20 ("use the existing admin collection tools"), and it was checked on 2026-11-21.

27. **Drafts, publishing and version history** (spec section 7) — **Built (2026-11-15) for the design and for the header, menu and footer.** Pages get the same when they exist.
    - **How it is built:** a change is saved as a draft by itself, a moment after it is made, and by "Save draft". Nothing reaches visitors until "Publish", which shows on the website within seconds. "Preview" opens the real website with the drafts in place, for the signed-in editor only, with a bar that says it is a preview. "Discard changes" asks once, then throws the draft away. The history lists every published version with who published it and when; "Bring back version N" puts that version into the draft, to look at and then publish or discard, and "Start again from the site as it was built" does the same with the original. When two people, or two windows, edit the same thing, the second to save is told who changed it and chooses between loading that version and saving over it. Decided as proposed: prices, hours, booking rules and money terms stay immediate.
    - **Asked:** save drafts; preview before publishing; Publish and Discard Changes; automatic saving; a history of published versions; restore an earlier version.
    - **What exists:** the admin's changes take effect at once (prices, hours, settings). There are no drafts and no version history for the website. Booking and status-label histories exist.
    - **Conflicts and proposed solutions:**
        - *Which changes wait for a publish.* Proposed: website content and design (requirements 21 to 26) are drafts. Prices, hours, booking rules and money terms stay immediate, as they are today, with a change history. Otherwise a draft could leave a price or a term different from what customers are told.
        - *Versions.* Each publish keeps a full copy of the published content. Restoring copies an old version into the draft; nothing is lost.
        - *Autosave* into the draft, with a warning when two people edit the same page.
        - *Previews of a draft* are for signed-in administrators only. Visitors never see a draft.
    - **Open:** confirm which changes are drafts.

28. **Admin permissions** (spec section 8) — **Built (2026-11-15), for appointments and clients, and for the part of the website that can be edited so far.** Two switches for managers came with the website editor, both off: change the design and the header, menu and footer as a draft; and publish, which includes bringing a version back. With the first alone a manager prepares drafts and an owner publishes them. An attendant never can. A third came with the pages (2026-11-16), off: add and edit pages as a draft. Publishing a page, taking one offline, and deleting or moving a published page need the publish switch as well.
    - **How it is built:** three levels. **Owners** (the `OWNER_EMAILS` list, which the admin cannot change) have everything. A **Studio Manager** (a new role, `manager`) always sees every day with its contact details, changes status labels, moves a booking, makes a booking for a client and sets the photographer's time. Six switches under Staff, "What Studio Managers can do", decide the rest: see prices, payments and receipts (off); record a payment and email a payment link (on); cancel a booking and refund a card, in the bookings list (off); open the customers list and a customer's page (on); correct loyalty stamps and rewards (off); block time in a studio (on). The defaults are the builder's: what runs the day is on, and what moves money back, changes a client's rewards or shows the studio's income is off until the owners switch it on. A **Studio Attendant** is the role that was called staff, with its eight switches unchanged. The owners add a person as either, and change a person's level, on the Staff page; it applies at that person's next request, without signing them out. A manager's menu shows only the pages they can open; every other admin page answers "not found". A manager without the prices switch sees a customer's page with no amount on it, and no payment or receipt. Returning a pet fee, taking back a recorded payment, recording a refund given by hand, the photography packages, and everything about prices, codes, staff and settings stay with the owners, whatever is switched on.
    - **Asked:** three levels. **Owner / Super Admin:** everything. **Studio Manager:** appointments, clients and selected website content, by permission. **Studio Attendant:** the calendar, check-ins and appointment statuses, with no design, pricing or critical settings.
    - **What exists:** two roles, owner (the `OWNER_EMAILS` list) and staff, with seven switches under Staff that decide what staff can see and do. Staff already have the calendar, labels and the day sheet, and no prices or settings by default.
    - **Conflicts and proposed solutions:**
        - *A third level.* Studio Manager needs a role between owner and staff, with a permission for each area: appointments, clients, and each website section. Proposed: a manager role that the owner sets in the database, with permissions as switches, like the staff ones.
        - *Studio Attendant* is today's staff role. Proposed: the same role, named "Studio Attendant" in the admin.
        - *Owners* stay in the `OWNER_EMAILS` list, which the admin cannot change. Proposed: keep it that way.
        - *A log of changes* made by managers and attendants, with who and when, as in requirement 19.
    - **Decided (2026-11-17), question 16:** the nine switches and their defaults, as built. Whether managers may see prices is one of them, off until the owners switch it on.

29. **Important requirements** (spec section 9) — **Built (2026-11-16).** Rule 6 is checked for pages, and since 2026-11-16 for a change of the design and of the header, menu and footer. Met so far: publishing changes the website (rule 1, on the local server until there is a host); the design, the header, the menu, the footer and the contact details are editable and no longer in the code (rules 2 and 3, in part); design changes cannot reach the sizes of the booking screens (rule 5); the header stays usable with any menu (rule 6, in part). Since 2026-11-16: new pages are made, edited and published from the admin, with their pictures (rules 2 and 3, for those pages); a sitemap, and redirects for moved pages and for the live site's known old addresses (rule 4). Since 2026-11-16 as well, the first part of the content inventory: blocks that show the studio's data (the studios, their prices, the add-ons, the address, phone and hours, the contact form) and live values in a page's text, so that a page made in the admin states the settings' numbers and the prices as they are, and follows a change in the admin at once (rule 3, for numbers and prices). Since 2026-11-16 too, the content inventory itself: the home, studios, rates, photography, guest information, policies and contact pages are pages of the admin, made of those blocks with the wording they had, and their code is gone (rules 2 and 3). What showed only at times in the code (the question about moving, the pet rules, the photography booking, the several-studios offer, how to get in) shows only at times on the pages, by conditions the owners can see and change. Their addresses, titles and redirects are kept (rule 4). What stays in code is what the owner named: the booking and payment steps with their messages and the card form, and the account and the admin, which are screens rather than pages. Since 2026-11-16 as well, the guard rails of requirement 24: a page that would break on a phone is not published (rule 6, for pages). To confirm: that the emails stay in code (the builder's reading of "all other text").
    - **Asked:** seven rules: (1) publishing changes the live website; (2) all website content stays editable; (3) nothing an administrator should update is hardcoded: text, prices, images or page layouts; (4) existing pages and SEO information are kept through every update; (5) design changes never interfere with the booking system; (6) the site stays responsive after edits; (7) the admin interface is clear and intuitive. The goal: complete creative and administrative control, like Squarespace, built into our own booking and management system.
    - **What exists:**
        - There is no live site yet. The site is not hosted, so changes are seen only on the local server, and nothing is published anywhere.
        - Static pages refresh about once a minute, and catalog changes call a cache refresh at once (`CATALOG_TAG`). Publishing can use the same mechanism.
        - Text is typed into the page files in many places. Two amounts are typed into the policies page: "a minimum $70 cleaning fee", which is not a setting anywhere, and "a $40 non-refundable pet fee". The services page lists its four packages (length, outfits, pictures) in the code; their prices come from the database.
        - The site has no photographs and no image tags. The studio pictures are coloured boxes, chosen by CSS class.
        - Twenty-six pages set their own metadata (title and, where set, description) in the code. There is no sitemap and no robots file.
        - Four live pages were saved on 2026-11-15: home, contact, guest information and services. Their live addresses include `/guestinfo` and `/services-2-1`; this site uses `/faq` and `/services`. Other live addresses (for example `/rates-1`, `/blanche-1`, `/studio-memberships`) have no redirect yet. As of 2026-11-21, on the hosted preview: the live sitemap lists 18 pages; `/` and `/contact` answer as they are; `/guestinfo`, `/rates-1`, `/services-2-1` and `/studio-memberships` are sent on (308) to `/faq`, `/rates`, `/services` and `/memberships`; and twelve answer 404: `/appointments`, `/blanche-1`, `/dayglow-1`, `/paris`, `/membership`, `/home-1`, `/christmas-2026-dayglow`, `/christmas-2026-paris`, `/styled-setups`, `/events`, `/store` and `/privacy-policy`. Where each leads is the owners' to say (point 14 of the preview test); an owner adds one under Admin, Website, Pages, "Old addresses".
    - **Conflicts and proposed solutions:**
        - *"Actually modify the live website".* There is no live site yet. Proposed: publishing writes to the database and refreshes the cache at once, so the public site changes within seconds once a host runs the database. Until a host is chosen (answer 1), "live" means the local server, and this rule cannot be met in full.
        - *"All content editable" against text in code.* Proposed: a content inventory, page by page, agreed with the owner. Stays in code: the booking and payment screens' steps and error messages, the card form, and the wording of emails unless the owner asks for them to be editable. Legal wording is editable, with its version history and a reminder that the owner should review it.
        - *"Nothing hardcoded".* Proposed: move the public pages' text, images and layouts into content records, in stages, most-edited pages first (home, studios, rates, services, guest information, contact). Prices appear through live values, read from the price tables. Layouts become blocks (requirement 23). This is most of the work of the editor.
        - *An amount that is only text.* The "$70 cleaning fee" has no setting behind it. Proposed: a setting, so the policies page and any booking rule agree. The owner confirms that $70 is still the fee.
        - *"Preserve existing pages and SEO".* Proposed: each existing page becomes a content page with its current title, description and address. Every changed address gets a redirect; for example `/guestinfo` to `/faq`, `/services-2-1` to `/services`, `/rates-1` to `/rates`. The live pages' titles and descriptions are exported from the live site before launch, since only four are saved. A sitemap is generated. Admin and booking pages stay out of search, as now.
        - *"Design must not interfere with booking".* Proposed: the booking, account, admin and staff screens keep their fixed design. The editor changes only the shared design values (colours, fonts, corner radius, spacing) that those screens read. Before any publish, the booking and mobile browser tests run against the draft, and publishing is refused if they fail.
        - *"Responsive after edits".* Proposed: the per-device settings and mobile checks of requirement 24, run on every draft.
        - *"Clear, intuitive admin".* Proposed: one "Website" area in the admin, with Pages, Design, Navigation and footer, Media and Versions. A live preview beside the editing form on a desktop, stacked on a phone. Save draft, Publish and Discard always visible, with plain words for what each does.
        - *"Complete control, built into our system".* This leans towards building the editor into this site (requirement 23, option a). The boundary to confirm: presentation and content are fully editable; booking rules, prices, money terms and the booking flow stay in the admin's existing pages.
    - **Decided (2026-11-15), the owner's answers:** (1) *What stays in code:* the steps of the booking and payment screens, their error messages and the card form. "All other text should be able to change from admin side." (2) *The $70 cleaning fee* is "not a setting, just a text": it stays a sentence of the policies page, editable with the rest of that page's text. (3) *Build or adopt:* see requirement 23. **Decided (2026-11-17), question 33:** the emails are the owners' to edit too: an admin section of templates where they create, edit and customise every automated email. Built on 2026-11-18: Admin → Emails (see "Answers of 2026-11-17").

**Order (the owner said to start on 2026-11-15):** (0) the content inventory and the rules of requirement 29: agreed, see its last line; (1) build or adopt: decided, see requirement 23; hosting is still open, and the media library is built against a storage interface until it is; (2) the manager role and permissions (28): done; (3) drafts and history (27): done for the first two documents; (4) design settings and navigation (21, 25): done; (5) page management (22): done, for pages made in the admin; (6) the media library (26): done, before the pages, because a page needs pictures; (7) the visual editor and the device settings (23, 24): done, except the mobile guard rails. (8) the content inventory (29): done (2026-11-16), first the blocks for the studio's data and the live values, then the seven public pages made again from them. (9) the guard rails (24): done (2026-11-16), for pages. Every item of the fourth set that needs nothing from the owner is built; what is left waits for their answers. The mobile guard rails (24) go in before anyone other than the owner uses the editor.

### Defaults chosen while building (placeholders until the owner decides)

All of these are data or settings, not code, unless noted.

- **Payment at booking** — the full amount is charged when booking, as on the live site. The prototype's 30% deposit option was dropped.
- **Durations** — only the durations on the live price list can be booked (for example Paris stops at 5 hours).
- **Notice and horizon** — bookings open 24 hours to 180 days ahead (`minLeadHours`, `maxAdvanceDays`). The live site asks for 24 hours' notice. A photography session needs two weeks (`photoMinLeadHours`, the owner's answer 12, confirmed 2026-11-17; how the two weeks count is question 17 of the list of 2026-11-17). A booking that is too soon is refused with that sentence, and the booking screen does not offer the dates.
- **Start times and the gap** — the owner's rule since 2026-11-15, no longer a placeholder: start times every 15 minutes (`slotIncrementMinutes`), and 15 minutes kept free after every booking in every studio (each studio's `bufferMinutes`, under "Studios & add-ons"), which is 7 minutes before and 7 after each appointment. Migration `0007_start_times_and_gap` moved the earlier defaults (30 and 10) to these. A booking made before keeps the 10 minutes held for it.
- **Included guests** — Blanche 6, Dayglow 6, Paris 4. Shown as information; not enforced.
- **Add-ons** — "1 to 2 extra people" $30 and "3 to 5 extra people" $50 for any studio, pet fee $40 for bookings that include Dayglow. The live site varies the extra-people tiers by studio. **Decided (2026-11-17), question 18, built 2026-11-18 (follow-up 2):** $5 for each additional person, in every studio, as one add-on taken in a quantity (five people, $25). What makes an add-on a pet fee is its kind, not its name: the refund rule of answer 11 applies to every add-on of kind `pet`.
- **Cancelling and moving** — a rental is refunded when cancelled 168 hours (7 days) or more ahead, a photography session 336 hours (14 days), both less a 5% fee (question 8, 2026-11-17, built the same day; answer 6 of 2026-11-15 had given rentals 14 days too) (`refundWindowHours`, `photoRefundWindowHours`, `refundFeePercent`); a reschedule code can be requested until 24 hours before the start (`rescheduleCutoffHours`), once free of charge (`maxReschedules`) and then for $20 each time (`rescheduleFeeCents`, follow-up 6), and is valid 90 days (`creditValidDays`). The first two follow the live terms; the last three were guesses, confirmed on 2026-11-17 (question 9). **Superseded in part 2026-11-20 (to build):** a client moves a booking online once; later moves are made by the studio, at $20 each ("Owner decisions of 2026-11-20"). Today a client can go on moving a booking online after the free move, $20 each time (`rescheduleOutcome` in `src/lib/domain/policy.ts`).
- **A code on a cancelled booking** (2026-11-15) — a percent code gets its use back, and an amount code the studio gave out gets its amount back, whoever cancels. A reschedule credit comes back only when the studio cancels before the visit starts. A customer who cancels a moved booking gets nothing back, as the terms say.
- **Add-ons once per booking** (2026-11-15) — the admin's "Most per booking" field was removed: the booking screen only ever offered a tick box. **Superseded (2026-11-17), not built yet:** an add-on can be taken in a quantity (extra people, question 18).
- **Loyalty** — one free hour for every 5 visit days, valid 90 days (answer 9; it was 60); a visit counts 2 hours after it ends (`loyaltyVisits`, `loyaltyValidDays`, `loyaltyDelayHours`, switched by `loyaltyEnabled`). One free hour per booking, because checkout takes one code. The rule itself is the owner's (requirement 2); only the one-per-booking limit is a guess. Switched off and on again, it starts over (decided and built 2026-11-17): `switchLoyalty` (settings.ts) keeps the moment in the setting `loyaltyStartedAt`, and stamps, the changes made by hand and the free hours owed are counted from it. Free hours sent before it stay valid and keep their numbers; the next one takes the number after them.
- **Staff** — see customers' phone numbers and emails by default (answer 15, 2026-11-15; the switch stays in Settings). They see the days the owner assigns, and not only today.
- **Sign-in** — codes last 10 minutes, lock after 5 wrong tries, at most 3 per address in 15 minutes; sessions last 30 days. These are constants in `src/server/auth.ts`, not settings.
- **Owners** — the addresses in the `OWNER_EMAILS` environment variable, and nobody else.
- **Policies page** — the live rental terms, with every number a live value: the refund windows and fee, how late a booking can move, the notice for a photography session, the pet fee and where pets may come; what depends on a switch (moving, pets, photography) follows its condition. The house rules are text the owners edit, and so is the "$70 cleaning fee", which is not a setting. A page of the admin since 2026-11-16.
- **Guest information page** (`/faq`) — follows the live site's guest information page, adapted to what this site does (automatic refunds, reschedule codes, two ways to book). Every number that is a setting or a price is a live value, so it follows the admin. Whether larger groups and pets can be booked, and in which studios, follows the kind of each add-on on offer, through conditions. The house rules are text the owners edit, here and on the policies page: change both together. A page of the admin since 2026-11-16.
- **Contact page** — address, phone and opening hours (read from the catalog) and a message form. At most 5 messages in 10 minutes per address. No copy goes to the visitor. Topics: studio rental, after-hours booking, photography session, membership, my booking, something else. The page shows no email address, as on the live site.
- **Menu** — "Contact" was added, so the links need about 960px. The menu switches to its compact form at 1000px and below (it was 768px).
- **Services page** (`/services`) — describes the photography sessions as the live page does: what is included, outfits and edited pictures by session length, delivery in 7 to 14 days. It does not name the photographer. It gives the lowest price and the notice, and leads to the photography booking; while no session can be booked, it asks for a message instead (the condition `photography`). The live page's Christmas mini sessions were left out. A page of the admin since 2026-11-16.
- **Not carried over yet** — seasonal-setup notices and seasonal mini sessions, the waitlist, and the privacy policy page (drafted for a lawyer's review on 2026-11-21; the page waits for the approved text). Memberships are built (follow-up 3, 2026-11-18).

### Policies and facts to confirm with the owner

- **Cancellation window** — **Decided (2026-11-17), question 8: 7 days before the start for studio rentals, 14 for photography sessions,** as the live terms say; later, nothing is refunded. It supersedes the reading of 2026-11-15 (answer 6: 14 days for both). Built 2026-11-17 (`refundWindowHours` 168, migration `0035_refund_window_and_food`).
- **Refund amount** — **Decided (2026-11-15): the 5% processing fee is kept (answer 6).** Confirmed on 2026-11-17 for rentals and photography (question 8).
- **Pets** — **Decided (2026-11-15): allowed in Studio Dayglow, up to 2 pets, $40 fee (answer 11).** This follows the live terms, not the live guest page's "no pets" line.
- **Membership prices** — **Decided (2026-11-17), question 24:** the prices charged at checkout today, Dayglow $330 and $600 a month, Paris $300 and $550; memberships of both studios are sold on the new site (see "Answers of 2026-11-17"). The live memberships page showed Dayglow at $280 and $520, and Paris at $270 and $500 as "Coming soon".
- **Newsletter** — **Decided in part (2026-11-20): a sign-up section is added; its service, and where sign-ups go, are chosen later.** The live site's sign-up form has no storage configured, so sign-ups appear not to be saved.
- **Social links** — **For the owners to set.** The live site's footer icons link to Squarespace's own accounts, not the studio's. Here the owners set the studio's own under Admin, Website, "Header, menu & footer" (`src/lib/site/frame.ts`); none is set by default.
- **Photography sessions** — **Built (2026-11-15): bookable online, see requirement 12.** The packages and prices were taken from the live price list. The live page spells the photographer's name two ways; this site does not name the photographer.
- **Door access code** — **Built (2026-11-15); the text is Open.** The live guest information says the confirmation email includes the access code for the building door. The owner now types how to get in under Settings, "Getting in". Customers with a confirmed booking get that text in the confirmation email and see it on their booking page until their visit ends (the end time is checked since 2026-11-15: before, the link of a visit long over still showed the current text). Public pages only say that the email explains it, and only once the text is set. It is empty until the owner fills it in. After a change, customers whose visit is still ahead see the new text on their booking page but keep the old email. Needs: the text itself. **Decided (2026-11-17), question 29:** sent with the confirmation right after booking (as built); a reminder 24 hours before the appointment, and a request for a review after it, are to be built. Both were built since (question 38 of the list of 2026-11-17, and follow-up 7); the text is still the owners' to write.
- **A discount on a moved booking** — **Decided (2026-11-15): the credit includes the discount (answer 7).** The discount code stays used up; checkout takes one code.
- **Terms that change after a booking is made** — **Decided (2026-11-17), question 10: a booking keeps the terms it was made under;** changed terms apply only to bookings made afterwards. Built 2026-11-18 (see "Terms that change later" above).
- **Food and drinks** — **Decided (2026-11-17), question 30: not allowed inside the studios,** as the live guest information says. Built 2026-11-17: the policies and the guest information page say so, in their starting content (`built-in-pages.ts`) and, through migration `0035_refund_window_and_food`, in a database that still had "not permitted on furniture".
- **Pet fee on a refund** — **Decided and built:** the fee becomes studio credit when an eligible cancellation or manual return applies. See follow-up 4 for the owner's manual workflow.
- **Names of the lights** — **Decided (2026-11-17), question 31: "Amaran 150, Godox and Neewer",** as the new page says. The live page lists them as "Amaram 150, Godos, and Neewer".
- **Sales tax on receipts** — **Awaiting implementation and accountant confirmation (requirement 16).** The owner requested tax-exclusive prices and a separate line at checkout and on receipts, with 6.5% provisional. See the November 20 decisions.
- **Apple Wallet loyalty system** — **Decided 2026-11-20: not used (requirement 17).** The site's own loyalty program takes its place once it is verified, and a progress email when a completed visit earns a stamp is to be built ("Owner decisions of 2026-11-20").

### Known gaps in what is built

Not connected to the outside world yet:

- **Square is verified in its sandbox only.** Through the site's own code: a charge, the same charge sent twice, four kinds of refused card, partial and full refunds, a refund read back while pending, an amount reserved then taken (and taking it twice), an amount reserved then given back (and giving it back twice), and a declined card refused when reserving (2026-11-16: Square accepted it as PENDING); a payment link made, asked for twice, read while unpaid, withdrawn, and read once paid. In a browser: Square's card form, a refused card, a paid booking and its refund; and the sandbox's test page for a payment link, where a link was paid and its payment then refunded. Never done: a payment with production keys, and Square's real payment page for a link, which the sandbox replaces with a test page. The browser tests still use the pretend processor, so the card form is checked by hand, not by a test that runs every time.
- **Email needs both Resend settings on the host.** Without `RESEND_API_KEY` and `EMAIL_FROM` a production server sends nothing, sign-in codes included, so nobody could sign in. The hosted preview has had both since 2026-11-19, and sends from the studio's own domain since 2026-11-20 (the decision log). The production host needs them too. `EMAIL_FROM` must be a mailbox someone reads: the site's emails to clients set no reply address.
- **The scheduled tasks run once a day, not every few minutes.** On the hosted preview Vercel calls `GET /api/cron/tick` (with `CRON_SECRET`) at 12:00 UTC (`web/vercel.json`), the most its Hobby plan allows; every five minutes needs Vercel Pro (step 10 of `HANDOFF.md`). Vercel starts it at 12:31 UTC, not on the hour (2026-11-20 and 2026-11-21). Between runs the admin overview and `GET /api/health` say the tasks are late, and reminders, free hours, review requests and refund retries wait for the next run. So a reminder set to 24 hours goes out at about 7:30 AM Eastern on the day of the visit, since the run of the day before comes more than 24 hours ahead of any visit after that time of day. A payment link that was paid is still recorded without it, when the client is brought back to their page or an administrator presses "Check whether it was paid"; a client who closes Square's page before being brought back is noticed only by the scheduled task.
- **No production database yet.** Vercel and Neon were accepted (2026-11-18) and the domain stays; the hosted preview runs on Vercel with Neon's staging database and Vercel's scheduler, as the owner chose (answer 4, 2026-11-15). Production's own Neon project, Vercel Pro and the domain's move come at the cutover (`HANDOFF.md`, steps 8 to 10).
- **The client portal's history from before this site** comes from Acuity's export, through the history import (2026-11-20), once it is run: it has not run on any shared database yet. Square's payment history (refunds, exact amounts) is not imported. Sign-in is still the emailed code only: no password, no Google.
- Two addresses of one person cannot be joined, and an owner cannot change a client's address for them (a member's, for one, which the client is sent to the studio for). The owner decided on 2026-11-18 (question 27) that two accounts of one person are merged if the effort is small. It is not small (stamps, free hours and memberships of both would have to be joined), and on 2026-11-21 the owner put it off: "Merging accounts is not an issue rn". The other two answers were built on 2026-11-21: a client changes their own address (question 58), and who opens a customer's page is logged (question 57).
- Receipts show no tax line, and the yearly PDF is a summary of payments, not a tax document. Decided 2026-11-20, to build: prices exclude tax, shown as a line of its own at checkout and on receipts, at 6.5% until the accountant confirms the rate and what it applies to. There is no privacy policy page yet: one is to be drafted for a lawyer's review (2026-11-20).
- Videos come from YouTube and Vimeo only (`src/lib/site/video.ts`). Instagram, which the owners named as a third source (question 22, 2026-11-18), is not built.

Known weaknesses:

- **Bookings, payments, clients and their history are kept with no end.** Since 2026-11-21 the daily task deletes email copies, sign-in codes and ended sessions older than the owners' retention period (5 years, Settings → Records), as `pruneRateLimits` deletes the rate limits' counts. Nothing deletes a client or a booking: a client who asks to be forgotten is a job done by hand in the database today.
- **The bank's check runs before the server checks the booking** (the review of 2026-11-20). The token, and the bank's check with it, are made on the page, and the server then checks the time, the price and the code. A time taken meanwhile, or a price changed, sends the client back, and the next try asks the bank again. The bank asks for a check only now and then for US cards, and such a clash is rare; checking with the server before asking the bank would cost every booking a request.
- Conditions follow the switches of the site (pets, moving, photography, free hours, getting in, several studios, add-ons). A number set to 0 is not one of them: with a refund fee of 0 the policies would say "(0%)", and the owners reword the sentence.
- A link cannot name a live value. The guest information's "Open in Maps" link and the studios page's "7457 Aloma Ave" are text: if the studio moves, change them with the header and footer.
- A page the website was built with and then deleted waits in the trash for good, so that it does not come back by itself.
- Puck is pinned at 0.24.0, and the site relies on facts read from its code (see "The page editor (Puck)" under Conventions): check them again before upgrading it.
- The page editor's undo history is Puck's, and is lost when the page is opened again.
- The trash of pages is emptied by the scheduled task, which nothing calls yet: until something does, deleted pages stay in the trash.
- All 25 fonts of the library are declared in every page's stylesheet (about 5 KB more to download, compressed), and no font is preloaded, because which fonts are in use is only known when a page is served. A visitor's first page therefore shows its text in a fallback font for a moment.
- An announcement with dates appears and goes within a few minutes of midnight on the studio's clock, not on the second: the published frame is cached and worked out again every few minutes.
- A preview belongs to the browser. Until "Leave the preview", signing out or closing the browser, that browser sees the drafts on the public pages too, under the bar that says so.
- The readability list compares six pairs of the design's colours. It knows nothing about words placed over a photograph.
- Two people in one editor are noticed when the second saves. Nothing shows that someone else has it open.
- An announcement a visitor closed shows again for an instant each time a page is loaded as a new document, until the page has started. Moving between pages inside the site does not show it.

- A full address of the site under a name the host also gives it, which is neither `SITE_URL` nor the address the owner is using when they add it, is not known for the site's own: an old address could lead to it and send visitors round.
- A cancellation's email that has already left when its card refund fails is followed by a second email that puts it right; the first still said the refund was on its way.
- When the page the editor is drawn again with after a publish does not arrive (the connection drops, or the server answers with an error), Next.js loads the page anew and "Published" is not said: the editor's status line then says that there is nothing to publish.
- A word broken in two is checked by the phone tests, not yet by the page editor's guard rails.
- The check for words broken in two does not model a width given to a cell, a column or an inline-block: where such a width squeezes a word, it reports a word that could not have fitted. It fails a test, not a page, and asks for a look.
- The contact form has no CAPTCHA. A hidden field that only programs fill in, and the limit of 5 messages in 10 minutes, are its only protection against automated spam.
- `POST /api/bookings` is rate-limited to 10 requests a minute per address, in memory. The contact form's limit works the same way. That is per server process, keyed by the `x-forwarded-for` header (everyone shares one bucket when it is missing), and nothing else is limited by address.
- Which kind of money a database holds is checked when it is opened. A server left running in test mode on the live database (a demo, staging, an old deployment) books test bookings into it: the live site's overview then shows them, and its next start refuses the database until `npm run db:clear-test-mode` removes them.
- With no card processor set up, the first booking of a database counts as real on the live site (`NODE_ENV` is production) and as test mode's anywhere else. A staging site run as production, with no processor and an empty database, would count its bookings as real: give it the pretend processor (`PAYMENTS_MODE=mock`).
- The clear keeps a free hour that visits of test mode helped to earn, and a code a test booking issued that a real booking used: it names both for the owners to cancel. Cash recorded for a booking of test mode goes with that booking, whichever site recorded it: the listing names the bookings that go.
- A booking whose reserved money could not be taken stands unpaid until the scheduled task takes it, and nothing calls that task yet: after 24 hours Square gives the amount back: the booking then owes its price, and the owner, who was emailed, collects it at the studio and records it, or cancels the booking. A booking cancelled while its money was only reserved gives it back in full, whatever the cancellation terms say.
- A refund Square rejects after accepting it is noticed by the scheduled task, which nothing calls yet. When a failed refund is retried, Square answers with the same rejected refund, because the refund's id is its idempotency key: the owner refunds such a payment by hand in Square, and records it on the admin's overview ("Refunded it in Square", `recordRefundMadeInSquare` in manage.ts, for a refund that failed only): the site then counts it as made and stops trying.
- The getting-in text reaches anyone who books. A booking made far ahead and cancelled in time costs only the 5% fee, and nothing when the fee is 0. Sending the text shortly before the visit, not at booking, would close this; the live site sends it at booking.
- An admin form the server refuses comes back with the stored values. The browser's checks stop the usual causes first (see "Conventions and gotchas"). Only the contact form hands typed values back.
- Two add-ons can have the same name (a pet fee for each of two studios). A booking with both lists the name twice, at two prices.
- A membership whose buying stopped before any answer about the card is given up an hour later, and the studio asked to look in Square for a payment: the site cannot ask Square about a payment it never heard of. Square's records carry the membership's code as the payment's reference.
- The pretend processor keeps nothing, so in local development a cancelled membership's subscription still "bills" as days pass; only the database knows it was cancelled, and the monthly check takes no month from its `ends_on` on.
- The development build sometimes reports a script error of React's own performance timeline when a signed-in customer or staff member opens an owner page ("Failed to execute 'measure' on 'Performance': '... [Prerender]' cannot have a negative time stamp"): the tester saw it on 2026-11-15, and it failed the staff test once on 2026-11-16. It is not the site's code, and a production build has no such timeline, so the browser tests leave this one message out (`frameworkNoise` in e2e/helpers.ts); any other error still fails a test.
- The development server has logged "Worker terminated due to reaching memory limit: JS heap out of memory" during the tester's hour-long session, and near the end of each full browser run since the follow-up 5 work (2026-11-18), after about eleven minutes of compiling every page. Each time it kept answering, and every test passed. Cause unknown; the browser tests run `next dev`, never a production build. If it starts failing tests (on CI above all), find which worker it is.
- A visit counts towards a free hour once it is completed. If staff mark it a no-show later than `loyaltyDelayHours` after it ended, a free hour it helped to earn has already been sent. The owner can cancel that code in `/admin/codes`; no second hour is sent for the same visits.
- The owner can edit each studio's tagline, description, guest number and clean-up time, and the add-ons, under "Studios & add-ons" (2026-11-15). A studio's photos are chosen there too, from the media library (2026-11-15), and its facts and floor plan are written there (2026-11-16). Since 2026-11-21 the owners rename a studio and take one off the site and back (question 59). Adding a studio is not built yet: the owners decided on 2026-11-18 that they do it without a deployment (question 29), and it needs their rules for a fourth studio first (`HANDOFF.md`). The Flask prototype's admin had a calendar, reports, a customer list, announcements and an iCal feed; none of them were carried over.
- A payment taken at the studio can be recorded only up to what the booking owes. Money taken for something else (overtime, the cleaning fee, damage) is recorded once it is added as an extra charge on the appointment (built 2026-11-18, follow-up 13): the charge raises what is owed, and the payment is recorded against it.
- Owners can return an unused pet fee manually under Admin → Codes with the booking reference. The appointment links to that form for eligible bookings and shows the issued code after the return.
- Square refuses to make a payment link when it does not like the buyer's email address (in the sandbox it refused an example.com address), so the site does not send Square the client's email with a link. The site learns that a link was paid by asking Square, not by being told (no webhook is set up). Square's documentation does not say whether a link expires by itself; an open link stays open here until it is paid or cancelled.
- There is one photographer, and no page to add a second or to rename them: that is a row of the `photographers` table. The site books the first active one. No second photographer is planned (2026-11-17, question 27).
- A studio shows a tinted box until the owners give it photos (Studios & add-ons). No real photograph has been uploaded yet: the tests use pictures of one colour.
- Uploaded pictures and fonts are kept in the database. That works anywhere and needs nothing set up, and it is not where a few hundred photographs should stay: a host's own file storage should take them over (`fileStore()` in `src/server/files.ts`).
- A photograph in Apple's HEIC format cannot be read: it is refused with a sentence that says which formats can. An iPhone sends JPEG to a web page by itself unless it is told otherwise.
