- Where a picture is used is found by looking for its id in the text of every document, which is right as long as the documents are few and small.
- The framework keeps the resized copies of a picture for as long as the picture's address is valid, and there is no way to clear them. A replaced picture gets a new address, so this never shows an old picture; the old copies only take room until the host clears its cache.
- The browser tests run with the mock processor: against the development server, and in WebKit against a production build too (CI's `webkit-production`, since 2026-11-20).
- In a production build the two booking pages log Next.js's warning "Unexpected cache miss after cache warming phase during prerendering" while a customer goes through them (about 160 times in a run of the browser suite), for `getCatalog()` at the top of each page. Next.js then draws that part when the page is asked for (its own comment in `use-cache-wrapper.js`), so nothing breaks; a plain prefetch of `/book` does not log it. Not understood yet: `NEXT_PRIVATE_DEBUG_CACHE=1` makes the server print the cache's decisions.
- **A page drawn while an owner's save is on its way can be cached with the old values.** Seen twice, in CI's production build (2026-11-21, `webkit-production` on `97a7efd` and on `ce33bfe`, the same test both times): the admin page's own prefetch of `/rates` left in the same tenth of a second as the save of a studio's guest number, read the studio before the save was committed, and its page went into the cache after the save's `updateTag(CATALOG_TAG)`. A visit 0.6 s later was served it in 10 ms: the new pet fee from the save before, the old guest number. It takes two saves a fraction of a second apart, which a test does and a person does not (the first save's screen is still prefetching the pages it links to), or a visitor's page drawn in the very instant of a save. The browser test now waits between its two saves (`requestsSettled`, "Conventions and gotchas"). Such a page stays until the cache's own minute passes (`cacheLife("minutes")`). What a booking costs is not touched: the server prices every booking itself and refuses a total that changed. Not fixed: whether the cache can refuse an entry begun before its tag's last update is to be found out.
- **The browser's Back button leaves the booking screen, and a refresh starts it again.** The steps are one page's state: nothing of them is in the address or kept in the browser, and closing the tab asks no question. Going forward again brings the step back (the browser's own memory of the page). Whether Back should go one step back is with the owners (the preview test of 2026-11-21, point 12).
- Mobile was checked in Chromium set to phone sizes. Safari on an iPhone lays some things out differently (date and time fields above all), and nothing was tried on a real phone, a real foldable or with a screen reader.
- A receipt after a refund that was partly the studio's goodwill lists the refund and what is still owed, but does not say which part was the gift. The site does not record what a payment was for: it reads what came in as paying the price first, then the charges (question 48). A booking's price summary says how it was paid and what it cost ("Paid by card and at the studio $276"), and keeps saying so after a goodwill refund: the refund shows on the receipt and the appointment's payment card, not in that line (seen by the tester on 2026-11-18, outside the change it tested).
- Repricing a moved booking (follow-up 5): when a move lowers the price of a booking whose card amount is still only reserved (taking it had not gone through yet), the whole reserved amount is taken later, and the difference shows as Remaining credit only then; the client's email about the move does not mention it. Credit that goes back on its code keeps the code's expiry date, even one already past: the owners issue new credit by hand under Codes if the client needs it.
- The link to a card payment opens Square's receipt of it, not the payment in Square's Dashboard, for which Square documents no address. Payments of memberships (Admin → Memberships) have no such link yet.
- The accessibility scan leaves out colour contrast and Puck's own canvas in the page editor. Since 2026-11-16 the pages that need something chosen first (a booking, an appointment, a customer, a receipt, a payment link, a collection, the page editor around the canvas) are scanned where the other tests make them, with something in them. Nothing was tried with a real screen reader. The tan accent falls short of the contrast WCAG asks for as the colour of links and under white words on a button; changing it is the owners' choice.
- A cancellation or move that waits for Square (question 54) is asked about again by the scheduled task, which runs once a day on the Hobby plan: until Vercel Pro, it is finished sooner only by an owner's "Check" or "Close it by hand", or the client's "Try again now". The browser suite does not reach it, since its pretend processor always answers; the unit and integration tests and `ManageBooking.test.tsx` cover it. Nor is it listed on the admin's overview: the owners' email and the appointment's page say it waits.

### Decision log

- 2026-11-14: Stack and the eight requirements proposed.
- 2026-11-14: Language and framework decided: TypeScript, Next.js (React), PostgreSQL.
- 2026-11-14: Owner said to start the rebuild. Node.js installed with nvm. New app scaffolded in `web/` on branch `rebuild`.
- 2026-11-14: First milestone built: data model, prices by weekday and weekend, availability, booking any set of studios, both booking flows, payment through a provider interface. Requirements 1, 4 and 6 are built with placeholder numbers.
- 2026-11-14: All eight requirements built with placeholder settings. Passwordless sign-in, customer accounts, staff day sheet, loyalty codes, reschedule codes, automatic refunds and retry, email delivery via Resend, owner admin dashboard (bookings, prices, hours, staff, codes, settings). Rate limiting on the booking endpoint. Playwright e2e test suite. 210 unit/integration tests passing.
- 2026-11-15: Verified in a real browser: all 13 browser tests pass from a cold start, with any browser console error failing the test. Found and fixed on the way: the booking screen kept its finished selection after paying (it now loads the booking page as a new document); two processes sharing one build folder corrupted it and made every page reload in a loop (the browser tests now build into `.next-e2e`). Two Claude Code sessions worked in this checkout at the same time that night; the three commits `8a1ab9e`, `54035c1` and `b1de4cd` came from the other one.
- 2026-11-15: The owner asked to delete everything old and keep only what the new site needs. Removed from the repository: the Flask prototype (`app/`, `migrations/`, `run.py`, `config.py`, `seed.py`, `requirements.txt`, the root `.env.example`) and its README, which a short one replaces. Removed from the development machine: the Python environment, the seed database (it held no bookings) and the old settings file. Kept from that settings file: its four Square sandbox lines, now in `web/.env.square-sandbox`. Git history was not rewritten, so the prototype can still be read at commit `f1420bc`.
- 2026-11-15: Added the guest information page (`/faq`) and the contact page with a message form (`/contact`), both following the live site. Queued email can now carry a reply address (migration `0004_email_reply_to`). The menu switches to its compact form at 1000px. Reading the live guest information raised four questions for the owner, listed under "Policies and facts to confirm": the door access code, food and drinks, the pet fee on a refund, and the names of the lights. 220 unit and integration tests and 15 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-15: Added the services page (`/services`). It describes the photography sessions and sends people to the contact page to ask for one, because photography cannot be booked online here yet. Whether it should be is a new question for the owner.
- 2026-11-15: First run against Square's sandbox, with the sandbox keys carried over from the prototype. The owner was asked whether to test with them and answered "keep going". `npm run check:square`: a charge, the same charge sent twice, four kinds of refused card, a partial and a full refund, a refund larger than the payment and a refund of an unknown payment all behaved as expected. In a browser: Square's card form appeared, a wrong security code was refused, a test card paid for a booking, and cancelling it refunded $64.60 through Square. Fixed on the way: a refused card showed Square's code instead of a sentence; requests to Square had no time limit and no retry; a charge with no answer told the customer the card was not charged. 239 unit and integration tests and 15 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-15: Added the getting-in text: the owner sets it in Settings, and customers with a confirmed booking get it by email and on their booking page. It is kept out of the public catalog because it can hold the door code. 245 unit and integration tests and 16 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-15: The scheduled task now notices a payment whose result was never recorded, which is what a server stopping in the middle of a charge leaves behind, and emails the owner to check it in Square.
- 2026-11-15: Added "Studios & add-ons" to the admin: the owner can change each studio's tagline, description, guest number and clean-up time, and add, change or stop offering add-ons. Bookings already made keep the add-on name and price they were made with. 254 unit and integration tests and 17 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-15: Added a tester agent (`.claude/agents/tester.md`) at the owner's request. So that it can work beside other sessions in this checkout, a second browser-test run can now use its own port and folders. Two runs side by side both passed all 17 tests.
- 2026-11-15: The tester's first pass. All four suites passed; by hand it found 16 defects, 12 of them in that day's work. Fixed, each with a test: a moved booking lost its discount (placeholder decision under "A discount on a moved booking"); the contact form refused a message its own box accepted and cut its end off, because line breaks were counted twice; a visit that was over still showed the getting-in text, including one set afterwards; the discount sentence promised three studios what only two get; "12,50" was saved as $1,250; an add-on withdrawn during checkout left the customer stuck; the admin offered a "most per booking" that the booking screen could not use (removed); guest information followed add-on names (add-ons now have a kind, migration `0005_addon_kind`); two add-ons with one name logged React errors; signing out left the owner's pages hidden in the document; a studio's paragraphs ran together; a NUL character caused a server error; the booking page gave wrong reasons when the refund window was not whole days and when moving was switched off; 15 minutes read "0.3 hours"; "1 days". Found by the builder on the way and fixed: an amount code from the studio was not given back on cancellation, and no code was given back when the studio cancelled. Not changed, and recorded above: admin forms lose typed text on a refusal (the browser's checks now stop the usual causes), terms that change after a booking is made, and the getting-in text for the price of the fee. 280 unit and integration tests and 18 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-15: The owner decided how loyalty works: a client who visits 5 times, on 5 separate days, gets 1 hour free at any studio, and the free hour is not applicable to any studio bundles. It replaces the placeholder (10% off after every visit), which is still what the site does. The rule, how it is read, what has to be built and what it leaves unanswered are under requirement 2. Nothing in the code was changed.
- 2026-11-15: The owner answered the builder's five questions about the loyalty rule (requirement 2). A free hour is valid 60 days. Every fifth visit earns one, and several can be collected before they are used. A visit paid for with a free hour does not count towards the next one. The one-hour price comes off: a one-hour booking is free and a 1.5-hour booking costs the remaining 30 minutes. A visit made as a bundle counts, at most one count a day. One point is still open: whether several collected hours can be used on one booking. The owner asked for no development until they have seen the site on their machine. Nothing in the code was changed.
- 2026-11-15: The owner sent a second set of requirements: an admin calendar with month, week and day views, appointment status labels with a history, 7-minute buffers with a 15-minute minimum gap and start times every 15 minutes, a photography booking flow, mobile design, and a list of tests. Recorded as requirements 9 to 14, with the owner's instructions on how to work on them. Nothing of it is built. Three of them conflict with what exists (statuses and rescheduling, buffers, bookings made by administrators), and the owner's own instruction is to propose a solution before changing anything.
- 2026-11-15: The owner looked at the site on their machine, said to keep developing, and had the work so far merged into `master` (pull request #5). The loyalty rule was then built (requirement 2): one free hour for every five visit days. A new kind of code, `free_hour` (migration `0006_loyalty_free_hour`), is sent by the scheduled task when a customer's fifth day is completed. The 10% code after every visit is gone, and the `loyaltyPercent` setting became `loyaltyVisits`. A booking takes one free hour, because checkout takes one code; whether several can be combined is still open. 309 unit and integration tests and 19 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-15: The owner answered the builder's proposals for the second set of requirements and said to start building once the answers were recorded. Buffers: 7 minutes before and after, so two appointments can be 15 minutes apart. Status labels, the administrator's "move to a new time" and bookings made by administrators: yes as proposed. Photography: the admin sets the photographer's availability and blocks their time, and a photography booking holds the photographer as a resource across all studios. The admin has two levels, owners and staff; the owners set what staff can do, without a redeploy; staff can change all the labels (requirement 15). A visit counts for loyalty from Completed, once per day.
- 2026-11-15: Built from the second set: start times every 15 minutes with a 15-minute gap (requirement 11), the status labels with their history (requirement 10) and the two admin levels with the owners' switches for staff (requirement 15). Still to build: the calendar with "move to a new time" and bookings made by administrators (9), photography (12), the mobile pass (13) and the owner's test list (14). 328 unit and integration tests and 20 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-15: The admin calendar was built (requirement 9): month, week and day, a colour for each studio, one studio or all, the buffers drawn, and every appointment a link to its page. With it came "move to a new time" for administrators and bookings made in the admin for a client, which finish requirements 10 and 11. Still to build from the second set: photography (12), the mobile pass (13) and the owner's test list (14). 347 unit and integration tests and 21 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-15: Photography was built (requirement 12): the section on the booking page, the photography flow, the packages of the live price list, the photographer as a resource that a session holds across all studios, and the admin page for their working hours and blocked time. Still to do from the second set: the mobile pass (13) and the owner's test list (14). 370 unit and integration tests and 22 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-15: The mobile pass (requirement 13) and the owner's test list (14). A new browser test file measures every page on eight screen sizes and makes whole bookings at four iPhone sizes; six defects it found were fixed. With that, every item of the second set of requirements (9 to 15) is built and has a test. Open: the photographer's real working hours, the points marked "to confirm" under requirements 2 and 12, and a check on real phones. 370 unit and integration tests and 31 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-15: The owner sent a third set of requirements, the client portal: accounts and appointments, receipts and payment history, rewards and loyalty stamps, a client dashboard, administrator controls and data security, and tests. Recorded as requirements 16 to 20, each with what exists, the conflicts and a proposed solution. Nothing is built, and the owner asked for no work on it yet. The spec's "photographers" were read as the clients who rent studios.
- 2026-11-15: The owner answered the 16 questions (see "Answers of 2026-11-15"), and the lines they change were updated. The owner sent a fourth set of requirements, the admin website editor, recorded as requirements 21 to 28; section 9 was missing from the message. Nothing is built, and the code has not changed. Also corrected: the staff switches number seven, not six.
- 2026-11-15: The owner sent section 9 of the editor spec: seven rules and a goal (publishing changes the live site, all content editable, nothing hardcoded, existing pages and SEO preserved, design never interferes with booking, responsive after edits, a clear admin). Recorded as requirement 29 with what exists, the conflicts and a proposed solution for each. Nothing is built, and the code has not changed.
- 2026-11-15: The owner said to start on what is recorded here while they think about the open questions. Built first, the answers that were decided: cancellations at 14 days for rentals too; free hours valid 90 days; photography sessions no longer count towards loyalty; the photographer works 9 am to 5 pm and is booked at least two weeks ahead; staff see contact details by default; the pet fee stays off every card refund and comes back as studio credit; and "mark as paid" for bookings made in the admin, with refunds given by hand. The policies page now reads its numbers from the settings. Migrations `0011_owner_answers` (stored values still at the old defaults) and `0012_pet_credit_and_manual_payments`, both tried on copies of older databases. 407 unit and integration tests and 32 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-15: The payment link of answer 14 was built, after checking that Square supports it. In Square's sandbox a link was made, paid on the sandbox's test page and refunded: its order goes from DRAFT to OPEN with one tender, whose payment is an ordinary one. The client is emailed a page of this site (`/pay/<id>`) that leads to Square's page and shows afterwards that the payment arrived. Migration `0013_payment_links`. Found on the way: Square refuses a link over an email address it does not like, so none is sent to it. 436 unit and integration tests and 32 browser tests pass; one browser test sends a link and pays it, in test mode, on a phone.
- 2026-11-15: The client portal was built as far as it needs no answer from the owner (requirements 16 to 18 and 20): the account's overview, My Appointments with upcoming and past and a filter, My Rewards with stamps, reward statuses and a Copy Code button, and Receipts & Payments with a receipt for each booking, a summary for each year and PDF files (`@cantoo/pdf-lib`). Stamps can be corrected by hand with a reason, and a reward cancelled or given back, through services that write `loyalty_ledger`; their admin screens are next. Each free hour is now numbered per customer, so a correction cannot send one twice (migration `0014_loyalty_ledger`). A free hour is always one use. 483 unit and integration tests and 34 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-15: The owners' side of the client portal was built (requirement 19, in part): Admin, Customers, with a page for each customer that has their appointments, their payments by year with PDF receipts, their stamps and their rewards. An owner corrects stamps and cancels or gives back a reward there, each with a reason that is recorded and shown. An owner can enter a client's reward or credit code when making a booking for them. Staff cannot open any of it. 494 unit and integration tests and 35 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-15: The owner answered the three questions about the editor. What stays in code is the booking and payment steps, their error messages and the card form; all other text is to be editable in the admin. The $70 cleaning fee is not a setting, just text. On build or adopt: "I assume, to integrate is much easier? if so, let's do it this way." It is much easier, so the editor will be an existing open-source one, Puck, embedded in this site's own admin, with the content in this site's database. A hosted platform stays the alternative if the owner prefers it; it was not chosen because it needs an account, a monthly fee and content on someone else's servers, none of which the owner has agreed to.
- 2026-11-15: The three admin levels of requirement 28 were built for appointments and clients: owners, Studio Managers (a new role, with six switches of their own) and Studio Attendants (the role that was called staff). `src/lib/staff-abilities.ts` became `src/lib/abilities.ts`. Found by the new browser test and fixed: the history of an appointment named amounts (a refund, a payment, a pet fee) to people who may not see prices; those notes now come without their amounts. Migration `0015_manager_role`. 513 unit and integration tests and 36 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-15: The first part of the website editor was built (requirements 21, 25 and 27, and the website part of 28): Admin, Website, with an editor for the design and one for the header, menu and footer. Each saves a draft by itself, shows the real website beside the form, publishes, discards, and keeps every published version to bring back. A preview is Next.js Draft Mode, for the signed-in editor only. The studio's name, address and phone moved out of the code into that content, and every email and receipt reads them from there. The fixed constants (`src/lib/site.ts`) are gone. Managers got two switches for it, both off. Found while looking at the result and fixed: the "Info" item sat indented in the phone menu, and a menu placed in the middle of the header folded when it would have fit. Found by the browser tests, in the full run only, and fixed: the design editor's preview rewrote an element of the previewed page, and when it did so before that page had started, React reported that the page differed from what the server sent. The preview now lays the design over the page as a stylesheet that belongs to no element. Migration `0016_website_content`. 566 unit and integration tests and 39 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-15: The media library was built (requirement 26), with the owners' own fonts (the rest of 21), a logo that is a picture (the rest of 25) and the studios' photos. Pictures are prepared with `sharp` when they arrive and served to each visitor at a size for their screen; a picture is named by its id everywhere, so replacing its file changes every page that shows it; one that is shown cannot be deleted. Files are kept in the database behind `FileStore` until a host is chosen. "Seasonal collections" was read as named sets of pictures with an optional first and last day. Migration `0017_media_library`. 603 unit and integration tests and 41 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-16: Pages made of blocks and the visual editor were built (requirements 22 to 24), with Puck 0.24.0 embedded in the admin. The blocks are described once by the site and turned into Puck's configuration; formatted text is cleaned on the server; the page store has drafts, versions, a trash and old addresses; the public route answers with real redirects and "not found"; there is a sitemap and a robots.txt; blocks have values for phones. Found by the browser tests and fixed: the address fields' pattern was invalid under the browser's `v` flag, and the picture chooser put a form inside Puck's. Migration `0018_pages_and_redirects`, tried on a copy of the local database. 659 unit and integration tests and 44 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-16: The first part of requirement 29's content inventory was built. A page can now show the studio's own data in blocks (the studios as cards or in full, the studios' prices, the add-ons, the address, phone and opening hours, the contact form), which read the admin's other pages, so they cannot disagree with what can be booked; and a table block. Its text can name live values in braces, such as `{refund window}`, which show the settings as they are now. The page editor lists them beside the page. Found while looking at the result and fixed: a block placed outside a section touched the edges of the page, and then shrank to the width of its words. 662 unit and integration tests and 45 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-16: Requirement 29's content inventory was finished. The home, studios, rates, photography, guest information, policies and contact pages are pages of the admin (Admin, Website, Pages), made of blocks with the wording the code pages had, and their code is gone. Every number in them is a live value, and what the code showed only at times (the question about moving, the pet rules, the photography booking, the several-studios offer, how to get in) shows only at times: every block has a "Show it" choice, and a paragraph, a list item or a question that starts with `{if pets}` shows only while pets can come. The pages are put into each database once (`ensureBuiltInPages`, migration `0019_built_in_pages`, tried on a copy of the local database); a page can start again from how it was built; the home page keeps its address and stays on the website. Two new blocks: a title band (a page's banner, or the home page's hero) and the address and map in a box. Wording changed where one sentence had to serve every setting: "A pet fee of $40 applies" (with each studio's fee when they differ), "The other studios and all common areas are pet-free", the policies' line on photography sessions, and two answers of the guest information whose last sentence depends on a switch now stand on their own. Found by the browser tests and fixed: the HTML cleaner read styles with a CSS parser that draws random numbers, which Next.js refuses while it prerenders a page; it now writes an alignment anew. 689 unit and integration tests and 46 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-16: The guard rails of requirement 24 were built. Before a page is published, the page editor draws its draft as visitors would get it, at 320px and at 390px, and measures it with the checks the phone tests use, which moved into the site's code so that both run the same rules (`src/lib/site/phone-checks.ts`). A page that would scroll sideways, have a control under 40px, a field with text under 16px or text under 11px is not published, and the editor says at which width and what. Found on the way: a check run in a frame out of sight sometimes measured the page before it had finished showing itself, and passed it; the frame is now drawn, transparent, behind the editor, and the check waits for the page. The browser tests now aim their drops through the editor's zoom when they drop into a column. 689 unit and integration tests and 47 browser tests pass.

- 2026-11-16: The tester's second pass, over all of the site at `87b0729` (47 minutes; Square's sandbox not used), found 9 defects, a browser test that failed at random, and 2 faults by reading the code. All were fixed the same day, each with a test. (1) Typing a date into the booking screen's date field with the keyboard crashed the page, because the field passes through dates such as 0002-12-28 while a year is typed; the screen now takes the date once it is a real one. (2) The pet fee could be given back twice: "The pet did not come" and then a reschedule code worth everything paid, or the owner's cancel offering the whole card amount. A fee returned as credit is now kept out of both. (3) A Table block that scrolls sideways on a phone could not be reached by keyboard; its frame is now a named region that takes focus. (4) Two old addresses could be pointed at each other, and `/x` at `/x?y=1`, which sent visitors round until the browser gave up; both are refused. (5) In a studio open overnight, the hour the clocks skip was offered, and booking it gave a server error or charged two hours for one; that hour is now taken time on that day, and the server refuses a time whose real length is not its booked length. (6) The Settings, Codes and Prices forms lacked the browser's checks for the server's ranges, so a value out of range was lost, and Settings named it by its internal key; the fields now carry the ranges, and the message names the field and its range. (7) A booking paid by card through a payment link read "Paid at the studio"; it now reads "Paid by card". (8) After the studio moved a booking at the client's request, the client was told the booking "was made with a reschedule code", and the email of the new time did not say that it would no longer be refunded; both now say what happened. (9) The first date the booking screen offered followed the device's time zone, not the studio's. (10) The browser test of a picture shaped for phones failed at random, because publishing measures the page on a phone first, which the development server can make slower than the test waited; the website tests now wait 60 seconds for a publish. From the code: (S1) money the scheduled task was taking while the customer cancelled would have been kept for a cancelled booking; it is now given back in full. (S2) A refund Square rejected after accepting it was shown on receipts as on its way; it is left out. Found while running the browser tests with another session loading the machine: a drag that started at the edge of the list of blocks, where the two new blocks had pushed "Contact form", made the editor scroll by itself, so `dragBlock` now puts the block in the middle of the list; and the accessibility scan could run in the moment after a form is sent, before the page's title is back, so it now waits for the title. Not covered by the tester: real phones, Safari and Firefox, a screen reader, Square, loyalty from completed visits, the retries after a processor's failure, the media library and the calendar by hand, and the day the clocks go back (beyond the booking horizon). 745 unit and integration tests and 53 browser tests pass.

- 2026-11-16: The tester's third pass, over what was built and fixed since its second (at `2a72582`), found 10 defects and 2 smaller things. All were fixed the same day, each with a test. (5) Old addresses could still go round: through a slash at the end, the site's own full address, a page that is only a draft, or a page later taken off the website. (6) A retry of the scheduled task that failed at the moment the owner recorded the refund as made in Square undid the record: a failure now changes only a refund still open. (7) With moving switched off, a move the studio made at the client's request still took the refund away, under a rule the policies no longer showed: it no longer counts, and the appointment page says so. (8) The design was checked on the home page and the booking page only, and buttons at the edges of the design's ranges broke other pages: every page of the sitemap and the account are checked. (9) The Codes and Prices forms still let through what the server refused, and Prices saved half a form: the value of a code follows its kind, an email needs a domain with a dot, a price form is saved whole or not at all with the field and the limit named, the discount fields have their range, and a whole number written "5.0" or "1e3" is read. (10) A picture's description could be taken away while a published page showed it: it cannot while the picture is shown anywhere. Smaller: a review of characters that take no room is no review; a refund recorded as made carries the day it was recorded. - 2026-11-16: The tester's sixth pass, over the fifth round of fixes (at `bd390c4`), found 8 defects, three smaller points and two races it suspected and could not bring about; the suites passed on its run. All were fixed the same day, each with a test. (1) In the page editor, the undo key changed the page during the phone check and after Publish, inert canvas or not, for Puck listens for it on the whole document: the change could be published unchecked, or lost. While the editor is busy the undo and redo keys are not passed on and no change is taken; and Publish sends the revision that was checked, so the server refuses a draft changed since, here or in another window. (2) On a phone, the booking summary broke words and amounts in two ("W/he/n", "$38/5", "-$227/.70"): its rows let either side shrink to a letter's width. They keep their words whole now, and the phone tests check every page and booking step for a word broken in two that would have fitted (`brokenWords`), with the longest summary at 320px. (3) When the studio cancelled a booking paid at the studio, nobody was told about that money: the owner's notice, the history and the client's email now name it, and the bookings list shows it beside the cancel form. (4) With a processing fee of 100%, a cancellation said the card had paid only a pet fee, or asked the studio to refund $0 by hand: the words now say the fee keeps it, and the owner is asked for nothing. (5) The booking screen, sign-in and the contact form did not follow the one email rule: they do, sign-in keeps what was typed, and the booking names a field in the screen's words, not "customer.email". (6) Moving a page dropped an old address's #section and ?query. (7) A page that already did not fit a phone kept every change of the design and of the header from being published, and the refusal blamed the change: each such page is measured again as visitors have it, and only what the change brings refuses it. (8) A whole number written with more than 40 characters passed the browser and not the server. Smaller: two empty lines in a row in a cancellation email; a booking paid at the studio was told its pet fee was not refunded "to your card". The races: a message put right just before delivery could go out as it was, and two attempts at one failing refund could tell the owner twice; neither can now. Not changed: when the page drawn again after a publish does not arrive, Next.js loads it anew and "Published" is not said (recorded under known weaknesses). 783 unit and integration tests and 56 browser tests pass.

- 2026-11-17: The tester's eighth pass, over the seventh round of fixes (at `4801ddc`), found 6 defects and 4 smaller points; one browser test failed once on its first run (a drag of the last block in the list) and passed on the second. - 2026-11-17: The tester's ninth pass, over the eighth round of fixes (at `c36ba86`), found 12 defects; the suites passed on its run. All were fixed, each with a test. (1) Once an owner cancelled a partly spent reschedule code, its booking's whole payment could be refunded again: a cancelled code limits the refund as one that can be spent does, so what was spent stays spent. (2) Migration 0024 filled in the wrong value for codes whose bookings had ended (released for a reschedule code, cancelled by the client of a moved booking, cancelled by the studio after the visit, or refunded off the code), so a pet fee could be refunded twice: it now works the value out from what became of each booking, and a test replays it on codes in every state. (3) The page a link leads to said "refunded in full" after any refund, a cancellation's part included: it says so only for a payment that arrived after the booking ended, and says when that refund has not reached the card yet. (4) The admin's buttons were squeezed to letters in tables ("Refunde/d it in/Square"): they wrap at words; and the check for broken words now measures a button's room by its container, and a table's cell by the table, so it sees a squeeze it missed. (5) A late payment's email and the admin's Check said "refunded" when the refund had failed. (6) The email for a refund off a reschedule code named credit left on a code that was cancelled, and was not put right when the card refund failed. (7) The fonts page was wider than a phone with a long one-word font name; the phone tests add one. (8) Two refusals named the wrong cause. (9) A link the processor could not answer about was withdrawn and marked cancelled, and a payment behind it would have been lost: it stays open until the processor can say. Found by the builder on the way: a booking whose hold ran out while its payment was unfinished (the server stopped while the card was answering) kept its code spent; it now gives it back, once, and a card that answers after the hold ran out is given back rather than confirmed. 819 unit and integration tests and 56 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-17: The tester's twelfth pass, over the eleventh round of fixes (at `814b98b`), found 5 defects and 3 smaller points; the suites passed on its run. All were fixed, each with a test. The phone tests now make such a page. (4) The overview's "Refunded it in Square" was still squeezed to letters in its table, being a summary, and the check missed it, being inside a details: words in a table's cells are not broken now, and the check climbs out of a cell from any depth. (5) Migration 0024 counted a refund that failed before a booking's release, and went through after it, as one taken off the reschedule code; migration 0025 sets a reschedule code's figure from the line in its booking's history that says what it was released for, which does no harm where 0024 was right. Smaller: the message to look again no longer names only a payment or a deadline; a booking attempt sent again after it made no booking says the card was not charged, as the first answer did; a page about a booking waits at most 4 seconds for the processor. 824 unit and integration tests and 56 browser tests pass.

- 2026-11-17: The tester's fourteenth pass, over the thirteenth round of fixes (at `a7fbc2f`), found 7 defects and 3 smaller points; the suites passed on its run. All were fixed, each with a test. (2) Last round's drawing of the client's page again after any refusal hid the refusal when the booking had ended meanwhile (the studio cancelled it while the client looked at it): such a refusal leaves the page as it was, and says that the booking ended meanwhile and nothing more was done; the box shows a refusal even when nothing is left to choose. - 2026-11-17: The tester's fifteenth pass, over the fourteenth round of fixes (at `3c4c94b`), found 5 defects and 5 smaller points; the suites passed on its run. All were fixed, each with a test, but for false alarms of the check for broken words that a width given to a cell or a box would raise (known weaknesses). (2) A link made with Square's sandbox keys stayed payable, and was asked about, after the live keys went in, both being "square": the sandbox is `square-sandbox` now, so its links are another processor's, and so are its payments: their refunds fail at once with what to do, and an amount only reserved there is left to lapse, instead of being sent to the live account, which does not know them. - 2026-11-17: The tester's sixteenth pass, over the fifteenth round of fixes (at `0d5f49f`), found 8 defects and 4 smaller points; the suites passed on its run. All were fixed, each with a test. (1) After a switch from test mode to the live keys, a payment taken in test mode still counted as money, and a reschedule code turned it into real credit that paid for a real booking: once the live processor is set up, what test mode left is retired when the database is opened, its payments voided and its links closed, and a booking that stands with nothing received owes its price and is listed on the overview. (2) Last round's marking of an amount another processor reserved as failed was for good, and could take a live reservation if the setting of Square's account was missing or wrong for one run of the scheduled task: what another processor made is now left untouched, and the account is read from the application id, the setting only allowed to agree. (3) The scheduled task asked the live account about the sandbox's refunds, for good, and fifty of them kept a live refund from being looked at: it asks only the processor set up now, and only about its own refunds; a refund of another processor's payment waits for it, and is no longer failed at once, which could have had an owner refund it by hand and then see it refunded again. (4) A link of another processor was "being checked" for good on the client's pages, and its page said it was cancelled: such a link is told apart (`foreign`), and the client is asked to say whether they paid it. (5) The check of what is wider than the screen named words inside a box made to scroll sideways. (6) The check for broken words missed a word reaching its box of a flex row through a table or an inline-block, and raised a false alarm beside cells with no row of their own; it no longer takes a box in a grid's track for one that could have had the row, which tracks set as a width given to a column does. (7) On a phone, the calendar's week list broke a long client name beside its status: the status goes below it, and the phone tests book a client with such a name. (8) On an iPad the touch rules seemed unmet: they apply on any touch screen, and a browser at that width without touch does not show them; the phone tests now measure a tablet with touch emulated. Smaller: the overview says when card payments go to Square's sandbox; the words "or with other keys" became "or with Square's other account". 844 unit and integration tests and 59 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-17: The tester's seventeenth pass, over the sixteenth round of fixes (at `34c7a0c`), found 7 defects and 4 smaller points; the suites passed on its run. All were fixed, each with a test. (1) Credit made from test money before the switch to the live keys stayed real credit afterwards (a reschedule code, a pet fee's credit, a free hour from test visits), because retiring what test mode left touched only its payments and links. The retirement was replaced: a database holds test money or live money, never both, and the site refuses to open one that would mix them with the processor set up now, saying what to do; `npm run db:clear-test-mode` removes test mode's bookings and everything they made, keeping what the owners set up. Retired payments, the overview's list of bookings paid in test mode and their wording went with it. (2) A reservation that lapsed before it was taken left the booking reading "Charged to card" with nothing to pay, and the studio could not record the money it collected: what a booking owes is now one rule, in which a lapsed reservation holds nothing, and the scheduled task marks it, writes it into the history and tells the owner. (3, 4) A payment link was refused for such a booking ("Nothing is owed"), and a booking paid at the studio read "Charged to card": the link follows the same rule, and the summary says where the money came from. (5) On a touch tablet wider than 1000px the menu's links, the logo and the booking button were under 40px: they are 44px there, and the tablet test also measures 1180px. (6) "The studio has been told" was untrue for a refund that waits for the right processor: it says why it waits, and the owner is emailed once. (7) With Square's setting disagreeing with its application id, the overview said no processor was set up: it gives the reason; and `.env.example` no longer sets the sandbox, which with live keys turned payments off. Smaller: a link while no processor is set up is not called another processor's; a refused move no longer promises a refund of a booking that stands; the check for broken words no longer gives a box in a grid's track the grid's width. 846 unit and integration tests and 59 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-17: The tester's eighteenth pass, over the seventeenth round of fixes (at `663c007`), found 6 defects and 9 smaller points; the suites passed on its run. All were fixed, each with a test. (1) The rule of one kind of money per database saw only card payments: `db:clear-test-mode` would have removed the bookings and cash of a database of the live account that had taken no card payment yet, without asking, and a pretend booking paid in pretend cash passed into the live account and became real credit. The database now says which kind of money it holds (`money_kind`, migration 0027, tried on copies of the local database and of the tester's), set by the processor that opens it; the live account refuses a database of test mode that still has bookings, and the clear refuses a database of the live account, and one never opened in test mode that has bookings. The clear first lists what it would remove (the bookings still to come by name) and keep, and removes it only with `-- --yes`; it keeps the contact form's messages and the owners' changes of stamps. (2) The owner was told a booking owed its price after it had been paid, when the studio collected it between the lapse of its card's reservation and the scheduled task: the task asks what the booking still owes first, and the client is told too. (3) On a touch screen wider than 1000px, the logo read "The VisualPhoto Studio" and the menu "Info▾": made flex boxes, they lost their spaces, which a gap puts back, in the phones' menu too. (4) With no card processor set up, the appointment's page called an open link another processor's, and said it could not be paid: it says card payments are off, and that a client with Square's page open could still pay. (5) `.env.example` and the README still described the retirement of test payments, and called `SQUARE_ENVIRONMENT` required. (6) A database refused when opened was opened anew by every call, and never closed (about 60 files and 80 MB more a request with PGlite): it is closed, and tried again after 10 seconds. Smaller: the refusals' words (a database of test mode with no payment, "test on a copy"); "1 bookings"; a refund waiting for its processor is no longer said to be retried, to the owner or to the client; an amount held on a card reads "Held on card", and the appointment's page shows it; a missed booking that owes reads "To pay"; a payment link is offered for a lapsed card only when links can be sent; a browser is no longer told why card payments are off; stale comments. 855 unit and integration tests and 59 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-17: The tester's nineteenth pass, over the eighteenth round of fixes (at `e9fc1d3`), found 5 defects and 3 smaller points; the suites passed on its run. All were fixed, each with a test. (1, 3) Marking the database as a whole as test or live, by the processor that opened it, could be contradicted: a server still in test mode beside the live account wrote test bookings into a database marked live, which the live site then refused and the clear refused too, with no way out; and bookings made with no processor set up took the kind of whatever opened the database next, so the clear could remove real ones. Each booking now says which kind of money it is, from the processor set up when it was made (`bookings.money_kind`, migration 0028, which drops 0027's table; tried on copies of the local database and of the tester's): the live account refuses a database with test bookings, test mode one with real bookings, and `db:clear-test-mode` removes test mode's bookings and what they made, and nothing else, so it is the way out in every case. The live site's overview says when a server in test mode has booked beside it. (2) Payments the sandbox recorded as `square` before it had a name of its own counted as the live account's: migration 0028 relabels them. (4) A lapsed card reservation was told to the owner and the client from a status read before the booking was locked, so a booking cancelled meanwhile was said to owe its price: it is read again under the lock. (5) The clear kept the stamps an owner added and removed the free hour they sent; and named neither the owners' codes nor where to find a customer with no booking left: a free hour stays with the stamps that sent it, and the listing names each code and each change of stamps with its page. Smaller: a client whose card only holds the amount is told what cancelling or moving does with it, and the appointment's page says such an amount on a cancelled booking is given back; the owner's note for a waiting refund says when no processor is set up. 858 unit and integration tests and 59 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-17: The tester's twentieth pass, over the nineteenth round of fixes (at `42f0eab`), was cut off by the account's weekly usage limit and handed its report back when resumed: 3 defects reproduced, 1 suspected and 3 smaller points; on its run one browser test failed twice with the machine under load (a block dragged in the page editor), and the four after it did not run. All were fixed, each with a test. (1) The clear removed a real booking, with its cash, for which a server in test mode had made a payment link, and the record of a real payment taken for a booking of test mode; the overview said nothing of either. A payment link is now of the same kind of money as its booking, or it is not made; the clear removes only what was pretend of a real booking, and keeps a booking of test mode the live account took money for, as real from then on; the overview and the refusal count pretend payments and links too, and an appointment's page says when its booking is of the other kind than the site's. (2) Credit of test mode paid for a real booking, made while card payments were off, and survived the clear unnamed: a code a booking of one kind issued is refused for a booking of the other, and a booking made with no processor set up is of the kind the database holds already. (3) So a moment without a card processor in a test set-up no longer makes test mode refuse the database for good. (4) `db:migrate` and `db:seed`, run as documented, would have ended in the refusal of test mode on the live database: the scripts open the database without that check. Found by the builder meanwhile, in the clear: it removed unsent mail about a real booking when that named a removed one, and a code a real booking had used; it gave a code back only what test bookings that stand held; and a client's free hours kept their numbers when some went, so that the next one earned would never have been sent. Smaller: the listing gives a code that stays the value it will have, and names a free hour of a client whose test visits go; under Pet fee, "less is held" became "less is left of what was paid", beside a card that holds an amount; "by Square itself" became the card processor. The page editor's drag in the browser tests is judged by its outcome and made again when the editor missed it. On the builder's own run, with the machine's load at 9, the staff test failed the same way once: an appointment's page was compiled inside the test, as the customers' pages, a client's account and the payment page were inside theirs, none of them being on the list of pages asked for before the tests start; they are now. 869 unit and integration tests and 59 browser tests pass.
- 2026-11-17: The owner (Katya) answered the business questions of the list of 2026-11-16, in the document "The Visuals Photo Studio — Website questions and decisions", pasted into this file: 26 of the 45 questions, and asked how a 27th (membership by yearly spend) differs from loyalty. The answers were recorded under "Answers of 2026-11-17" and where each subject is recorded, and the pasted text was taken out. The questions still open, and 15 the answers raised, are the list of 2026-11-17 (34 questions). The answers change what is built: rentals are refunded from 7 days ahead, not 14; extra people are $5 each, one add-on taken in a quantity; only three combinations of studios are bundles; a booking keeps the terms it was made under; a booking the studio moves or changes is priced again, with a payment section on its page; loyalty switched off and on again starts over; the photographer is available 9 to 4; the pet fee loses its button "The pet did not come". They ask for new work: weekend prices, memberships billed monthly as codes of minutes, requests to move a booking, a reminder and a review request around each visit, an editor of every email, photography's own codes, extra charges on an appointment, an Instagram feed, and after-hours requests. Nothing is built yet: the owner asked for the record first.
- 2026-11-17: The tester's twenty-first pass, over the twentieth round of fixes (at `397254c`), found that the fixes held wherever it attacked them (among other things, 90 random runs of a live and a test server on one database, cleared, against a database only the live one touched), and 5 defects, none about money, and 6 smaller points; the suites passed on its run, twice, the second with the site on node-postgres through a bridge of its own to PGlite. All were fixed, each with a test, but one smaller point, which was the field doing its job. (1) The two upload addresses read a body of any size from any signed-in visitor, a customer included, before checking their role or the body's size, when the client sent no length: they ask who may upload first, and read the body a part at a time, stopping at the limit (`readFormData`). (2) Queued mail had no age limit: sign-in codes that waited for an email service went out weeks later, long expired; a message can now carry when it is no longer any use (`email_outbox.expires_at`, migration 0029, tried on copies of the local database and of the tester's), and such a message is not sent. (3) An appointment of the other kind of money than the site's still offered a payment link, then refused it: the offer follows the same rule as the link (`payLinkPossible`). (4) Queries at once on a transaction's one connection: node-postgres warned at the clear of test mode and at a publish, and its next major version refuses them; such queries go through `together`. (5) The date 9999-12-31 answered with a server error, its day ending in a year the database cannot read: a date outside the booking window is answered with its problem, without its taken time. Smaller: a free hour that visits of test mode helped to earn is test mode's, so a real booking refuses it; a booking of test mode whose live link was withdrawn unpaid is no longer counted as real; cash recorded for a booking counted as real is named in the listing; an amount held by another card processor is not said to be tried again; "which their clients may think they have"; `db:seed` run before `db:migrate` says to migrate. The design's colour field takes the first 7 characters of what is pasted into it, as its length allows: not changed. And documented: the build opens the database too, so test mode is cleared before the first build with the live keys. 881 unit and integration tests and 59 browser tests pass.

- 2026-11-17: Built follow-up 9. Loyalty switched off and on again starts every customer's stamps over, and free hours already sent stay valid. `switchLoyalty` (settings.ts) records the moment in the setting `loyaltyStartedAt` when the switch goes from off to on. Only visits that began since then, and stamps added by hand since then, count; the free hours owed are counted from it too, and the next one takes the number after those sent before. Settings, the client's rewards page and the client's page in the admin say from which day stamps are counted. Four new tests cover it, in place of the test of the earlier rule (what was earned while it was off came at the next visit).

Migration `0035_refund_window_and_food` does both for a database that already has values and pages stored: a stored 336 becomes 168, any other value is kept, and only the original sentence about food is replaced, with a new version of each page. - 2026-11-17: Agreed with the owner a go-live plan for keeping the site from failing, kept as a checklist in `HANDOFF.md`. Built the parts that need no decision: Dependabot (monthly update pull requests into `rebuild`, and security alerts), one Node version in `web/.nvmrc` read by CI, and a scheduler heartbeat. The scheduler's address (`/api/cron/tick`) now records each run (`src/server/heartbeat.ts`, kept in the settings table under a key that never reaches the browser); `GET /api/health` answers 503 when the database does not answer or, in production, the scheduler has not run for 15 minutes, for an uptime monitor; and the admin overview's "Set-up still needed" says when the scheduler stopped, or, in production, that it never ran. Six new tests.
- 2026-11-17: At the owner's request, the tests became a gate for merging. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs the type check, lint and unit tests (job `checks`) and the browser suite (job `browser`) on every pull request into `master` and every push to `rebuild`, in UTC. Branch protection on `master` requires both jobs, for administrators too, and refuses direct pushes. The unit tests were also run locally in UTC first: all 896 pass.

- 2026-11-18: The tester's pass over memberships and follow-up 5 (at `67c6740`; the suites passed: 1,012 unit tests, 63 browser tests) found 13 defects, all fixed the same day: (1) a month Square took before the member cancelled was never recorded: cancelling now records it first, and the scheduled task asks about a cancelled membership until its months up to its end are recorded; (2) credit refunded and the booking moved back to the dearer day owed nothing: a refund records the part of it that gave back remaining credit (`refunds.credit_cents`, migration `0046`), which is owed again should the price go up, while the rest of a refund (goodwill) never makes a booking owe (`paidTowardPriceCentsByBooking` in money.ts); (3) the membership checkout charged the plan's price of the moment, not the one shown: the page sends what it showed, and a changed plan is refused with nothing charged (`price_changed`), the page then showing the new price with the terms to agree to again; (4) the clear of test mode kept a membership that paid for a booking made real, and the live account then refused the database for good: every membership of test mode goes, the booking keeping in its price what the hours paid, named, with a line in its history; (5) the payment card said a moved booking's card "was not charged": it says the card paid, and the rest is what the price went up by; (6) the booking screen showed membership hours paying for a studio the plan does not cover, after the studios were changed: it leaves them out and says why; (7) a special date added after a booking was made changed how a move repriced it: a booking keeps the kind of day it is priced as (`bookings.priced_as`, migration `0046`, filled in for older bookings from their day); (8) "its total is now $0 instead of $0" for a free hour: nothing is said when what the client pays does not change; (9) a month not paid was told twice once a later month was paid: the problem stays until no month is left unpaid, and the task keeps asking hourly meanwhile, so a month Square takes on a later try is recorded at once; (10) "Charged to your card: $0" in the confirmation of a booking the studio made that hours paid in full: no card or studio line when nothing is left to pay; (11) the account's My Membership did not list the bookings that used hours: it lists every change to them; (12) the receipt said "at the prices it was booked at" for a repriced booking: reworded; (13) the member's cancel message disappeared as the page was drawn again: it stays. The tester's probe tests were folded into the suites. Migration `0046` was tried on copies of two browser-test databases (weekday and weekend bookings filled in, a refund's credit refused above its amount, a second run changing nothing), and its filling in by a test with a special date.
- 2026-11-18: The tester's second pass (at `5ea5457`; the suites passed: 1,026 unit tests, 63 browser tests) found that 12 of the 13 fixes of the first pass held, and 8 defects, all fixed the same day: (1) what a cleaning or damage charge is for never reached the client: the client's booking, receipt (screen and PDF) and a payment link's email now name it ("Damage charge: Broken light stand", `clientChargeLabel` in `src/lib/charge-label.ts`), and the payment link's email lists the charges it includes (part `charges`); (2) a paid extra charge travelled into a client's cancellation refund or reschedule code once the studio had moved a booking that started: what paid the charges is left out of the money they use (`factsFor` in manage.ts: the price is taken as paid first, the rest up to the charges paid them, off money taken at the studio first); (3) releasing a booking promised the old fee for moving the new one: the fee said is today's, which the new booking takes (`nextTerms` of `rescheduleOutcome`); (4) a request to move a booking written over several lines was refused under the box's own limit: its line breaks are counted once (`formText`); (5) the owners could save emails without the money they state: every part that states money is required (the payment link's amount and charges, a payment's amount and balance, the reminder's to pay, a lapsed reservation's due, a pet fee's credit, a membership's price); the reschedule code's value and the late payments' amount stay optional, as their required parts state the same money; (6) the payment card said a card's hold lapsed for a booking paid with membership hours or credit: it says what paid it (`cardLapsed`); (7) a receipt that still owes said "Partly refunded": it says "Partly paid" while something is owed, and the payment card no longer names a card amount beside its Paid figure; (8) the notice after "Book for the client" said the price is paid at the studio when nothing was left to pay: it says so. The tester's probes were folded into the suites (`charges`, `manage`, the booking page's action, the email templates, `move-reprice`).

- 2026-11-18: The tester's fourth pass (at `8891b5e`; the suites passed: 1,046 unit tests, 63 browser tests) found 6 defects and one low point in the third pass's fixes, fixed the same day. One cause: the third pass's reading of which payment paid what (payment by payment, in the order they came) and what a booking owes (its totals) read the same money differently. (1) For a charge whose money went back to the card, the site advised a refund of it again, which the studio then gave twice; (2) a charge whose money was given back could be taken back, and the next charge was then owed only in part, and one the reading counted as paid could be taken back too; (3) a charge paid in cash and given back to the card, (4) a link for the price paid after a charge was added, read as paying the charge, and (5) money paid beyond the price that paid a charge, each made a client's cancellation refund and reschedule code wrong after a studio's move; (6) the payment link's email listed charges adding up to more than its amount; and, low, a lapsed card's words said to collect "the price" where charges, a code or hours made it otherwise. No one order of payments answers all of them (a link for the price wants the price paid first, a charge paid before the price was paid wants the charge first), so a booking with a charge is not moved (`CHARGES_BLOCK_MOVE`), and every reading left, what it owes, its credit, taking back a charge and the link's email, reads the price as paid first (`chargesPaidOf`, replacing `moneyByPurposeOf`); a lapsed card's words say to collect what is still to pay. A second refund of the same money is not refused: the site cannot tell what a refund was for, and it no longer advises one. The builder's readings, for an OK: question 48. The tester's probes became `charges-paid.test.ts`, and the suite's tests of a studio's move after a charge now expect it refused.

- 2026-11-19: An independent security review (a separate agent, reading the code and making about 28 light requests to the preview) found no critical issue, and these, fixed the same day. (High) Sign-in codes: guesses sent at the same moment all read "no tries yet", so the five-try limit could be passed; each try is now counted in one statement before the code is compared (`verifyLoginCode`), and an address is sent at most 10 codes a day (`MAX_CODES_PER_DAY`): at most 50 guesses a day at a million codes. (Medium) An open redirect after sign-in and on the preview's exit: a tab or line break in `next` ("/\t/evil.example") was let through; `safeNextPath` now refuses control characters, spaces and backslashes and checks the path stays on the site. (Medium) Rate limits were kept in each server copy's memory: they are counted in the database now (`limitRate`, table `rate_limit_hits`, migration `0048_rate_limits`, tried on a Neon branch of staging: the photos untouched, a third request refused), for bookings, membership joins, the contact form, and new per-IP limits on sign-in (10 codes asked for and 30 tried in 15 minutes from one address; the browser tests raise them with `SIGN_IN_REQUESTS_PER_IP`); the scheduled task removes old counts. (Low) Security headers on every response: `frame-ancestors 'self'`, X-Frame-Options, nosniff, a referrer policy and a permissions policy, and no `X-Powered-By`; a full content security policy waits, as Square's card form needs careful allowances. Not done yet: shorter sessions for owners and staff (30 days today), Square's buyer verification and risk rules before the live keys, Vercel's deployment protection for previews, and two-factor sign-in on every account and on the inboxes in `OWNER_EMAILS`. Verified: type check, lint, 1,070 unit tests, the whole browser suite (63 passed, after a first run stopped by a full disk).

The limit on asking for sign-in codes counted a live request the same day; the limit on tries waits for a code to be entered. `/api/health` answers 503 between the daily scheduler runs, as the Hobby plan makes it; production needs Vercel Pro with the scheduled tasks every five minutes.

- 2026-11-20 (night, later): The browser suite in WebKit, Safari's engine, for the first time.
  - **The first run:** 36 passed, 9 failed and 18 did not run. Found:
    - The page editor's history wrote its dates with Intl. Node joined date and time with "at", WebKit with a comma, so the page failed to hydrate in Safari and was drawn again. Fixed with `studioClock` and `studioDayAndClock`. The editor's conflict message also used the device's own time zone; it now uses the studio's.
    - Selects were 24px tall in WebKit, which ignores their height when they are drawn in its own style: the booking page's extra guests, the contact form's topic. Fixed by drawing selects plain on touch and narrow screens, with an arrow of their own.
    - The rewards test granted a clipboard permission that WebKit does not have. It now grants and reads the clipboard only in Chromium.
    - The other failures followed from these: bookings that tests depend on were never made.
  - **After the fixes:** 53 passed. The one failure was Next's development tools failing to load a chunk, which a production page does not have. The page editor's tests, run alone, passed 9, and one simulated drag missed three times.
- 2026-11-20 (night): Migration `0050_lookup_indexes`. PostgreSQL does not index a foreign key by itself, and four were read often with no index starting with them:
  - a booking's payments, read in 45 places;
  - a payment's refunds, 12;
  - a membership's months, 5;
  - a booking's membership minutes, 3.

  Only indexes, nothing else, for the 10,000 bookings the plan expects. Tried on a Neon branch of staging: the indexes were made, and a booking's payments are then found through `payments_booking_idx`. To be applied to staging with the next merge; the code does not depend on them.

- 2026-11-20: The Safari issues the night left (the owner: "fix those safari issues"):
  - **The page editor's simulated drag in WebKit.** The traces showed the block carried over the page and the page's drop area lit, but no blue line where the block would go, and Puck drops a block only where it has drawn that line (`previewIndex`). A first fix, letting go only once the line was there, held for the test alone and not in the whole suite. A probe in WebKit then settled why:
    - a drag whose mouse jumped onto the page in one burst of moves (Playwright's `mouse.move` with `steps`, as the test made it) was never taken: 0 of 4, even right after a drag that was;
    - a drag that glided there, a move each time the browser draws, as a hand moves a mouse, was taken every time: 8 of 8, the first drag in a newly opened editor included, with the line there as it arrived;
    - where on the block it aimed made no difference.

    The test's drag now glides, and lets go once the line is there (`[data-puck-line-placeholder]`). For a drop into an empty place for blocks (a column), the line must be inside that place. Gliding over the page on the way, the editor drew its line on the page first, and WebKit moved it into the column more than 100 ms later, so a release then put the block after the columns. Not a defect of the site: a person's mouse glides, and they let go where they see the line.
  - **Errors of a page being left, in WebKit.** The pictures test failed once on "script error: Load failed". Its trace showed why: the test opened the next page 450 ms after the collection's "Saved." showed, while the rest of that server action's answer was still streaming in. WebKit reports a request cut off by leaving the page as an error of that page, and React passed it on; Chromium says nothing. The server had done the work, so nothing was lost. The six copies of the tests' browser watcher are now one, `watchBrowser` in `e2e/helpers.ts`, which leaves out "Load failed" only from the request for the next document until that document is shown. At any other moment it still fails the test.
  - **Next's development tools in WebKit**, once, in the font test: their own hot-reload chunk failed to load, and the overlay's request about it was refused. The overlay's message was seen again on the page being left above, which is most likely what happened in the font test too. Not brought about on purpose (48 quick loads, closes and navigations in each engine). `frameworkNoise` leaves out those two messages, which only the development tools can make; a production build has neither.
  - `tsconfig.json` lists the WebKit run's build folder, as it does the tester's, so `next dev` no longer rewrites the file at each WebKit run.
  - **Verified in WebKit:** the page editor's file passed whole, 14 tests (5.8 min), its last four for the first time. The suite's other 49 tests passed in a whole-suite run earlier that afternoon, before the drag's last two changes and before the watchers became one.

Six things nothing used were deleted: `EDITOR_NOTE`, `FONT_GROUPS`, `countLiveCodes`, `dayOf`, `isChargeKind` and `SETTING_KEYS`, and charges.ts's re-export of `chargeLabel`. `web/knip.jsonc` says what counts: the scripts run by hand and `neon.ts` are entry points, a type used in its own file may be exported, and `@neon/env` stays (installed by `neon init`). `npx knip@5.88.1` reports nothing.

- 2026-11-20: Asked what is best practice for WebKit, the builder suggested one retry of a failed test in CI, and making `webkit` required after five passing runs in a row. The owner agreed ("let's do it"). The retry was not added: the serial test files cannot be retried (see "Conventions and gotchas"), and neither of CI's two chance failures would have passed on one. Instead, a failed job is read, then run again by hand, and CI refuses a `test.only` (`forbidOnly`). `AGENTS.md` says so. `webkit` then passed five runs in a row, all 63 tests each time: on `928173f`, on `c275685`, and three times on `6f04df9` (its push, and two runs started by hand; 16.3, 12.8 and 15.5 min). Since then `master`'s branch protection requires it beside `checks` and `browser`. The rest of the protection is as it was.
- 2026-11-20: Asked to keep going, the builder made the improvement it had suggested with that advice: the browser suite against a production build. CI's fourth job, `webkit-production`, runs it in WebKit, served over HTTPS; it is not required for merging. Locally it passed all 63 tests in 7.5 min (the development server takes 11 to 16), and so did its first CI run (`b5f230e`). How it works is under "Conventions and gotchas". What it found:
  1. **A preview read the clock while being prerendered.** In Draft Mode a production build's prefetches of linked pages, drawn with the owner's cookies, reached the session's `new Date()` and missed their caches, which Next.js logged as errors ("encountered the unstable value `new Date()` while prerendering", 143 in the three accessibility tests alone). `isPreview` (`src/server/site.ts`) now calls `connection()` first, so a preview is drawn when it is asked for; none since.
  2. **On a server Next.js runs itself, a missing page and an old address answer 200 on their first visit.** Vercel answers 404 and 308, as the hosted preview showed (see "Conventions and gotchas").
  3. **In WebKit, leaving a page while the rest of it is still coming in makes the framework load it again in full.** The tests now wait for it (`followLink`).
  4. **The booking pages' cache-miss warnings** (Known gaps), not understood yet.
