### Handoff for the next coding session

The handoff is in `HANDOFF.md` at the repository root since 2026-11-17: where the work stands, what is verified, what is next and what waits on the owners. Every tool reads it at the start of a session and rewrites it before stopping (`AGENTS.md` says how). The numbered answers below are the owner-approved follow-ups; record each implementation and its verification here, and its status in `HANDOFF.md`.

### Katya's answers to the 16 follow-up questions

The numbers below follow the Google Docs follow-up list. Points that still need Katya's confirmation are marked **to confirm**.

**Prices and memberships**

1. **Weekend prices for longer bookings** — **Decided and built (2026-11-17):** Katya approved the prices below. Weekend pricing applies on Saturday and Sunday.

   | Length | Blanche | Dayglow | Paris |
   | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
   | 1 hour | $95 | $82 | $72 |
   | 1.5 hours | $142.50 | $123 | $108 |
   | 2 hours | $190 | $164 | $144 |
   | 3 hours | $285 | $246 | $206 |
   | 4 hours | $345 | $300 | $259 |
   | 5 hours | $416 | $368 | $318 |
   | 8 hours | $653 | $568 | — |
   | 10 hours | $777 | — | — |

   A dash marks a length with no proposed price.

2. **Extra people** — **Decided and built (2026-11-18):** $5 per additional person for the entire booking, with a quantity selector. Blanche and Dayglow each include 6 guests and allow 6 extra. Paris includes 4 guests and allows 4 extra. Katya gave a limit of 10 people for studio bundles and 20 for the three-studio bundle. **Confirmed 2026-11-17:** the bundle limits (10 for a two-studio bundle, 20 for all three) count every guest, not only the additional ones, because the limit is about room. The builder's suggestion, confirmed by the owner. **Built:** one add-on, "Extra guest", $5, taken in a quantity on the booking screen and in the admin's "Book for the client" (a number from 0 to what the studios take, which the page states); the two extra-people tiers are switched off, and bookings made with them keep their own name and price. Each studio's extra guests are set under Studios ("Extra guests allowed": Blanche 6, Dayglow 6, Paris 4), the limits for studios together under Settings ("Most guests for two studios together", 10; "for all three", 20). The price refuses more (`extraGuestsAllowed` in `src/lib/domain/pricing.ts`, the same on the screen and on the server). Migration `0038_extra_guests`. What that gives a two-studio bundle: question 37, confirmed 2026-11-18.
3. **Memberships** — **Decided:** offer two monthly plans per studio or bundle.

   | Studio or bundle | 5 hours/month | 10 hours/month |
   | --- | ---: | ---: |
   | Dayglow | $330 | $600 |
   | Paris | $300 | $550 |
   | Blanche | $380 | $700 |
   | Paris + Dayglow | $520 | $950 |
   | Paris + Blanche | $660 | $1,200 |
   | Paris + Dayglow + Blanche | $780 | $1,450 |

   - Each plan gives 300 or 600 minutes per monthly billing cycle. ~~Unused minutes expire at the end of the cycle.~~ The allowance applies only to the studio or bundle purchased ~~and to appointments that take place within the active membership period~~. Bundles give simultaneous access to all included studios. The table lists all available bundles. (Struck through: changed by the answers of 2026-11-18 below.)
   - Each member gets a unique code tied to their membership. The same code can be used over several appointments. The system deducts minutes after each booking, shows the balance and prevents spending beyond the allowance. A client can apply the minutes left to a longer booking and pay the balance.
   - On weekends, membership minutes cover the weekday price for the time they pay for, and the client pays the difference to the weekend price. **Changed 2026-11-18 (question 41):** "a membership only looks into the time balance, not the price": the minutes cover weekend time in full, and a member pays no weekend difference. The switch `memberWeekendDifference` is off since then (migration `0047_member_weekend_full`). Membership codes cannot be combined with promotions, loyalty rewards or other membership codes.
   - Square handles payment details and bills automatically each month. Members can cancel before their next billing date, keep the membership through the end of the paid period and incur no further charges.
   - The admin can create, edit, activate and deactivate memberships and set prices and included minutes. The client's profile shows membership information. The admin can see each member's subscription status, billing history, remaining minutes and appointments booked with minutes, and adjust the minute balance.
   - The client account shows the active membership, remaining minutes, billing date and booking history.

   **Answers of 2026-11-18** to the builder's eight questions, from Katya through the owner. Where they differ from the lines above, they stand.

   1. **Unused hours carry over** to the following month. They do not expire at the end of a billing cycle.
   2. **Weekends and any length.** Members use their hours however they like, weekends included, for a booking of any length. A member with fewer minutes left than a booking needs uses what is left and pays for the rest: with 60 minutes left, a 2-hour booking takes the 60 minutes, and the member pays for the other hour (what that hour costs: question 42; weekends: question 41).
   3. **Bundles are flexible.** A bundle membership can be used for any one of its studios, as well as for the bundle.
   4. **Cancelling gives the minutes back** to the balance by itself when a member cancels at least 48 hours before the start; later, they are used. (Moving: question 43.)
   5. **Billing.** Monthly, from the day of sign-up. A member cancels at any time, with no minimum term; cancelling stops the future monthly charges. Unused hours stay available after the membership is cancelled. Membership payments are not refunded.
   6. **Online.** Clients buy a membership on the site themselves, as on today's site. Their account shows the membership, the minutes left and the payment history.
   7. **Studio time only.** A membership covers the rental time; extra guests, overtime and add-ons are paid separately, at the usual prices.
   8. **Loyalty.** Member visits earn stamps, one a day at most whatever the number of bookings or hours; a booking made with a free hour earns none. Both as loyalty works today.

   **Status:** built on 2026-11-18, in the five steps of the plan below; the owners answered questions 41 to 46 on 2026-11-18: 42, 43, 45 and 46 as built, 44 at $380 and $700 as built, and 41 changed: no weekend difference for members (built the same day: the switch is off). Square bills each month, as decided above: through Square's subscriptions, which keep the card at Square, so that this site never holds one.

   **The builder's plan (2026-11-18).** Built in five steps, each verified and committed on its own:

   1. **Plans.** Table `membership_plans`: name, the studios it covers (one, or one of the three bundles), minutes a month (300 or 600), the monthly price, on or off, and its Square plan. The twelve plans of the table above to start with. Admin → Memberships → Plans, owners only: add, change, switch on and off. A new price applies to members who join afterwards, as a booking keeps its terms (question 10); Square's plans cannot change a price under existing subscribers either. A public page, Memberships, lists the plans that are on.
   2. **Buying online.** From the Memberships page: the client's details (or their account), the card in Square's form, and the membership terms to agree to (the owners' text). The first month is charged then and there, so the client knows at once that it went through; the card is kept at Square from that payment, and Square's subscription bills it monthly from the same day of the next month. The minutes are added when each month is paid. A membership belongs to the client's email address, like their bookings; a client may hold more than one. New client emails, with templates: the membership started, a month paid and its minutes added, a payment that failed, the membership cancelled.
   3. **Spending minutes.** Each membership has its own code, entered at checkout with the member's email like any code, or offered to a signed-in member. A booking can use it when every studio booked is one its plan covers (a bundle membership for one studio of the bundle, too); for studio rentals, not photography sessions, whose price is mostly the photographer's. It takes the booking's length in minutes, or what is left, and the price drops by what the minutes cover (questions 41 and 42); add-ons and extra guests are paid as usual, and no other code goes on the same booking. Fully covered with nothing else to pay, no card is asked for. A cancellation at least 48 hours ahead gives the minutes back; the part paid by card follows the booking's own refund terms. Moving: question 43. Member visits count for loyalty as any other visit does.
   4. **Each month.** The scheduled task asks Square about each membership whose billing date has passed: a paid month adds its minutes and goes into the member's payment history; one Square could not charge adds nothing, and the member and the studio are told. Cancelling (in the account, or by an owner) stops the subscription at the end of the paid month: no more charges, and the minutes stay usable for as long as there are any.
   5. **The member's and the owners' view.** The account: each membership's plan, its state, minutes left, next billing date, its code, its payments, and each booking that used minutes; "Cancel membership". Admin → Memberships: every member with their plan, state, minutes left and next billing date; a member's page with the payments, every change to the minutes (a month paid, a booking, a cancellation giving them back, a correction), and an owner's correction of the minutes, with a reason, as stamps are corrected.

   Data: tables `memberships` (the client's email, the plan and its price as joined, Square's customer, card and subscription, the state, the dates), `membership_minutes` (a line for every change, so that the balance is their sum and every change has a reason) and `membership_payments`; a booking keeps the membership it used and the minutes it took. The pretend card processor gets subscriptions too, so that the tests and local development can run a month forward; `npm run check:square` tries the real calls against Square's sandbox.

   **Step 1 built (2026-11-18): the plans.** Tables `membership_plans` and `membership_plan_studios` (migration `0042_membership_plans`, which gives a database that has the three studios the twelve plans of the table; a new one gets them from the seed, `STARTING_PLANS` in `src/db/seed.ts`). Admin → Memberships, owners only: each plan's name, hours a month (whole or half hours, 0.5 to 100), price a month (up to $10,000) and description, saved one by one; "Stop offering" and "Offer again"; "Add a plan" for one studio or a set that can be booked together (not Blanche and Dayglow without Paris, `studioCombinationError`). A plan's studios cannot be changed once it is made, and a plan is never deleted. The page says that clients cannot buy a membership yet. The plans' names are "Studio Dayglow, 5 hours a month" and so on; the single studios' descriptions are the live site's, the bundles' say the hours can be used in either studio or both together. The live site sells Blanche at $370 and $670 a month; the owners' table says $380 and $700, which the site starts with (question 44). `src/server/memberships.ts`.

   **Step 2 built (2026-11-18): buying online, and cancelling.** A website page, Memberships (`/memberships`, a page of the owners' like the others, added to a database that does not have it; the live site's `/studio-memberships` leads to it), lists the plans on sale (the block "Memberships on sale", which reads them from the catalog) with "How It Works" and the "Membership Terms" (at `#terms`), as the owners answered. "Join" opens the checkout at `/book/membership?plan=…`: the plan, the client's details as for a booking, Square's card form, and the terms to agree to, which say that Square keeps the card for the monthly payments. Joining charges the first month with the card's one-time token, keeps the card from that payment (Square's Cards API), makes the plan's price at Square the first time it is needed (`membership_plan_prices`), and starts a Square subscription that bills the same card from the same day of the next month; then the plan's minutes are added, the membership is `active`, and the client is emailed "Membership started" with their code (`MEMBER-XXXX-XXXX`), a template the owners can change. Each step at Square carries a key made from the membership's id, and a membership being bought is found again by the page's request id, so pressing again after no answer, or twice, buys once. A declined card starts nothing. When the card cannot be kept or the subscription cannot be made, the first month is refunded at once and nothing starts; if that refund fails too, the studio is emailed to refund it by hand. The account has "My Membership": each membership's state, hours left, monthly hours and price, its code, its payments, and "Cancel membership" (asked twice), which was planned for step 4 but is here because the terms promise it: Square stops billing, the month paid for runs to its end (Square's `canceled_date`), the hours stay, and the client is emailed "Membership cancelled" (a template too). An owner can cancel one through the same code; the admin's page for it is step 5. The setting "Membership hours given back (hours before start)" (`memberReturnHours`, 48) is in place for step 3 and stated on the page (`{member hours back}`). Tables `memberships`, `membership_payments`, `membership_minutes` and `membership_plan_prices` (migration `0043_memberships`). Every call to Square was tried against its sandbox with the site's own code (`npm run check:square`): a customer, the first month, its card kept, a plan and a second price, a subscription starting next month (pending, then cancelled before billing anything) and one starting at once (Square charged the kept card by itself, read back as paid, then cancelled to the end of the month). Square stops a subscription at once when its customer's email address is one it refuses (an `example.com` one, in the sandbox): a real client's address is not. A membership was also bought in a real browser with Square's own card form against the sandbox, then cancelled in the account: the site recorded the payment, the minutes and the cancellation, and Square showed the subscription waiting for its first day and cancelled on that day, so it never bills. What was decided in building it: question 45.

   **Step 3 built (2026-11-18): spending minutes.** The membership's code goes in the booking's code field, like any code, with the member's email address, on the booking screen and in the studio's "Book for the client". It is for a studio rental whose every studio its plan covers (a bundle's plan for one of them or for them together), never a photography session, nor with another code. The booking takes its length in minutes, or what is left when that is less, and the price drops by the part of the studio price they cover: all of it, or the share of a longer booking (question 42), with the weekday price at weekends while the owners keep that switch on (question 41); add-ons and extra guests are paid as usual. With nothing left to pay, no card is asked for. The minutes are taken in the same step that holds the time, after locking the membership, so that two bookings never spend the same minutes; a booking whose card is refused or whose hold runs out gives them back. The booking keeps the membership, the minutes and what they paid (`bookings.membership_id`, `membership_minutes`, `membership_cents`, migration `0044_booking_membership`), and its summary, receipt and confirmation say so ("Paid with your membership hours (2 hours): $136"). A client who cancels at least 48 hours before the start gets the minutes back (the booking keeps that number with its other terms, `memberReturnHours`), and is told so before confirming and in the email; later, they are used. The studio's cancellation of a visit that has not started always gives them back, as it does a code. Moving one online: question 43. Member visits count for loyalty like any other: only a free hour's visit and a photography session do not. A booking the studio moves keeps its price for now, so its minutes stay as they were (repricing a move is follow-up 5's payment section). The confirmation of a booking the studio made, part of which a code or minutes paid, now says what is left "To pay at the studio" instead of a card line, which it said wrongly before for a credit code too.

   **Step 4 built (2026-11-18): each month.** The scheduled task (`runMembershipBilling`, from `runScheduledTasks`) asks Square, at most once an hour, about each active membership whose next month has come (`paid_through` reached; `billing_checked_at`, migration `0045_membership_billing`). Every month Square was paid is recorded once (`membership_payments.processor_id` is its invoice), with its minutes and the member's email "A month paid"; `paid_through` follows Square's own date. A month not taken by the day after it was due, or payments Square stopped (the membership then `stopped`), are told to the member ("A membership's payment did not go through", a template) and the studio once; a month paid later clears it. A subscription cancelled at Square is cancelled here, with the member's email. No month from the day a cancelled membership ends is ever counted. A membership whose buying stopped after its first month was paid is carried on an hour later (its card kept, its subscription made); one with no payment recorded is given up, and the studio asked to look in Square for a payment to refund. Only memberships of the processor set up now are asked about. The readings: question 46.

   **Step 5 built (2026-11-18): the owners' side.** Admin → Memberships opens with the members: each with their plan and price, its state (active, cancelled, stopped, being set up), when it is billed next or until when it was paid, any problem with a payment, and the hours left; those never started are listed apart, under "Not started", with why (for the owners to check Square when no answer came about a card). A member's page (`/admin/memberships/members/[id]`) shows the plan, the state, the hours left, the monthly hours and price, the code, the member's email (a link to their page under Customers) and phone; every change to the hours, newest first (a month paid, a booking with a link to it, hours given back by a cancellation, an owner's correction with its reason and who made it); every month paid; a correction ("Hours to add (a minus takes away)", in quarter hours, with a reason of 3 to 300 characters, never below nothing, as stamps are corrected; `correctMinutes`); and "Cancel membership" for the member, asked twice, which does what the member's own does. Owners only.

**Bookings, changes and emails**

4. **Unused pet fees** — **Decided and built (2026-11-17):** owners issue booking-linked studio credit manually under Admin → Codes after being told the pet did not attend. An eligible appointment links to the form and fills in the booking reference. Issuance uses the existing safeguards against duplicate credits and emails the customer. The appointment-specific "The pet did not come" button was removed.
5. **Changes made by the studio** — **Decided:** if the new price is lower, the difference is refunded manually or after the studio approves a refund. The appointment's page shows what was paid, links to the Square transaction, and shows any balance owed or credit left. **Confirmed 2026-11-17:** only a new date or time reprices a booking, the one change the site supports today. Length, studios and add-ons are not repriced for now. The builder's suggestion, confirmed by the owner. **Built (2026-11-18):** a move to a day of the other kind (weekday or weekend) prices the booking for that day at today's prices; the move form says what the price becomes before the move, and the client's email says it after. The appointment's payment card shows Paid, Outstanding balance and Remaining credit, a refund of the remaining credit to the card, and for each card payment taken through Square a link to Square's receipt of it. How, in detail, and what the builder decided on its own reading: question 47 under "Rules built on the builder's reading", confirmed by the owner on 2026-11-18. Code: `src/server/reprice.ts`, `moveBooking` and `cardReceiptOf` in `src/server/appointments.ts`, `creditLeftCentsByBooking` in `src/server/money.ts`.
6. **Second and later reschedules** — **Decided and built (2026-11-17):** charge $20 for each additional reschedule. The first customer reschedule is free when made at least 24 hours before the appointment. Administrators can reschedule as often as needed. The fee is the setting `rescheduleFeeCents` (Settings: "Fee for each further reschedule ($)", $20), and `maxReschedules` is now the number of free reschedules (1). The fee is taken off the reschedule code, so the client pays it when the new booking is made: this is the builder's reading (question 35 of the list above). The booking page says what the code will be worth and why before the client confirms, and the email and the booking's history say it too. A move the studio makes at the client's request still counts as their free reschedule, and its email says what moving again costs. A booking worth no more than the fee is not moved again online. With the fee set to 0, a booking cannot be moved online again after the free ones, as before. The guest information and the policies state the rule from the settings (`{reschedule fee}`, `{if reschedule fee}`), and migration `0036_reschedule_fee_copy` rewrites the original wording in stored pages. **Changed 2026-11-20 (to build):** a client moves a booking online once, free when at least 24 hours ahead; later moves are made by the studio, and each costs $20. Whether the fee applies to a move the studio itself starts is open ("Owner decisions of 2026-11-20"). The studio's moves charge no fee today.
7. **Review-request email** — **Decided and built (2026-11-17):** the scheduled task queues one request per completed appointment at or after 24 hours from its end time. It skips no-shows and cancellations. Migration `0034_review_requests` skips historical appointments whose 24-hour deadline passed before rollout. Delivery uses the existing email queue and requires the production scheduler and email transport. [The studio's Google review page](https://g.page/r/CTQ9ea1fatgnEBM/review)
8. **Blanche and Dayglow together** — **Decided and built (2026-11-17):** the two studios cannot be booked together without Paris, including at the full price for both. Paris + Dayglow, Paris + Blanche and the three-studio bundle stay available.

**Loyalty**

9. **Free hours already issued** — **Decided and built (2026-11-17):** when loyalty is turned off and later restarted, the stamp count starts over. Free-hour rewards already issued remain valid. Switching it on again in Settings records the moment (`loyaltyStartedAt`); only visits that began since then, and stamps added by hand since then, count. Settings, the client's rewards page and the client's page in the admin say from which moment (day and time) stamps are counted, and the admin marks earlier changes as not counted. While loyalty is off, stamps cannot be changed by hand (the tester's finding of 2026-11-17: a change then would be discarded when loyalty is switched on). Saving Settings with the switch as it was changes nothing.

**Photography**

10. **Photographer's hours** — **Decided and verified as built (2026-11-17):** the last session may start at 4 p.m. A one-hour session starting then ends at 5 p.m. The photographer's day has been 9 a.m. to 5 p.m. since 2026-11-15 (the starting catalog and migration `0011_owner_answers`), and the owners change it under Admin → Photography. The existing test confirms that a one-hour session at 4:00 is accepted, one at 4:15 is refused, and a two-hour session at 4:00 is refused.
11. **Minimum advance notice** — **Decided and verified as built (2026-11-17):** photography requires 336 hours of advance notice. A client booking on the 1st can book the 15th from the matching time of day onward. The boundary test verifies that a start 15 minutes short of 336 hours is refused and a start exactly 336 hours away is accepted.
12. **Photography credits and free hours** — **Decided and built (2026-11-17):** photography uses its own discount codes. Owners can create and cancel a photography-only percentage discount under Codes. Checkout refuses rental discounts, studio credit, rental reschedule codes, unused pet-fee credit and free hours for sessions. A rescheduled photography appointment still issues credit for rebooking photography, and its email and confirmation link back to photography checkout. Migration `0030_photography_codes` gives existing photography reschedule credits the same scope and keeps their balances.

**Studio operations and website**

13. **Overtime, cleaning and damage** — **Decided and built (2026-11-18):** charge overtime at the studio's hourly rate in 15-minute blocks, with each started block charged at one quarter of the hourly rate. An administrator enters the cleaning and damage charges for each case. Allow payment at the studio or by payment link. The appointment's page has an "Extra charges" card once the appointment has started: overtime in plain view (minutes past the end, with the rate and an example shown), cleaning and damage folded under it (an amount and what it is for). A charge adds to what the booking owes, so "Mark as paid" and "Email a payment link" collect it, and it shows as a line in the price summary and on the client's receipt. Table `booking_charges` (migration `0037_booking_charges`), `src/server/charges.ts`. How the rate is worked out, and who may add and take back a charge: question 36 under "Rules built on the builder's reading".
14. **Instagram feed** — **Decided:** use `thevisuals.photostudio` for the home-page feed. The owner will connect the account later. The proposed integration requires a business or creator account and a one-time owner sign-in.
15. **After-hours requests** — **Decided and built (2026-11-17):** the contact form has an “After-hours booking” topic. The booking page and policies explain that after-hours bookings are by request.

**Annual-spend discounts**

16. **Discount based on yearly spend** — **Decided:** do not offer an annual-spend discount. Visit loyalty gives a free hour every fifth visit day, and memberships give studio minutes paid for monthly.
